How Uber Computes ETA at Half a Million Requests per Second

Uber har second half a million ETA requests compute karta hai — har baar jab aap app kholte ho ya ride book karte ho, ETA nikalta hai. ETA ka matlab hai: driver aap tak kitni der me pahunchega, aur ride kitni der me complete hogi.

ETA compute karne ke liye Uber real-time traffic data + historical patterns + ML models use karta hai. Road network ko graph me rakha jaata hai, aur shortest path algorithms (A* jaisi) se route nikalta hai. Traffic ki current speed ko GPS data se measure kiya jaata hai — lakhon drivers ke GPS points se pata chalta hai kaunsi road par kitni speed hai.

Half a million requests per second par har request ko fresh compute karna impossible hai — isliye pre-computation + caching use hoti hai: popular routes ke ETAs pehle se compute karke cache me rakhe jaate hain, aur sirf unusual routes fresh compute hote hain. ETA ko thoda stale dikhana acceptable hai — 30 second purana ETA bhi kaam karta hai.

  • Key number: 500,000 ETA requests per second
  • Core pattern: road graph + real-time GPS traffic + ML models + pre-computed ETAs for popular routes
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: ETA me perfection nahi chahiye — 30 second purana cached ETA bhi chalega, lekin har request fresh compute karna scale par impossible hai

How Uber Finds Nearby Drivers at 1 Million Requests per Second

Uber har second 1 million requests par nearby drivers dhoondhta hai — har rider ke liye paas wale drivers ki list. Ye geospatial search ki sabse badi scale story hai.

Uber iske liye geohash use karta hai: duniya ko chhote grid cells me baanta jaata hai, aur har driver apne cell me register hota hai. Rider ke liye paas wale drivers dhoondhne ka matlab hai: rider ke cell aur aas-paas ke cells me drivers ko scan karna — poore duniya ko scan karne ki jagah sirf kuch cells. Ye search in-memory (Redis) hoti hai, database se nahi, kyunki isse milliseconds me jawab chahiye.

Drivers ki locations har kuch second me update hoti hain — lakhon drivers × har 3 second = bahut zyada writes. Ye writes ko Kafka stream se handle kiya jaata hai, aur in-memory index ko incrementally update kiya jaata hai. Agar koi cell me driver na mile to search radius badha di jaati hai — pehle 500m, phir 1km, phir 2km.

  • Key number: 1 million nearby-driver requests per second
  • Core pattern: geohash grid cells + in-memory index (Redis) + expanding radius search
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: "nearby" dhoondhne ke liye poora data scan mat karo — duniya ko cells me baanto aur sirf paas wale cells dekho
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/how-does-uber-find-nearby-drivers

How Uber Payment System Handles 30 Million Transactions Per Day

Uber ka payment system har din 30 million transactions handle karta hai — rides, eats, aur driver payouts, sab ek saath. Payment me sabse bada requirement hai correctness: ek bhi galat charge ya miss hua payout unacceptable hai.

Uber ki payment architecture me ledger central hai — har transaction ek ledger entry hai, aur ledger hi source of truth hai. Payment flow hai: ride complete → fare calculate → authorize + capture (pehle hold, phir charge) → ledger me entry → driver ko payout. Har step idempotent hai, taaki retry par double charge na ho.

Scale par payments ko async rakha gaya hai: ride complete hote hi user ko turant "ride complete" dikhta hai, aur payment background me process hoti hai. Agar payment fail ho to retry logic aur dunning (dobara try karna) chalta hai. Reconciliation har roz hoti hai — ledger, payment gateway aur bank ke records ko milaya jaata hai, taaki ek paisa bhi hisaab se bahar na ho.

  • Key number: 30 million transactions per day
  • Core pattern: ledger as source of truth + authorize/capture + idempotency + async processing + daily reconciliation
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: payment me ledger hi sach hai — har paisa ek entry hai, aur roz reconciliation se hisaab milna chahiye
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/payment-system-design

8 Reasons Why WhatsApp Was Able to Support 50 Billion Messages a Day With Only 32 Engineers

WhatsApp ne 50 billion messages a day ko sirf 32 engineers se handle kiya — ye system design ki sabse famous kahani hai. Iska raaz hai: simple architecture, sahi technology, aur "kam cheezein, lekin achhe se" ka principle.

WhatsApp ka core hai Erlang/OTP — ek aisi language jo concurrency ke liye bani hai. Har connection ek halka process hai, aur Erlang millions of processes ko ek saath chala sakta hai. Messages ko store-and-forward kiya jaata hai: sender ka message server par aata hai, receiver online ho to turant deliver, nahi to store karke rakha jaata hai aur receiver online aate hi deliver hota hai.

WhatsApp ne FreeBSD + custom stack use kiya, aur har server par millions of connections handle kiye — kyunki Erlang processes bahut halke hote hain. Database ke liye Mnesia (Erlang ki built-in DB) use hui. Aur sabse bada lesson: WhatsApp ne features kam rakhe — no ads, no games, sirf messaging — isliye 32 engineers kaafi the. Complexity kam rakho to chhoti team bhi massive scale sambhal sakti hai.

  • Key numbers: 50 billion messages/day, sirf 32 engineers
  • Core pattern: Erlang/OTP (lightweight processes) + store-and-forward + Mnesia + FreeBSD
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: scale ka raaz aksar "kam karo, achhe se karo" hai — har extra feature ek extra system hai jo maintain karna padta hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/whatsapp-engineering

WeChat ki neev hai microservices + sharded storage: har feature (chat, moments, pay, mini-programs) ki apni services hain, aur data ko user ID ke hisaab se shard kiya gaya hai. Chat messages ke liye store-and-forward use hota hai, payments ke liye strongly consistent ledger, aur moments (feed) ke liye fan-out.

Mini-programs WeChat ki sabse interesting architecture hai: third-party apps WeChat ke andar chalte hain, lekin sandboxed tareeke se — unhe sirf wahi APIs milti hain jo WeChat deta hai. Isse WeChat ek platform ban jaata hai, sirf app nahi. 1.67 billion users par har feature ka apna scale pattern hai, lekin common principle hai: har feature ki apni service, apna datastore, aur apni scaling story.

  • Key number: 1.67 billion monthly users — chat + payments + shopping + mini-programs, ek app me
  • Core pattern: microservices + user-based sharding + feature-wise scaling (chat, pay, feed alag)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: super-app me har feature ko alag system samjho — ek feature ka scale pattern dusre par mat thopo

11 Reasons Why YouTube Was Able to Support 100 Million Video Views a Day With Only 9 Engineers

Kyunki video views me power law hoti hai — kuch videos ko lakhon views milte hain, baaki ko bahut kam — to top videos ko cache karne se hi 90%+ traffic handle ho jaata hai.

YouTube ne database ko hot path se hata diya: video serve karte waqt database ko chhoona hi nahi padta — CDN aur cache se sab milta hai. Aur team chhoti thi kyunki architecture simple thi: kam components, kam moving parts, aur har component ka ek clear kaam. Scale ka raaz hamesha "aur technology" nahi hota — aksar "kam technology, sahi jagah" hota hai.

  • Key numbers: 100 million video views/day, sirf 9 engineers (early YouTube)
  • Core pattern: Python + MySQL + CDN + aggressive caching of popular videos (power law)

How YouTube Was Able to Support 2.49 Billion Users With MySQL

YouTube 2.49 billion users ko MySQL par serve karta hai — aur ye is baat ka saboot hai ki relational database bhi massive scale par chal sakta hai, bas use karne ka tareeka sahi hona chahiye. YouTube ka secret weapon hai Vitess.

Vitess ek database clustering system hai jo MySQL ke upar baithta hai: ye MySQL instances ko shards me baant ta hai, aur application ko ek single logical database dikhata hai. Vitess query routing, sharding, aur resharding ko handle karta hai — application ko pata bhi nahi chalta ki peeche kai MySQL instances hain. YouTube ne Vitess ko open source bhi kiya hai.

YouTube ke data model me videos, users, comments aur subscriptions hain — aur inme se har ek ka apna sharding strategy hai. Vitess se YouTube ne online resharding haasil ki — bina downtime ke shards ko baant sakte hain. Lesson: MySQL ko chhodne se pehle use scale karne ke tareeke dhoondho — Vitess jaisi layer se MySQL bhi billions of users sambhal sakta hai.

  • Key number: 2.49 billion users, MySQL + Vitess par
  • Core pattern: Vitess — MySQL sharding + query routing + online resharding, app ko single DB dikhta hai
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: database badalne se pehle sharding layer try karo — MySQL + Vitess billions of users tak chal sakta hai

5 Reasons Why Zoom Was Able to Support 300 Million Video Calls a Day

Zoom ne 300 million video calls a day handle kiye — pandemic me ye number hafton me 10 guna badh gaya tha. Video conferencing ki sabse badi mushkil hai real-time media routing: har participant ka video/audio sab tak pahunchana, bina delay ke.

Zoom ki architecture me SFU (Selective Forwarding Unit) central hai: har participant apna video ek SFU ko bhejta hai, aur SFU use baaki participants ko forward karta hai — bina video ko decode/re-encode kiye. Isse server ka load bahut kam hota hai, kyunki video ko process nahi karna padta, sirf forward karna hota hai.

Scale par Zoom ne global data centers + cloud bursting use kiya: apne data centers ke saath-saath AWS par bhi capacity li, taaki achanak badhe traffic ko handle kar sakein. Aur video quality ko adaptive rakha — network slow ho to quality kam, lekin call chalti rahe. Pandemic wali growth se lesson ye hai: capacity ko pehle se flexible rakho, kyunki traffic kabhi bhi 10 guna ho sakta hai.

  • Key number: 300 million video calls per day
  • Core pattern: SFU (forward, don't transcode) + global data centers + cloud bursting
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: video call me video ko forward karo, process mat karo — SFU hi scale ka jawab hai; aur capacity hamesha flexible rakho
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/zoom-architecture
← PehleHow YouTube Was Able to Support 2.49 Billion Users With MySQLAgla →How Zapier Automates Billions of Tasks

How Zapier Automates Billions of Tasks

Zapier billions of tasks automate karta hai — ye ek aisa platform hai jo alag-alag apps ko jodta hai: "agar Gmail me naya email aaye to Slack me message bhejo" — aise rules (Zaps) se. Har Zap ek trigger → action ka flow hai.

Zapier ki architecture me event-driven polling + webhooks central hai: trigger app me kuch hota hai (naya email, naya row) to Zapier ko event milta hai — webhook se ya polling se. Phir Zapier workflow engine se actions chalata hai: data ko transform karna, doosre app ki API call karna, aur result ko log karna. Har Zap run ek task hai, aur billions of tasks ka matlab hai massive async processing.

Scale par sabse badi mushkil hai third-party APIs ki reliability: har app ki API alag hai, rate limits alag hain, aur kabhi bhi fail ho sakti hai. Isliye Zapier me retry with backoff, dead letter queues, aur per-integration rate limiting hai. Aur har task ko idempotent banaya gaya hai, taaki retry par kaam dobara na ho.

  • Key number: billions of tasks automated
  • Core pattern: trigger (webhook/poll) → workflow engine → actions, sab async + retry + DLQ
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: automation me third-party API kabhi bhi fail ho sakti hai — retry, backoff aur idempotency ke bina billions of tasks impossible hain
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/zapier-architecture
← Pehle5 Reasons Why Zoom Was Able to Support 300 Million Video Calls a DayAgla →Back of the Envelope
67System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

Back of the Envelope

Back-of-the-envelope calculation system design interview ka pehla step hai — bina calculator ke, rough numbers se estimate karna ki system kitna bada hoga. Iska maksad exact answer nahi hai, maksad hai ye dikhana ki aap scale ko samajhte ho.

Kuch numbers yaad rakhne chahiye: 1 million requests/day ≈ 12 requests/sec, 1 billion requests/day ≈ 12,000 requests/sec. Ek saal me ~30 million seconds hote hain. Ek user agar din me 10 requests kare to 10 million users ≈ 1,150 requests/sec. Storage ke liye: 1KB × 1 million users × 365 din ≈ 365GB per year.

Latency ke rough numbers bhi yaad rakho: memory access ~100ns, SSD read ~100µs, network round trip same data center me ~0.5ms, aur cross-region ~50-150ms. Interview me jab bhi koi system design karo, pehle ye estimates likho — isse pata chalta hai ki database kitna bada hoga, cache kitna chahiye, aur kitne servers lagenge.

  • Yaad rakhne wale numbers: 1M req/day ≈ 12 req/sec; 1 saal ≈ 30M seconds; memory 100ns, SSD 100µs, network 0.5ms (same DC)
  • Key trade-off: rough estimate (fast, interview me kaafi) vs exact calculation (slow, zaroori nahi)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: estimate hamesha assumptions ke saath likho — "agar har user din me 10 baar aata hai to..." — interviewer ko tumhari soch dikhni chahiye, sirf number nahi
Source:https://systemdesign.one/back-of-the-envelope/
← PehleHow Zapier Automates Billions of TasksAgla →How to Troubleshoot if You Can’t Access a Particular Website?
68System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

How to Troubleshoot if You Can’t Access a Particular Website?

Website nahi khul rahi — ye troubleshooting ka classic sawaal hai, aur iska jawab OSI model ki layers me chhupa hai. Systematic tareeka hai: neeche se upar check karo — physical, network, DNS, aur phir application.

Pehle check karo: kya internet hi nahi hai, ya sirf ek website nahi khul rahi? Agar sirf ek website hai to DNS check karo — nslookup ya dig se dekho domain resolve ho raha hai ya nahi. Phir ping se dekho server reachable hai ya nahi, aur traceroute se dekho packet kahan ruk raha hai.

Agar DNS aur network theek hai to HTTP level check karo: curl -v se dekho kya response aa raha hai — connection refused (server down), timeout (firewall ya server overload), ya SSL error (certificate issue). Browser cache aur proxy bhi kabhi-kabhi culprit hote hain. Har step par ek hypothesis banao, test karo, aur aage badho — random try karne se debugging slow hoti hai.

  • Troubleshoot order: internet? → DNS resolve? → ping/traceroute? → curl (HTTP status?) → browser/proxy?
  • Key tools: nslookup/dig (DNS), ping (reachability), traceroute (kahan ruk raha hai), curl -v (HTTP detail)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: pehle scope tay karo — "sirf ye site" ya "saara internet" — isse aadhi problem solve ho jaati hai
Source:https://systemdesign.one/how-to-troubleshoot-if-you-cannot-access-a-website/
← PehleBack of the EnvelopeAgla →What Happens When You Type a URL Into Your Browser?
69System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

What Happens When You Type a URL Into Your Browser?

Browser me URL type karte hi ek lambi chain shuru hoti hai — aur interview me ye sabse zyada poochha jaane wala sawaal hai. Poora flow samajh lo to networking ki neev pakki ho jaati hai.

Pehle browser URL ko parse karta hai aur DNS lookup karta hai — domain name ko IP address me badalna. Phir browser us IP se TCP connection banata hai (3-way handshake), aur HTTPS ho to TLS handshake bhi hota hai — certificate verify, encryption keys exchange. Phir browser HTTP request bhejta hai, server HTTP response bhejta hai — HTML, CSS, JavaScript.

HTML milte hi browser rendering shuru karta hai: HTML parse karke DOM tree, CSS parse karke CSSOM, dono se render tree, phir layout aur paint. Beech me JavaScript, images aur fonts ke liye aur requests jaati hain. Poora flow milliseconds me hota hai, lekin isme DNS, TCP, TLS, HTTP aur rendering — paanch alag systems kaam karte hain.

  • Flow: URL parse → DNS → TCP handshake → TLS handshake → HTTP request/response → parse → render
  • Key numbers: DNS ~20-100ms, TCP+TLS ~100-200ms, har step me latency judti hai
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: har step ek alag system hai — interview me flow ko steps me batao, ek saans me nahi
Source:https://systemdesign.one/what-happens-when-you-type-url-into-your-browser/

System Design Interview Cheat Sheet

System design interview ka cheat sheet ek page par poora framework deta hai — interview me time kam hota hai, isliye ek clear structure hona chahiye. Classic framework hai: requirements → estimation → API → high-level design → deep dive → bottlenecks → trade-offs.

Requirements me functional aur non-functional dono poochho: system kya karega, kitne users honge, availability aur latency kitni chahiye. Estimation me back-of-the-envelope se scale nikalo. API design me endpoints aur data model tay karo. High-level design me components aur unke beech ka data flow banao.

Deep dive me ek-do components ko detail me design karo — database schema, caching, ya sharding. Aakhir me bottlenecks batao aur trade-offs discuss karo: consistency vs availability, SQL vs NoSQL, sync vs async. Cheat sheet ka matlab hai ye framework ratna nahi — isse practice me use karke apni soch ko structure dena.

  • Framework: requirements → estimation → API → high-level design → deep dive → bottlenecks → trade-offs
  • Key trade-off: breadth (poora system) vs depth (ek component detail me) — dono ka balance chahiye
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: interview me sabse pehle requirements clarify karo — bina iske design karna time waste hai

  • Prep plan: fundamentals → case studies → mock interviews (coding + design + behavioral, teeno)

  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: interview prep me practice hi sab kuch hai — padhna 30%, mock karna 70%

72System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

7 Simple Ways to Fail System Design Interview

System design interview me fail hone ke 7 aasan tareeke — inhe jaan lo to in se bachna aasan hai. Pehla: requirements clarify na karna — seedha design shuru kar dena. Interviewer dekhna chahta hai ki tum sawaal poochhte ho ya nahi.

Dusra: estimation na karna — scale ka andaza lagaye bina database aur servers chunna. Teesra: ek hi solution par atakna — trade-offs discuss kiye bina ek design ko final kar dena. Chautha: buzzwords phenkna — Kafka, Kubernetes, microservices ke naam lena bina ye samjhe ki wahan zaroorat hai ya nahi.

Paanchva: deep dive se bachna — high-level boxes bana kar ruk jaana, database schema ya API detail me na jaana. Chhatha: time manage na karna — ek component par 30 minute laga dena. Saatvaan: bottlenecks na batana — apne design ki kamzori khud na pehchan paana. In saaton se bacho to interview bahut behtar jaata hai.

  • 7 fail tareeke: no clarification → no estimation → no trade-offs → buzzwords → no deep dive → no time management → no bottleneck analysis
  • Key lesson: interviewer tumhare design se zyada tumhari soch ko judge karta hai
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: har decision ke saath "kyun" batao — "Kafka use karenge" nahi, "Kafka isliye kyunki..." bolo

73System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

Amazon Frugal Architecture Explained

Amazon ki frugal architecture ka matlab hai: har dollar ka hisaab rakhna aur resources ko waste na karna. Amazon ke founder ka principle hai — "frugality breeds resourcefulness" — aur ye Amazon ki architecture me dikhta hai.

Frugal architecture ke rules hain: pehle measure karo (kahan paisa ja raha hai), sahi size chuno (over-provisioning mat karo), managed services ka sahi use (jo sasta hai use karo, jo mehenga hai use optimize karo), aur architect for cost (cost ko design requirement banao, baad ki soch nahi).

Example: S3 me lifecycle rules se purana data saste storage class me bhejna, Lambda se idle cost zero karna, aur right-sizing se EC2 instances ko actual load ke hisaab se chunna. Frugal hone ka matlab kanjoos hona nahi hai — matlab hai har resource ka return samajhna. System design interview me cost ka zikr karna tumhe alag khada karta hai, kyunki bahut kam candidates is par sochte hain.

  • Core rules: measure → right-size → lifecycle rules → cost as design requirement
  • Key trade-off: performance (zyada resources) vs cost (kam resources) — sahi balance business decide karta hai
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: interview me cost ka estimate bhi do — "ye design kitne ka padega" poochhna interviewer ko pasand aata hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/frugal-architecture

74System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

11 System Design Concepts Explained, Simply

System design ke 11 core concepts ko simple tareeke se samjho — ye concepts har interview me kaam aate hain: scalability, availability, consistency, caching, load balancing, database sharding, replication, message queues, CDN, rate limiting, aur CAP theorem.

Scalability ka matlab hai load badhne par system ko badhana — vertical (bada server) ya horizontal (aur servers). Availability ka matlab hai system kitne time up rehta hai — 99.9% matlab saal me ~8.7 ghante downtime. Consistency ka matlab hai sab users ko same data dikhe. Caching ka matlab hai frequently-used data ko memory me rakhna taaki database par load kam ho.

Load balancing traffic ko servers me baant ta hai. Sharding data ko baant ta hai. Replication data ki copies banata hai. Message queues services ko decouple karte hain. CDN content ko user ke paas le aata hai. Rate limiting abuse rokta hai. Aur CAP theorem batata hai ki network partition me consistency aur availability me se ek chunni hogi. Ye 11 concepts hi system design ki neev hain — inhe example ke saath yaad rakho.

  • 11 concepts: scalability, availability, consistency, caching, load balancing, sharding, replication, message queues, CDN, rate limiting, CAP
  • Key numbers: 99.9% availability = saal me ~8.7 ghante downtime; 99.99% = ~52 minute
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: har concept ko ek real example se jodo — "caching matlab Instagram ki feed Redis me" — tabhi yaad rehta hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/11-system-design-concepts-explained

Part 1 me neev ke concepts hain: client-server, DNS, load balancing, caching, databases, aur CAP theorem. Part 2 me aate hain: consistent hashing, Bloom filters, rate limiting, message queues, sharding, aur replication strategies. Part 3 me advanced topics hain: distributed transactions, consensus (Raft/Paxos), vector clocks, CRDTs, aur chaos engineering.

114 concepts ka matlab ye nahi ki sab ratne hain — matlab ye hai ki system design ek bada field hai aur har concept ka ek use case hai. Interview ke liye pehle 30-40 core concepts pakke karo, baaki ko "suna hai, samajhta hoon" level par rakho. Is SPA me inme se har important concept ka apna section hai — ye series un sabka index hai.

  • Structure: Part 1 core → Part 2 intermediate → Part 3 advanced (kul 114 concepts)

76System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

How Timsort Algorithm Works

Timsort wo sorting algorithm hai jo Python aur Java me use hota hai — aur ye isliye khaas hai kyunki ye real-world data ke liye optimize hai. Real data aksar partially sorted hota hai (jaise timestamps ya naam), aur Timsort iska fayda uthata hai.

Timsort merge sort + insertion sort ka combination hai: pehle array me chhote sorted "runs" dhoondhta hai (ya insertion sort se banata hai), phir un runs ko merge karta hai. Agar data pehle se sorted hai to Timsort O(n) me kaam kar deta hai — jabki plain merge sort hamesha O(n log n) karta hai.

Interview me Timsort isliye poochha jaata hai kyunki ye batata hai ki theory aur practice me farak hota hai: textbook me merge sort best hai, lekin real world me Timsort behtar hai kyunki real data me pattern hote hain. System design me bhi yahi principle hai — real workload ko samajh kar design karo, textbook ko nahi.

  • Core pattern: sorted runs dhoondho/banao → merge karo; sorted data par O(n)
  • Key trade-off: worst-case O(n log n) same hai, lekin real data par Timsort bahut fast hai
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: real data me pattern hote hain — jo algorithm unka fayda uthaye, wahi production me jeet ta hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/timsort-algorithm

Part 1 me mobile basics hain: offline-first design, sync strategies, aur push notifications. Part 2 me aate hain: data sync conflicts (do devices par same data badle to kya ho), background sync, aur efficient APIs (mobile par har byte mehenga hai). Part 3 me advanced topics: mobile-specific caching, battery-aware design, aur app size optimization.

Mobile system design interview me sabse zyada poochha jaane wala sawaal hai: "Design WhatsApp/Instagram mobile app ka backend" — aur iska jawab mobile-specific constraints se shuru hota hai: unreliable network, limited battery, aur intermittent connectivity. Ye series unhi constraints ko detail me samjhati hai.

  • Structure: Part 1 basics (offline-first, push) → Part 2 sync conflicts, efficient APIs → Part 3 caching, battery, app size
  • Key trade-off: offline-first (complex, lekin reliable UX) vs online-only (simple, lekin network par dependent)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: mobile design hamesha constraints se shuru karo — network, battery, storage — phir architecture banao

78System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

Actor Model

Actor Model ek concurrency model hai jisme system actors se banta hai — har actor ek independent unit hai jiska apna state aur mailbox hota hai. Actors ek dusre se sirf messages se baat karte hain, kabhi shared memory se nahi.

Is model ka sabse bada fayda hai: locks ki zaroorat nahi. Kyunki har actor apna state khud handle karta hai aur ek waqt par ek hi message process karta hai, race conditions hoti hi nahi. Actors bahut halke hote hain — ek system me millions of actors chal sakte hain, jabki threads hazaaron me hi bhaari pad jaate hain.

Actor Model ka sabse famous implementation hai Erlang/OTP (WhatsApp isi par chalta hai) aur Akka (JVM par). PayPal ne isi model se 1 billion transactions/day ko 8 VMs par handle kiya. Jab bhi system me bahut saare independent kaam ek saath chalne hon, Actor Model sochne layak hai.

  • Core pattern: actors + mailboxes + message passing; no shared state, no locks
  • Key numbers: millions of actors per system; PayPal: 1B transactions/day on 8 VMs
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: concurrency ki mushkil shared state se aati hai — state ko hata do to locks ki zaroorat hi khatam
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/actor-model
← PehleMobile System Design Concepts (Part 1–3)Agla →How API Gateway Works
79System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

How API Gateway Works

API Gateway ek single entry point hai jo clients aur microservices ke beech baithta hai — client ko sirf gateway dikhta hai, peeche kitni services hain ye pata nahi chalta. Ye microservices architecture ka front door hai.

Gateway ke kaam hain: routing (request ko sahi service tak bhejna), authentication/authorization (token check karna), rate limiting (abuse rokna), request/response transformation (client ko chahiye us format me data dena), aur logging/monitoring (har request ka hisaab).

Gateway ke bina har client ko har service ka address pata hona chahiye, aur har service ko khud auth aur rate limiting karni hogi — jo duplication hai. Gateway ye cross-cutting concerns ek jagah centralize karta hai. Lekin gateway khud single point of failure ban sakta hai — isliye use highly available banana hota hai (multiple instances + load balancer).

  • Core jobs: routing + auth + rate limiting + transformation + logging
  • Key trade-off: gateway (centralized, simple for clients) vs direct service calls (no bottleneck, lekin har client ko sab pata hona chahiye)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: gateway ko sirf routing aur cross-cutting kaam do — business logic kabhi gateway me mat daalo
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/how-api-gateway-works
← PehleActor ModelAgla →API Gateway vs Load Balancer vs Reverse Proxy
80System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

API Gateway vs Load Balancer vs Reverse Proxy

API Gateway, Load Balancer aur Reverse Proxy — teeno request ke beech me baithte hain, lekin inke kaam alag hain. Inhe confuse karna interview me common galti hai.

Load Balancer ka kaam hai traffic ko kai servers me baantna — Layer 4 (TCP) ya Layer 7 (HTTP) par. Iska focus hai distribution aur health checking. Reverse Proxy client ki taraf se server se baat karta hai — caching, SSL termination, aur request forwarding iska kaam hai. API Gateway isse ek step aage hai — ye microservices ke liye bana hai: routing ke saath auth, rate limiting, aur API-specific logic bhi karta hai.

Simple rule: agar sirf traffic baantna hai to load balancer; agar ek server ke aage caching/SSL chahiye to reverse proxy; agar kai microservices ke aage ek smart front door chahiye to API gateway. Real systems me aksar teeno hote hain — load balancer ke peeche API gateway, aur gateway ke peeche services.

  • Load Balancer: traffic distribution + health checks (L4/L7)
  • Reverse Proxy: caching + SSL termination + forwarding (ek server ke liye)
  • API Gateway: routing + auth + rate limiting + transformation (microservices ke liye)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: teeno ek saath bhi use hote hain — LB → Gateway → Services, ye common stack hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/api-gateway-load-balancer-reverse-proxy
← PehleHow API Gateway WorksAgla →Best Practices for API Design
81System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

Best Practices for API Design

API design karna ek kala hai — achhi API use karne me aasan aur badalne me safe hoti hai. Pehli best practice: RESTful conventions follow karo — resources ko nouns se naam do (/users/123), actions ko HTTP methods se (GET, POST, PUT, DELETE).

Dusri: versioning — API ko version karo (/v1/users), taaki badlav se purane clients na tootein. Teesri: pagination — badi lists ko pages me do (?limit=20&offset=40), poori list ek saath kabhi mat bhejo. Chauthi: consistent errors — har error me same format (code, message, details) aur sahi HTTP status codes (400, 401, 404, 429, 500).

Paanchvi: idempotency — POST bhi retry-safe banao jahan possible ho. Chhathi: filtering aur sorting ko query params se do. Saatvi: documentation — OpenAPI/Swagger se API ko document karo, taaki clients ko guess na karna pade. Achhi API ka test hai: kya ek naya developer bina poochhe use kar sakta hai?

  • Core rules: RESTful nouns + versioning + pagination + consistent errors + idempotency + OpenAPI docs
  • Key trade-off: strict design (safe, lekin slow to build) vs loose design (fast, lekin clients toot-te rehte hain)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: API ek contract hai — ek baar publish ho gayi to use todna bahut mehenga hai, isliye pehle se soch kar banao
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/best-practices-for-api-design
← PehleAPI Gateway vs Load Balancer vs Reverse ProxyAgla →Best Practices for API Security
82System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

Best Practices for API Security

API security ka matlab hai: sirf wahi log API use karein jo authorized hain, aur data leak na ho. Pehli line of defense hai authentication — OAuth 2.0 ya API keys se pehchano kaun hai. Dusri hai authorization — pehchanne ke baad check karo use kya karne ki permission hai.

Teesri: HTTPS everywhere — bina TLS ke API kabhi mat chalao, kyunki tokens aur data plain text me jaayenge. Chauthi: rate limiting — brute force aur abuse se bachao. Paanchvi: input validation — har input ko validate karo (SQL injection, XSS se bachao). Chhathi: least privilege — har token ko sirf wahi permission do jo use chahiye, zyada nahi.

Saatvi: secrets ko kabhi client me mat rakho — API keys aur passwords server par rakho, frontend code me kabhi nahi. Aathvi: logging aur monitoring — har request log karo, taaki attack ka pata chal sake. API security me sabse badi galti hai "hum chhote hain, humein koi attack nahi karega" sochna — bots har API ko scan karte hain.

  • Core layers: auth (OAuth/API keys) → authorization → HTTPS → rate limiting → input validation → least privilege
  • Key trade-off: security (zyada checks, thoda slow) vs UX (kam friction) — security me compromise mat karo
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: API keys ko kabhi frontend ya git me mat daalo — leak hui key ko turant revoke karna aana chahiye
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/api-security-best-practices
← PehleBest Practices for API DesignAgla →API Versioning
83System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

API Versioning

API versioning ka matlab hai: API badle to purane clients na tootein. Jab aap API me breaking change karte ho (field hatana, response format badalna), to purane clients ko wahi purana behavior chahiye hota hai — isliye versions hote hain.

Teen common tareeke hain: URL versioning (/v1/users → /v2/users) — sabse common aur simple; header versioning (Accept: application/vnd.api.v2+json) — URL saaf rehta hai, lekin discover karna mushkil; query param (?version=2) — kam use hota hai.

Best practice hai: backward compatible changes bina version badhaye karo (naya field add karna safe hai), aur breaking changes ke liye naya version banao. Purane versions ko turant band mat karo — deprecation period do (jaise 6-12 mahine) aur clients ko migrate karne ka time do.

  • 3 tareeke: URL (/v1, /v2) — sabse common; header — saaf URL; query param — kam use hota hai
  • Key rule: additive changes (naya field) bina version ke; breaking changes ke liye naya version + deprecation period
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: version badhana aasan hai, purana version band karna mushkil — deprecation policy pehle se tay karo
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/api-versioning
← PehleBest Practices for API SecurityAgla →High Performance APIs
84System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

High Performance APIs

High performance API ka matlab hai: kam latency, zyada throughput. Iske liye har layer par optimization hoti hai — database, cache, serialization, aur network.

Pehla lever hai caching: frequently-read data ko Redis me rakho, taaki har request par database na chhoona pade. Dusra: database queries — N+1 query problem se bacho, indexes sahi lagao, aur sirf chahiye wahi columns select karo. Teesra: serialization — JSON ki jagah protobuf ya MessagePack use karo, response size aur parse time dono kam hote hain.

Chautha: connection pooling — har request par naya database connection banana mehenga hai, pool se reuse karo. Paanchva: async processing — heavy kaam (email, report) ko queue me daal kar turant response do. Chhatha: pagination aur field filtering — client ko sirf wahi data do jo use chahiye. Performance ka sabse bada dushman hai unnecessary kaam — har request par poochho "ye kaam zaroori hai ya nahi?"

  • Levers: caching → query optimization → binary serialization → connection pooling → async heavy work → pagination
  • Key trade-off: performance (complex, zyada code) vs simplicity (simple, lekin slow) — hot paths ko optimize karo, sab kuch nahi
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: pehle measure karo (profiling), phir optimize karo — bina measure kiye optimization aksar galat jagah hoti hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/api-performance
← PehleAPI VersioningAgla →API Testing Techniques (Part 1–2)
85System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

API Testing Techniques (Part 1–2)

API testing ke techniques do parts me cover hain — Part 1 testing ke types aur Part 2 advanced techniques. API ko test karna UI test karne se alag hai: yahan request/response, status codes, aur data contracts check hote hain.

Part 1 ke types hain: functional testing (endpoint sahi response deta hai ya nahi), contract testing (API ka response documented schema se milta hai ya nahi), validation testing (galat input par sahi error milta hai ya nahi), aur security testing (auth ke bina access rukta hai ya nahi).

Part 2 me aate hain: load testing (kitne requests per second handle hote hain), performance testing (latency kitni hai), chaos testing (dependency fail ho to API kya karti hai), aur end-to-end testing (poora flow — signup se payment tak). Contract testing microservices me sabse important hai, kyunki ek service ka badlav dusri ko tod sakta hai.

  • Part 1: functional → contract → validation → security testing
  • Part 2: load → performance → chaos → end-to-end testing
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: contract testing microservices me sabse zyada kaam aata hai — producer aur consumer ke beech contract hi sach hai

Dono parts ke links: Part 1 · Part 2

API design concepts ke do parts me wo sab kuch hai jo har software engineer ko API design ke baare me jaanna chahiye — Part 1 core concepts aur Part 2 advanced patterns.

  • Part 1: REST + naming + methods/status codes + pagination + errors
  • Part 2: versioning + idempotency + rate limiting + webhooks + GraphQL vs REST
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: API ka user developer hai — uski DX hi tumhari API ki quality hai

87System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

Bloom Filter

Bloom filter ek probabilistic data structure hai jo batata hai ki koi cheez set me "shayad hai" ya "pakka nahi hai". Iska use hota hai jahan memory kam hai aur false positives acceptable hain — jaise "ye username pehle se taken hai ya nahi" check karna.

Bloom filter kaam karta hai bit array + hash functions se: koi element add karte waqt use kai hash functions se hash karke bit array me kuch bits set kiye jaate hain. Check karte waqt wahi hash positions dekhi jaati hain — agar koi bit 0 hai to element pakka nahi hai; agar saari bits 1 hain to element shayad hai (false positive possible hai, false negative impossible).

Iska fayda hai memory: 1 million elements ke liye sirf ~1MB chahiye, jabki hash set me 10 guna zyada lagta. Isliye Bloom filters databases (Cassandra, HBase) me use hote hain — "ye key is SSTable me hai ya nahi" check karne ke liye, taaki bekaar disk reads bach sakein. CDN aur browsers me bhi use hote hain.

  • Core pattern: bit array + k hash functions; false positive possible, false negative impossible
  • Key numbers: 1M elements ~1MB me (1% false positive rate par)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: Bloom filter "nahi hai" ko pakka batata hai, "hai" ko shayad — isliye use wahan karo jahan negative answer se kaam bachta hai
Source:https://systemdesign.one/bloom-filters-explained/
← PehleAPI Design Concepts (Part 1–2)Agla →Cell Based Architecture
88System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

Cell Based Architecture

Cell-based architecture me system ko cells me baanta jaata hai — har cell ek self-contained unit hai jisme apna compute, storage aur data hota hai. Ek cell fail ho to dusre cells chalte rehte hain — blast radius sirf ek cell tak limited rehta hai.

Har cell me users ka ek subset hota hai (jaise 5% users), aur cells ke beech koi shared dependency nahi hoti. Agar ek cell ka database corrupt ho jaaye ya uska deploy fail ho to sirf us cell ke users affect hote hain — baaki 95% users ko pata bhi nahi chalta. Naya cell add karke capacity badhai ja sakti hai, aur purane cell ko drain karke hata sakte hain.

Ye architecture multi-tenant SaaS me bahut use hoti hai (jaise Slack, Shopify): har tenant ya tenant-group ek cell me hota hai. Isse noisy neighbor problem bhi solve hoti hai — ek tenant ka heavy load dusre tenant ko slow nahi karta. Lekin cells ke beech data share karna mushkil hota hai, isliye cross-cell features ko carefully design karna padta hai.

  • Core pattern: self-contained cells (compute + storage + data), no shared dependencies, limited blast radius
  • Key trade-off: isolation (ek cell fail ho to baaki safe) vs cross-cell features (mushkil aur mehenga)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: cell-based design ka maksad blast radius chhota karna hai — failure ko ek cell me qaid karo
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/cell-based-architecture
← PehleBloom FilterAgla →Consistency Patterns
89System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

Consistency Patterns

Consistency patterns batate hain ki distributed system me data ko consistent kaise rakha jaaye — kyunki jab data ki kai copies hon to har copy par same value hona mushkil hai. Teen main patterns hain: strong, eventual, aur causal consistency.

Strong consistency me har read latest write dikhata hai — bank balance jaisi cheezon ke liye zaroori hai. Eventual consistency me reads thode stale ho sakte hain, lekin eventually sab copies same ho jaati hain — social media feed ke liye kaafi hai. Causal consistency beech ka rasta hai — related writes ka order preserve hota hai (jaise comment apne post ke baad hi dikhe).

Iske alawa read-your-writes (apni likhi cheez turant dikhe), monotonic reads (pehle naya dikha to phir purana na dikhe), aur session consistency jaise patterns hain. Interview me jab bhi consistency poochha jaaye to pehle poochho: "is data ke liye kitni strong consistency chahiye?" — jawab se pattern chuno.

  • Patterns: strong → causal → eventual; + read-your-writes, monotonic reads, session consistency
  • Key trade-off: strong consistency (slow, lekin correct) vs eventual (fast, lekin stale reads)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: har data ko strong consistency nahi chahiye — consistency ka level data ki zaroorat se tay karo
Source:https://systemdesign.one/consistency-patterns/
← PehleCell Based ArchitectureAgla →Top 5 Caching Patterns
90System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

Top 5 Caching Patterns

Caching ke top 5 patterns har system design interview me aate hain: cache-aside, read-through, write-through, write-behind, aur refresh-ahead. Har pattern me cache aur database ke beech data ka flow alag hota hai.

Cache-aside me application pehle cache dekhti hai, miss ho to database se la kar cache me daalti hai — sabse common pattern. Read-through me cache khud database se laata hai — application ko pata bhi nahi chalta. Write-through me write cache aur database dono me ek saath hoti hai — consistency achhi, lekin write slow. Write-behind me write pehle cache me hoti hai, database me baad me async — fast write, lekin crash par data loss ka risk.

Refresh-ahead me cache expiry se pehle hi data refresh kar leta hai, taaki kabhi miss na ho. Pattern chunne ka rule hai: reads zyada hain to cache-aside/read-through; writes ki consistency important hai to write-through; writes bahut fast chahiye to write-behind (risk ke saath).

  • 5 patterns: cache-aside, read-through, write-through, write-behind, refresh-ahead
  • Key trade-off: consistency (write-through) vs speed (write-behind) vs simplicity (cache-aside)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: cache-aside se shuru karo — sabse simple aur sabse common; baaki patterns tab jab zaroorat ho
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/caching-patterns
← PehleConsistency PatternsAgla →How to Scale Code Reviews
91System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

How to Scale Code Reviews

Code review ko scale karna team badhne par mushkil ho jaata hai — pehle 5 logon ki team me har PR sab dekh lete the, lekin 100 logon ki team me review bottleneck ban jaata hai. Is entry me code review ko scale karne ke tareeke hain.

Pehla tareeka hai CODEOWNERS: har file/directory ka owner tay karo, taaki sahi reviewer automatically assign ho. Dusra: small PRs — chhote PRs jaldi review hote hain, bade PRs hafton latke rehte hain. Teesra: automation — linter, formatter aur tests se style aur basic galtiyan pehle hi pakdo, taaki reviewer sirf logic dekhe.

Chautha: review SLA — PR ko 24 ghante me review karne ka rule, taaki kaam ruke nahi. Paanchva: pair programming ya async review ka balance — critical code par pair, normal code par async review. Code review ka maksad sirf bugs pakadna nahi hai — knowledge sharing bhi hai, isliye review ko learning ka mauka banao.

  • Scale levers: CODEOWNERS + small PRs + automation (lint/tests) + review SLA + async review
  • Key trade-off: thorough review (slow, lekin quality) vs fast review (speed, lekin bugs nikal sakte hain)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: chhote PRs hi code review ko scale karte hain — 200-line PR review hota hai, 2000-line PR latka rehta hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/how-to-do-code-review
← PehleTop 5 Caching PatternsAgla →Code Review Best Practices
92System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

Code Review Best Practices

Code review best practices wo rules hain jo review ko useful banate hain — na ki ek formality. Pehli practice: review karo code ko, insaan ko nahi — comment me "ye galat hai" ki jagah "is approach me ye risk hai, isliye ye tareeka behtar hoga" likho.

Dusri: chhote aur focused comments — har comment ek issue par ho, aur suggestion ke saath ho. Teesri: nitpick aur critical ko alag rakho — naming aur formatting ke comments ko "nit" tag karo, taaki author ko pata chale kya zaroori hai aur kya optional. Chauthi: positive feedback bhi do — achha code dekh kar "nice" likhna bhi review ka hissa hai.

Paanchvi: khud bhi review ke liye taiyaar raho — apne PR me description likho, tests dikhao, aur reviewer ka time bachao. Chhathi: security aur edge cases par dhyaan do — happy path to author ne test kar liya hoga, tum edge cases dhoondho. Achha reviewer wo hai jiske comments se author kuch seekhe.

  • Core rules: code ko review karo, insaan ko nahi; nit vs critical alag karo; suggestion ke saath comment karo
  • Key trade-off: strict review (quality, lekin slow) vs lenient review (fast, lekin bugs nikal sakte hain)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: review ka maksad author ko behtar banana hai, use haraana nahi — tone hi sab kuch hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/code-review-best-practices
← PehleHow to Scale Code ReviewsAgla →Consistent Hashing
93System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

Consistent Hashing

Consistent hashing ek aisi hashing technique hai jisme nodes add ya remove karne par sirf thoda sa data move hota hai — normal hashing me ek node badalte hi lagbhag saara data dobara hash hota hai. Ye distributed caches aur databases ki neev hai.

Normal hashing me key ka hash lekar hash % N se node chuna jaata hai — N badalte hi saare keys ki jagah badal jaati hai. Consistent hashing me keys aur nodes dono ko ek circular ring par rakha jaata hai — har key us node par jaati hai jo ring par uske baad aata hai. Node add ho to sirf uske hisse ka data move hota hai, baaki sab wahi rehta hai.

Virtual nodes is technique ko aur behtar banate hain: har physical node ring par kai virtual positions leta hai, taaki load evenly baante. Dynamo, Cassandra aur Memcached — sab consistent hashing use karte hain. Interview me ye concept caching aur sharding dono me poochha jaata hai.

  • Core pattern: hash ring — key → next node clockwise; virtual nodes for even distribution
  • Key numbers: node add/remove par sirf 1/N data move hota hai (N = nodes), normal hashing me ~100%
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: consistent hashing ka maksad hai node badalne par data movement kam karna — yehi distributed caching ka raaz hai
Source:https://systemdesign.one/consistent-hashing-explained/
← PehleCode Review Best PracticesAgla →Concurrency Is Not Parallelism
94System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

Concurrency Is Not Parallelism

Concurrency aur parallelism do alag cheezein hain — inhe confuse karna common galti hai. Concurrency ka matlab hai kai kaam ek saath manage karna (ek waqt par ek hi chal raha ho, lekin system unke beech switch kar raha ho). Parallelism ka matlab hai kai kaam ek saath execute karna (alag cores par, sach me ek hi waqt par).

Example: ek waiter jo 5 tables ko serve karta hai — wo concurrent hai (har table ka order leta hai, kitchen ko deta hai, wapas aata hai), lekin parallel nahi (wo ek waqt par ek hi kaam karta hai). 5 waiters hon to wo parallel hain. Computer me single-core par threads concurrent hote hain (time-slicing se), aur multi-core par parallel hote hain.

System design me ye farak isliye important hai kyunki I/O-bound kaam (network, disk) concurrency se fayda uthata hai — ek request ka wait karte waqt dusri process kar lo. Lekin CPU-bound kaam (calculation) ko parallelism chahiye — aur cores, nahi to wo fast nahi hoga. Go ki goroutines aur Erlang ke processes concurrency ke liye bane hain.

  • Concurrency: kai kaam manage karna (switch karke) — I/O-bound ke liye best
  • Parallelism: kai kaam ek saath execute karna (alag cores) — CPU-bound ke liye zaroori
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: I/O wait karte waqt concurrency kaam aati hai, calculation me parallelism — pehle pehchano kaam kis type ka hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/concurrency-is-not-parallelism
← PehleConsistent HashingAgla →15 Pitfalls That Break Cloud Systems
95System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

15 Pitfalls That Break Cloud Systems

Cloud systems ko todne wale 15 pitfalls — ye wo galtiyan hain jo har team kam se kam ek baar karti hai. Pehle 5: single point of failure (ek AZ ya ek database par sab kuch), no retries with backoff (fail hone par turant dobara try karna aur system ko aur load dena), unbounded queues (queue bhar jaaye to memory khatam), missing timeouts (ek slow dependency poora system rok de), aur no circuit breaker (fail hui service ko baar-baar call karna).

Agle 5: ignoring backpressure (producer consumer se zyada fast ho), chatty services (har chhote kaam ke liye network call), no idempotency (retry par double kaam), secrets in code (API keys git me), aur no observability (logs/metrics bina debugging impossible).

Aakhri 5: over-provisioning (paisa waste), under-provisioning (peak par crash), no load testing (production hi pehla test), manual deploys (human error), aur ignoring cost (bill dekh kar hairaan hona). In 15 me se har ek ka solution is SPA ke alag sections me hai — ye section unka checklist hai.

  • Top 5 killer pitfalls: SPOF, no timeouts, no circuit breaker, unbounded queues, no idempotency
  • Key principle: har pitfall ka solution ek pattern hai — timeout, breaker, backoff, idempotency, observability
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: cloud me failure normal hai — jo system failure ko handle nahi karta, wo tootega hi
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/cloud-system-design
← PehleConcurrency Is Not ParallelismAgla →Cybersecurity Terms Every Software Engineer Must Know
96System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

Cybersecurity Terms Every Software Engineer Must Know

Cybersecurity ke terms har software engineer ko jaanne chahiye — kyunki security ab sirf security team ka kaam nahi hai, har developer ki zimmedari hai. Sabse pehle: authentication vs authorization — pehchan (kaun ho) vs permission (kya kar sakte ho).

Encryption data ko unreadable banata hai (at rest aur in transit), hashing one-way hoti hai (passwords ke liye), aur salting hash ko unique banata hai. XSS (Cross-Site Scripting) me attacker tumhari site par script chala deta hai, CSRF me user ke browser se fake request bhejta hai, aur SQL injection me query me malicious code daalta hai.

Zero trust ka matlab hai: network ke andar bhi kisi par bharosa mat karo — har request verify karo. Least privilege ka matlab hai: har service/user ko sirf wahi permission do jo use chahiye. OWASP Top 10 web vulnerabilities ki standard list hai — use ek baar zaroor padho.

  • Must-know terms: authN vs authZ, encryption, hashing + salting, XSS, CSRF, SQL injection, zero trust, least privilege, OWASP Top 10
  • Key trade-off: security (zyada checks) vs convenience (kam friction) — security me compromise mat karo
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: sabse common attacks (injection, XSS) ka solution input validation aur parameterized queries hain — basic cheezein hi sabse zyada kaam aati hain
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/cybersecurity-fundamentals

The Entire Computer Science Stack, Explained In 51 Images

Ye entry poora computer science stack 51 images me samjhati hai — hardware se lekar application tak. Stack ka matlab hai layers: hardware → OS → networking → databases → backend → frontend — aur har layer ke upar agli layer banti hai.

Neeche ki layers hain: transistors, logic gates, CPU, memory hierarchy (registers → cache → RAM → disk). Uske upar OS aata hai: processes, threads, scheduling, file systems. Phir networking: TCP/IP, DNS, HTTP. Phir data: SQL/NoSQL, indexes, transactions. Phir backend: APIs, queues, caching. Aur sabse upar frontend: HTML, CSS, JavaScript, aur frameworks.

Is stack ko samajhne ka fayda ye hai: jab koi problem aaye to pata chalta hai wo kis layer ki hai — slow query database layer ki hai, packet loss network layer ki, aur memory leak application layer ki. Interview me full-stack soch dikhana bahut pasand kiya jaata hai.

  • Stack layers: hardware → OS → networking → data → backend → frontend (51 images, har layer visual)
  • Key principle: har layer apne neeche wali layer par abstraction banati hai — aur har abstraction ka cost hota hai
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: problem aaye to layer-by-layer neeche jao — jawab hamesha kisi ek layer me milta hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/computer-science-101
← PehleCybersecurity Terms Every Software Engineer Must KnowAgla →How CDN Works
98System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

How CDN Works

CDN (Content Delivery Network) duniya bhar me faile servers ka network hai jo content ko user ke sabse paas se serve karta hai. Bina CDN ke, har user ko content origin server (jo shayad doosre continent me hai) se aata — slow aur mehenga.

CDN kaam karta hai edge caching se: jab pehla user kisi file ko request karta hai to edge server use origin se la kar apne paas cache kar leta hai. Agle users ko wahi file edge se milti hai — milliseconds me. Popular content lagbhag hamesha edge par hota hai, isliye origin par load bahut kam aata hai.

CDN me TTL (Time To Live) tay karta hai ki content edge par kitni der rahega. Static content (images, videos, JS) ka TTL lamba hota hai; dynamic content ka chhota ya zero. Cache purging se content ko turant edge se hataya ja sakta hai jab wo update ho. Cloudflare, Akamai aur CloudFront duniya ke bade CDNs hain.

  • Core pattern: edge servers + caching + TTL + purging; origin sirf pehli baar
  • Key numbers: edge se latency ~10-50ms, origin se ~200-500ms (cross-continent)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: static content ko hamesha CDN se serve karo — origin ko sirf dynamic requests milni chahiye
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/how-does-cdn-work
← PehleThe Entire Computer Science Stack, Explained In 51 ImagesAgla →How CI/CD Works
99System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

How CI/CD Works

CI/CD ka matlab hai Continuous Integration / Continuous Deployment — code ko baar-baar aur safely production me pahunchane ka automated tareeka. CI ka matlab hai: har developer ka code roz main branch me merge hota hai aur automated tests chalte hain. CD ka matlab hai: tests pass hote hi code automatically production me deploy ho jaata hai.

CI/CD pipeline ke stages hote hain: build → test → deploy. Build me code compile hota hai aur artifacts bante hain. Test me unit, integration aur end-to-end tests chalte hain. Deploy me code pehle staging par, phir production par jaata hai — aksar canary ya blue-green tareeke se, taaki gadbad ho to turant wapas jaa sakein.

CI/CD ka sabse bada fayda hai chhote aur frequent releases: ek badi release me 100 changes hote hain aur kuch tootta hai to pata nahi chalta kya toota; roz ki chhoti releases me har change traceable hota hai. Isliye aaj ki best teams din me kai baar deploy karti hain.

  • Pipeline: build → test → deploy (staging → canary/blue-green → production)
  • Key trade-off: fast deploys (zyada releases, lekin risk) vs slow deploys (safe, lekin features late)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: CI/CD ka maksad speed nahi, safety ke saath speed hai — automated tests ke bina CD khatarnak hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/how-does-a-ci-cd-pipeline-work
← PehleHow CDN WorksAgla →How Databases Keep Passwords Securely
100System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

How Databases Keep Passwords Securely

Databases me passwords ko kabhi plain text me store nahi kiya jaata — ye security ka pehla rule hai. Agar database leak bhi ho jaaye to passwords safe rehne chahiye. Iske liye hashing + salting use hota hai.

Hashing one-way hoti hai: password ko hash function se ek fixed-length string me badla jaata hai, aur wapas password nahi nikala ja sakta. Login karte waqt user ka password hash karke stored hash se compare kiya jaata hai. Salt ek random value hai jo har password ke saath jodi jaati hai — taaki do users ka same password bhi alag hash bane, aur precomputed rainbow tables kaam na karein.

Modern best practice hai slow hashing algorithms use karna — bcrypt, scrypt ya Argon2. Ye algorithms jaan-boojh kar slow hote hain (ek hash ~100ms), taaki attacker brute force na kar sake. Fast hash (MD5, SHA-256) passwords ke liye galat hai — wo bahut fast hain, isliye attacker billions of guesses per second kar sakta hai.

  • Core pattern: password + unique salt → slow hash (bcrypt/Argon2) → store hash + salt
  • Key numbers: bcrypt ~100ms per hash (attacker ke liye slow, user ke liye acceptable)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: passwords ke liye kabhi fast hash (MD5/SHA) mat use karo — slow hash hi sahi hai; aur plain text to kabhi nahi
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/how-to-store-passwords-in-database
← PehleHow CI/CD WorksAgla →How DNS Works
101System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

How DNS Works

DNS (Domain Name System) internet ki phonebook hai — ye domain names (jaise google.com) ko IP addresses me badalta hai. Bina DNS ke aapko har website ka IP yaad rakhna padta.

DNS lookup ka flow hai: browser pehle local cache dekhta hai, phir OS resolver ko poochhta hai, phir recursive resolver (ISP ya 8.8.8.8) ko. Recursive resolver root server se poochhta hai (".com kahan hai?"), phir TLD server se (".com" wala), phir authoritative server se (jo google.com ka IP jaanta hai). Har step ka jawab cache hota hai, taaki agli baar lookup fast ho.

DNS me TTL batata hai ki ek record kitni der cache me rahega. DNS ke records hote hain: A (IPv4), AAAA (IPv6), CNAME (alias), MX (mail server), TXT (verification). DNS hi load balancing bhi karta hai — ek domain ke kai IPs de kar traffic baanta ja sakta hai.

  • Lookup chain: browser cache → OS → recursive resolver → root → TLD → authoritative → IP
  • Key numbers: DNS lookup ~20-100ms; TTL se caching control hoti hai
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: DNS hierarchy hi internet ko scale karta hai — har level apna hissa cache karta hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/what-is-a-dns-server-and-how-does-it-work
← PehleHow Databases Keep Passwords SecurelyAgla →Deployment Patterns
102System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

Deployment Patterns

Deployment patterns wo tareeke hain jinse naya code production me pahunchaya jaata hai — bina users ko pareshan kiye. Sabse common patterns hain: rolling, blue-green, aur canary.

Rolling deployment me servers ko ek-ek karke update kiya jaata hai — purana version hat-ta hai, naya aata hai, aur traffic hamesha chalta rehta hai. Blue-green me do environments hote hain — blue (current) aur green (new); green taiyaar ho to traffic ek saath switch kar diya jaata hai, aur gadbad ho to turant wapas. Canary me naya version pehle chhote % users (jaise 5%) ko diya jaata hai, metrics dekhe jaate hain, aur sab theek ho to dheere-dheere 100% par le jaaya jaata hai.

Har pattern ka apna cost hai: rolling sasta lekin slow rollback; blue-green fast rollback lekin double resources; canary sabse safe lekin complex. Aaj ke standard me canary + automated rollback sabse behtar mana jaata hai.

  • 3 patterns: rolling (ek-ek karke) → blue-green (ek saath switch, fast rollback) → canary (5% → 100%, metrics dekh kar)
  • Key trade-off: safety (canary) vs cost (blue-green needs double resources) vs simplicity (rolling)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: deploy ka maksad sirf naya code pahunchana nahi hai — gadbad ho to turant wapas jaana bhi hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/deployment-patterns
← PehleHow DNS WorksAgla →Distributed Systems Deep Dive
103System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

Distributed Systems Deep Dive

Distributed systems wo systems hain jo kai machines par chalte hain aur ek saath kaam karte hain — aur inki sabse badi mushkil hai: machines fail hoti hain, network fail hota hai, aur time sync nahi hota. In teen sach ko accept karke hi distributed system design hota hai.

Is deep dive ke core topics hain: consistency models (strong vs eventual), consensus (Raft/Paxos — machines kaise agree karein), partition tolerance (network tutne par kya ho), replication (copies kaise sync hon), aur failure detection (kaunsi machine zinda hai, kaise pata chale).

Distributed systems me sabse important mental model hai CAP theorem aur fallacies of distributed computing — jaise "network reliable hai", "latency zero hai", "bandwidth infinite hai" — ye sab galat assumptions hain. Jo engineer inhe samajhta hai, wahi sahi distributed system bana sakta hai.

  • Core topics: consistency → consensus (Raft/Paxos) → partition tolerance → replication → failure detection
  • Key principle: network kabhi reliable nahi hota — har design me failure ko pehle se socho
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: distributed system me "sab kuch theek hai" assume karna hi sabse badi galti hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/distributed-systems
← PehleDeployment PatternsAgla →How Docker Works
104System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

How Docker Works

Docker containers ko banane aur chalane ka platform hai — container ek halka, portable package hai jisme app aur uski dependencies hoti hain. VM se farak ye hai: container host OS ka kernel share karta hai, isliye ye seconds me start hota hai aur bahut kam memory leta hai.

Docker ke peeche do Linux technologies hain: namespaces (har container ko apna isolated view milta hai — apna process list, network, filesystem) aur cgroups (har container ko CPU/memory ki limit milti hai). Docker image layers se banti hai — har Dockerfile instruction ek layer hai, aur layers cache hoti hain, isliye rebuild fast hota hai.

Docker ka workflow hai: Dockerfile → image → container. Image ek template hai, container uska running instance. Kubernetes jaise orchestrators in containers ko kai machines par chalate hain. Aaj ki deployment ka standard hai: app ko container me pack karo, phir use kahin bhi chalao — laptop, server, ya cloud.

  • Core tech: namespaces (isolation) + cgroups (resource limits) + layered images
  • Key trade-off: containers (fast, halke, shared kernel) vs VMs (poori isolation, lekin bhaari)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: container ka matlab hai "app + dependencies, ek package me" — "mere laptop par to chalta hai" problem ka jawab hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/how-do-docker-containers-work
← PehleDistributed Systems Deep DiveAgla →16 Database Types
105System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

16 Database Types

Duniya me 16 tarah ke databases hain — aur har ek ka apna use case hai. Sabse pehle relational (SQL): Postgres, MySQL — structured data aur transactions ke liye. Key-value: Redis, DynamoDB — fast lookups ke liye. Document: MongoDB — flexible JSON-like data ke liye.

Wide-column: Cassandra, HBase — massive writes aur time-series ke liye. Graph: Neo4j — relationships (friends, recommendations) ke liye. Time-series: InfluxDB, TimescaleDB — metrics aur events ke liye. Search: Elasticsearch — full-text search ke liye. Vector: Pinecone, pgvector — AI embeddings ke liye.

Baaki types hain: columnar (analytics), in-memory (cache), ledger (immutable records), spatial (location), object, multi-model, aur NewSQL (Spanner, CockroachDB — SQL + distributed scale). Interview me jab bhi database chuno to pehle data model aur access pattern batao, phir type chuno — "MongoDB use karenge" bina reason ke galat jawab hai.

  • Main types: relational, key-value, document, wide-column, graph, time-series, search, vector, columnar, NewSQL (+6 aur)
  • Key rule: pehle data model + access pattern, phir database type — ulta kram galat hai
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: ek system me kai database types hote hain — har access pattern ke liye sahi type chuno
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/types-of-databases-and-when-to-use-them
