21 Frontend System Design Concepts for Software Engineers

Frontend system design ke 21 concepts — ye wo topics hain jo frontend interviews me poochhe jaate hain: rendering, state management, performance, aur architecture. Backend system design se alag, yahan focus hai browser aur user experience par.

Core concepts hain: SSR vs CSR (server-side vs client-side rendering), hydration, code-splitting, virtual DOM, state management (Redux/Zustand), caching strategies (service workers), performance metrics (LCP, FID, CLS), accessibility, aur design systems.

Frontend system design interview me aksar poochha jaata hai: "Design a news feed / autocomplete / chat UI" — aur iska jawab components, state flow, API design aur performance se banta hai. Backend ki tarah yahan bhi trade-offs hote hain: SSR (fast first load, lekin server load) vs CSR (rich interactions, lekin slow first load).

  • Core concepts: SSR/CSR, hydration, code-splitting, virtual DOM, state management, service workers, Core Web Vitals
  • Key trade-off: SSR (fast first paint, server cost) vs CSR (rich UX, slow first paint)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: frontend me performance user ko dikhti hai — LCP, CLS jaise metrics hi tumhare design ki report card hain
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/frontend-system-design
← Pehle16 Database TypesAgla →Gossip Protocol
107System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

Gossip Protocol

Gossip protocol ek aisa tareeka hai jisse distributed system ke nodes ek dusre ko state batate hain — jaise log gossip karte hain: har node randomly kuch nodes ko apni jaankari batata hai, aur wo aage batate hain. Kuch rounds me saara system update ho jaata hai.

Protocol simple hai: har node har kuch second me random peers ko apna state bhejta hai. Jo node naya state paata hai, wo use apne state me merge karke aage bhejta hai. Isse information exponentially failti hai — jaise virus failta hai. Kuch hi rounds me saare nodes ko pata chal jaata hai.

Gossip ka use hota hai failure detection (Cassandra, Dynamo me — kaunsi node zinda hai), membership (cluster me kaun hai), aur state propagation me. Iska fayda hai: koi central server nahi chahiye, aur kuch nodes fail bhi hon to gossip chalti rehti hai. Nuksaan hai: information pahunchne me time lagta hai (eventual consistency), aur exact timing guarantee nahi hoti.

  • Core pattern: random peer selection + periodic state exchange → exponential spread
  • Key trade-off: no central server (resilient) vs eventual propagation (slow convergence)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: gossip se "sabko pata chal jaayega" — lekin "kab" ka guarantee nahi hota; failure detection ke liye best hai
Source:https://systemdesign.one/gossip-protocol/
← Pehle21 Frontend System Design Concepts for Software EngineersAgla →21 Git Commands
108System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

21 Git Commands

Git ke 21 commands jo har software engineer ko aane chahiye — version control ki neev. Pehle basics: clone, status, add, commit, push, pull — ye roz ke commands hain.

Phir branching: branch, checkout/switch, merge, rebase — feature branch banao, kaam karo, wapas merge karo. rebase history ko saaf rakhta hai, merge history ko sachha rakhta hai — dono ka apna use hai. stash se beech ka kaam save karo, cherry-pick se ek commit ko dusri branch me le jao.

Advanced: log, blame, bisect (bug dhoondhne ke liye), reset, revert (galti wapas lene ke liye — reset history badalta hai, revert naya commit banata hai), reflog (khoya hua commit dhoondhne ke liye), aur tag (releases mark karne ke liye). Git ko samajhne ka raaz hai: commit ek snapshot hai, branch ek pointer hai — ye samajh lo to sab commands aasan lagti hain.

  • Daily commands: status, add, commit, push, pull, branch, merge/rebase
  • Rescue commands: stash, reflog, reset vs revert, bisect
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: commit = snapshot, branch = pointer — ye model samajh lo to Git kabhi confusing nahi lagega
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/commands-in-git
← PehleGossip ProtocolAgla →How GitOps Works
109System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

How GitOps Works

GitOps ka matlab hai: Git ko infrastructure ka source of truth banana. Servers aur Kubernetes clusters ki desired state Git repo me likhi hoti hai (YAML files), aur ek agent (jaise ArgoCD) continuously check karta hai ki cluster ki actual state Git se match karti hai ya nahi — nahi karti to use sync kar deta hai.

GitOps ka flow hai: developer Git me change karta hai (PR) → review aur merge → ArgoCD change detect karta hai → cluster par apply karta hai. Isse har change reviewed, versioned aur auditable hota hai — kisne, kab, kya badla, sab Git history me hai. Rollback ka matlab hai purana commit par wapas jaana — simple aur reliable.

Traditional tareeka tha: kubectl ya dashboard se seedha cluster badlo — isme pata nahi chalta kisne kya badla, aur rollback mushkil hota hai. GitOps se infrastructure bhi code ki tarah treat hoti hai — isliye ise Infrastructure as Code ka next step kehte hain.

  • Core pattern: Git (desired state) → ArgoCD (sync agent) → cluster (actual state), continuous reconciliation
  • Key trade-off: GitOps (auditable, safe, lekin Git hi single source — Git down ho to problem) vs direct kubectl (fast, lekin untracked)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: infrastructure me bhi "code review" hona chahiye — GitOps wahi deta hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/how-argocd-works-gitops-kubernetes
← Pehle21 Git CommandsAgla →Hinted Handoff
110System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

Hinted Handoff

Hinted handoff ek aisa mechanism hai jo temporary node failure ko handle karta hai — jab data ka asli owner node down ho to uska data kisi aur node par temporarily store kar diya jaata hai, aur owner wapas aate hi use de diya jaata hai.

Kaam aise karta hai: coordinator node ko pata hai ki key ka owner kaun hai. Agar owner down hai to coordinator data ko ek hint ke saath kisi aur node par likh deta hai — hint me likha hota hai "ye data actually node X ka hai". Jab node X wapas aata hai to hint wala node use data de deta hai — handoff ho jaata hai.

Isse system write availability bani rehti hai — node down hone par bhi writes fail nahi hote. Hinted handoff Dynamo aur Cassandra me use hota hai. Lekin ye sirf temporary failures ke liye hai — agar node lambe time tak down rahe to anti-entropy (repair) se data sync karna padta hai.

  • Core pattern: write to temp node with hint → owner returns → handoff the data
  • Key trade-off: write availability (fail hone par bhi write chalega) vs extra storage aur complexity
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: hinted handoff temporary hai — permanent failure ke liye anti-entropy repair chahiye
Source:https://systemdesign.one/hinted-handoff/
← PehleHow GitOps WorksAgla →How Does HTTPS Work
111System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

How Does HTTPS Work

HTTPS ka matlab hai HTTP + TLS — web traffic ko encrypt karne ka tareeka. Bina HTTPS ke, aapka data (passwords, card numbers) plain text me jaata hai aur beech me koi bhi padh sakta hai. HTTPS se data encrypted hota hai aur server ki identity verify hoti hai.

TLS handshake ka flow hai: browser server ko "hello" bhejta hai → server apna certificate bhejta hai (jisme uski public key hoti hai) → browser certificate ko Certificate Authority (CA) se verify karta hai → dono mil kar ek session key banate hain (asymmetric se symmetric me switch) → uske baad saara data session key se encrypt hota hai.

HTTPS ke teen fayde hain: confidentiality (koi beech me data padh nahi sakta), integrity (data badla nahi ja sakta), aur authentication (server wahi hai jo wo kehta hai). Aaj ke browsers HTTP sites ko "Not Secure" batate hain — isliye har production site par HTTPS hona chahiye.

  • Handshake: hello → certificate → CA verify → session key → encrypted data
  • 3 guarantees: confidentiality + integrity + authentication
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: TLS me asymmetric crypto sirf handshake ke liye hai — actual data symmetric key se encrypt hota hai (fast)
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/how-does-https-work
← PehleHinted HandoffAgla →Must Know HTTP Headers
112System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

Must Know HTTP Headers

HTTP headers wo metadata hain jo request aur response ke saath jaate hain — aur inhe jaanna har backend engineer ke liye zaroori hai. Sabse important headers hain: Content-Type (data kis format me hai), Authorization (token), Cache-Control (cache kitni der rahe), aur Content-Length.

Security headers hain: Strict-Transport-Security (hamesha HTTPS use karo), Content-Security-Policy (kaunsi scripts chal sakti hain — XSS se bachao), X-Content-Type-Options (MIME sniffing roko), aur X-Frame-Options (clickjacking se bachao).

Performance headers hain: ETag/If-None-Match (cache validation), Accept-Encoding (gzip/br support), aur Keep-Alive. CORS headers (Access-Control-Allow-Origin) batate hain kaunsi sites tumhari API ko browser se call kar sakti hain. Interview me headers ka sawaal aaye to security aur caching wale headers zaroor batao.

  • Must-know: Content-Type, Authorization, Cache-Control, ETag, Strict-Transport-Security, CSP, CORS headers
  • Key trade-off: zyada headers (zyada control) vs har header ka thoda overhead
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: security headers ko default on rakho — CSP aur HSTS lagana 5 minute ka kaam hai aur bahut bade attacks rokta hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/http-headers
← PehleHow Does HTTPS WorkAgla →What Is High Availability
113System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

What Is High Availability

High availability (HA) ka matlab hai: system zyada se zyada time up rahe — downtime kam se kam ho. Availability ko "nines" me naapa jaata hai: 99.9% (3 nines) = saal me ~8.7 ghante downtime, 99.99% (4 nines) = ~52 minute, 99.999% (5 nines) = ~5 minute.

HA haasil karne ke tareeke hain: redundancy (har component ki kam se kam 2 copies), failover (ek fail ho to dusra sambhale — automatic hona chahiye), multi-AZ/multi-region deployment (ek data center ya region down ho to dusra chale), aur health checks (kharab instance ko traffic se hatao).

HA ka sabse bada dushman hai single point of failure (SPOF) — wo component jiske fail hone se poora system ruk jaaye. Har design me pehle SPOF dhoondho aur use hatao. Lekin yaad rakho: har extra nine ka cost 10 guna badhta hai — 99.9% se 99.99% jaana 99% se 99.9% jaane se kai guna mehenga hai. Business se poochho kitni availability chahiye, phir design karo.

  • Availability math: 99.9% = 8.7h/year downtime; 99.99% = 52min; 99.999% = 5min
  • Core pattern: redundancy + automatic failover + multi-AZ/region + health checks
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: har extra nine 10 guna mehenga hai — availability ka target business se lo, apne man se nahi
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/what-is-high-availability
← PehleMust Know HTTP HeadersAgla →HTTP1 vs HTTP2 vs HTTP3 (Part 1–2)
114System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

HTTP1 vs HTTP2 vs HTTP3 (Part 1–2)

HTTP ke teen versions — 1.1, 2 aur 3 — har version ne pehle wale ki kami ko door kiya. HTTP/1.1 me har request ke liye alag connection ya head-of-line blocking hoti thi — ek slow request ke peeche saari requests ruk jaati thin.

HTTP/2 ne multiplexing la di: ek hi connection par kai requests/responses ek saath chal sakti hain, aur header compression (HPACK) se headers chhote ho gaye. Server push se server client ko pehle se resources bhej sakta hai. Lekin HTTP/2 TCP par chalta hai, to TCP-level head-of-line blocking abhi bhi thi — ek packet kho jaaye to poora connection ruk jaata hai.

HTTP/3 ne TCP ko hi hata kar QUIC (UDP-based) par chalaya: har stream independent hai, ek stream ka packet kho jaaye to dusri streams chalti rehti hain. Handshake bhi fast hai (0-RTT). Aaj ke browsers aur CDNs HTTP/3 support karte hain, aur performance me khaas taur par lossy networks (mobile) par bahut farak padta hai.

  • HTTP/1.1: ek connection, ek request — head-of-line blocking
  • HTTP/2: multiplexing + header compression (TCP par — TCP blocking abhi bhi)
  • HTTP/3: QUIC over UDP — per-stream independence, 0-RTT, mobile par best
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: har version ne pehle wale ki blocking hatayi — HTTP/3 ka jawab hai "TCP hi hata do"

Dono parts ke links: Part 1 · Part 2

Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/http-concepts-explained
← PehleWhat Is High AvailabilityAgla →How JWT Works
115System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

How JWT Works

JWT (JSON Web Token) ek aisa token hai jo user ki identity ko securely carry karta hai — bina har request par database check kiye. JWT ke teen hisse hote hain: header, payload, aur signature — teeno base64 me encode hokar dots se jude hote hain.

Flow hai: user login karta hai → server JWT banata hai (payload me user id, expiry, etc.) aur secret key se sign karta hai → client token ko store karta hai → har request me token bhejta hai (Authorization: Bearer <token>) → server signature verify karke token ko trust karta hai — database check kiye bina.

JWT ka fayda hai statelessness: server ko sessions store nahi karne padte, isliye horizontally scale karna aasan hai. Nuksaan hai: token ko revoke karna mushkil hai — ek baar token mil gaya to expiry tak valid hai (isliye expiry chhoti rakho aur refresh tokens use karo). Aur JWT me sensitive data mat daalo — payload sirf encode hota hai, encrypt nahi.

  • Structure: header.payload.signature — signature se tampering pakdi jaati hai
  • Key trade-off: stateless (scale aasan, lekin revoke mushkil) vs sessions (revoke aasan, lekin state chahiye)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: JWT me kabhi password ya sensitive data mat daalo — payload padha ja sakta hai, sirf signature verify hoti hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/how-jwt-works
← PehleHTTP1 vs HTTP2 vs HTTP3 (Part 1–2)Agla →How Kafka Works
116System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

How Kafka Works

Kafka ek distributed event streaming platform hai — ye events ko durably store karta hai aur kai consumers ko real-time me deliver karta hai. Kafka ko "distributed commit log" bhi kehte hain — ye uski sabse sahi definition hai.

Kafka ke core concepts hain: topics (event categories), partitions (topic ko baantna — har partition ek ordered log hai), producers (events likhte hain), consumers (events padhte hain), aur consumer groups (kai consumers mil kar ek topic ko parallel me process karte hain). Har event ka ek offset hota hai — consumer apna offset track karta hai, taaki crash ke baad wahi se dobara padh sake.

Kafka ki khaasiyat hai durability + replay: events disk par store hote hain aur configurable time tak rehte hain — consumer chahe to purane events dobara padh sakta hai. Isliye Kafka sirf message queue nahi hai, ye event log hai — aur event sourcing, CDC, aur stream processing (Kafka Streams, Flink) ka foundation hai.

  • Core concepts: topic → partitions (ordered logs) → producers/consumers → consumer groups + offsets
  • Key trade-off: Kafka (durable, replayable, lekin operationally bhaari) vs simple queue (halka, lekin no replay)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: Kafka me message delete nahi hota, offset aage badhta hai — replay hi iski superpower hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/how-kafka-works
← PehleHow JWT WorksAgla →How Kubernetes Works
117System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

How Kubernetes Works

Kubernetes (K8s) containers ko kai machines par chalane aur manage karne ka system hai — container orchestration. Aap batate ho "mujhe is app ki 3 copies chahiye", aur Kubernetes use ensure karta hai — server crash ho to bhi.

Kubernetes ke core concepts hain: Pod (ek ya zyada containers ki unit), Deployment (kitne replicas chahiye, kaise update honge), Service (pods tak stable network address), Ingress (bahar se traffic), ConfigMap/Secret (configuration), aur StatefulSet (stateful apps ke liye).

Control plane me API server, scheduler, aur controller manager hote hain — ye desired state (Git ya YAML me) ko actual state se match karte rehte hain. Agar ek pod crash ho to controller naya pod bana deta hai — isse self-healing milti hai. Kubernetes ka sabse bada fayda hai declarative management: aap "kya chahiye" batate ho, "kaise hoga" Kubernetes tay karta hai.

  • Core objects: Pod → Deployment → Service → Ingress (+ ConfigMap/Secret)
  • Key principle: desired state vs actual state — control plane hamesha dono ko match karta rehta hai
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: Kubernetes ka maksad hai "app hamesha chalni chahiye" — self-healing aur autoscaling iske core hain
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/kubernetes-architecture
← PehleHow Kafka WorksAgla →How Load Balancing Algorithms Work
118System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

How Load Balancing Algorithms Work

Load balancing algorithms tay karte hain ki aane wala request kis server par jaaye — aur sahi algorithm se throughput aur latency dono behtar hote hain. Sabse common algorithms hain: round robin, least connections, IP hash, weighted, aur least response time.

Round robin requests ko baari-baari servers me baant ta hai — simple, lekin ye nahi dekhta kaunsa server busy hai. Least connections us server ko chunta hai jiske paas sabse kam active connections hain — long-lived connections ke liye best hai. IP hash client IP se server chunta hai — same client hamesha same server par jaata hai (session persistence).

Weighted round robin powerful servers ko zyada requests deta hai, aur least response time us server ko chunta hai jo sabse fast respond karta hai. Algorithm chunne ka rule: short requests ke liye round robin kaafi hai; long-lived ya uneven load ke liye least connections; aur session stickiness chahiye to IP hash.

  • Algorithms: round robin (simple) → least connections (busy-aware) → IP hash (sticky) → weighted (power-aware) → least response time (fastest)
  • Key trade-off: simplicity (round robin) vs fairness (least connections) vs stickiness (IP hash)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: algorithm ko workload se chuno — short requests me round robin kaafi hai, long connections me least connections
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/load-balancing-algorithms
← PehleHow Kubernetes WorksAgla →Micro Frontends
119System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

Micro Frontends

Micro frontends ka idea hai: frontend ko bhi microservices ki tarah tod do — har team apna hissa independently develop, test aur deploy kare. Ek badi monolithic frontend ki jagah, page alag-alag independent apps se banta hai.

Implement karne ke tareeke hain: Module Federation (Webpack — runtime par micro apps load hote hain), iframes (sabse simple, lekin UX aur performance me compromise), web components (native browser standard), ya server-side composition (edge/server par page assemble hota hai).

Micro frontends ka fayda hai team independence — har team apni tech stack aur release cycle rakh sakti hai. Nuksaan hai complexity: shared design system, consistent UX, performance (kai bundles), aur cross-app communication — ye sab manage karna padta hai. Ye pattern sirf badi organisations ke liye hai jahan kai teams ek hi frontend par kaam karti hain.

  • Approaches: Module Federation, iframes, web components, server-side composition
  • Key trade-off: team independence (fast releases) vs complexity (shared UX, performance, communication)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: micro frontend sirf tab jab kai teams ek frontend share karti hain — chhoti team ke liye monolith hi behtar hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/micro-frontends
← PehleHow Load Balancing Algorithms WorkAgla →How Shopify Handled 30TB per Minute With a Modular Monolith Architecture
120System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

How Shopify Handled 30TB per Minute With a Modular Monolith Architecture

Shopify ne Black Friday jaise peak events par 30TB per minute traffic handle kiya — aur wo microservices par nahi, modular monolith par chalte hain. Ye case study batati hai ki monolith bhi scale kar sakta hai, agar use sahi tareeke se banaya jaaye.

Modular monolith ka matlab hai: ek hi codebase aur deployment, lekin andar se modules me bante hue — har module ki apni boundaries hain, apna database schema hai, aur modules ke beech communication clear interfaces se hoti hai. Shopify ne apne monolith ko modules me tod kar, har module ko independently scale aur deploy karne layak banaya.

Is approach ka fayda hai simplicity: distributed systems ki complexity (network calls, eventual consistency, distributed debugging) ke bina scale. Shopify ka lesson hai: microservices default jawab nahi hai — pehle monolith ko modular banao, aur sirf wahi hissa alag karo jo sach me alag scale ya team maangta hai.

  • Key numbers: 30TB per minute peak traffic, modular monolith par
  • Core pattern: modular monolith — ek deployment, lekin andar clear module boundaries + per-module schemas
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: pehle monolith ko modular banao — microservices tab jab koi module sach me alag scale ya alag team maange
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/modular-monolith
← PehleMicro FrontendsAgla →1 Simple Technique to Scale Microservices Architecture
121System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

1 Simple Technique to Scale Microservices Architecture

Microservices architecture ko scale karne ki ek simple technique hai: har service ko stateless banao aur state ko bahar rakho. Ye ek line lagti hai, lekin iska asar poore architecture par padta hai.

Jab service stateless hoti hai to use horizontally scale karna aasan hai — kitni bhi copies chalao, koi bhi request kisi bhi copy par ja sakti hai. State (sessions, carts, counters) ko Redis ya database me rakho — taaki service ki koi bhi copy crash ho to state safe rahe.

Iske saath do aur simple rules hain: service ke paas apna database ho (shared database se coupling banti hai), aur communication asynchronous ho jahan possible (events/queues se, taaki ek slow service dusri ko na roke). Ye teen rules follow karo to microservices scale hona shuru ho jaate hain.

  • Technique: stateless services + state bahar (Redis/DB) + apna database + async jahan possible
  • Key trade-off: stateless (scale aasan, lekin har request me state fetch) vs stateful (fast, lekin scale mushkil)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: state ko service se alag karo — yehi microservices scaling ka pehla aur sabse bada kadam hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/how-to-scale-microservices
← PehleHow Shopify Handled 30TB per Minute With a Modular Monolith ArchitectureAgla →How Message Queues Work
122System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

How Message Queues Work

Message queues do systems ke beech asynchronous communication ka tareeka hain — ek system message bhejta hai aur aage badh jaata hai, dusra system apne time par message process karta hai. Dono ko ek saath online rehne ki zaroorat nahi hoti.

Queue ka flow hai: producer message ko queue me daalta hai → broker (jaise RabbitMQ, SQS) use durably store karta hai → consumer message ko uthata hai aur process karta hai → phir acknowledge karta hai, tab message delete hota hai. Agar consumer crash ho jaaye to message wapas queue me aata hai aur kisi aur consumer ko milta hai.

Message queues ke fayde hain: decoupling (producer aur consumer ek dusre ko nahi jaante), buffering (traffic spike par queue bhar jaati hai, system crash nahi hota), aur reliability (message tab tak safe rehta hai jab tak process na ho). Nuksaan hai: eventual consistency aur ordering ki complexity — FIFO chahiye to alag configuration lagti hai.

  • Flow: producer → broker (durable store) → consumer → ack → delete; crash par redelivery
  • Key trade-off: decoupling + buffering (resilient) vs eventual consistency + ordering complexity
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: queue ka maksad hai "abhi nahi to baad me" — spike aur failure dono ko absorb karna
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/what-is-a-message-queue
← Pehle1 Simple Technique to Scale Microservices ArchitectureAgla →22 Microservices Design Patterns
123System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

22 Microservices Design Patterns

Microservices ke 22 design patterns — ye wo reusable solutions hain jo microservices architecture ki common problems ko solve karte hain. Patterns 5 groups me batte hain: communication, data, resilience, observability, aur deployment.

Communication patterns hain: API Gateway, Backend for Frontend (BFF), service registry/discovery, aur event-driven. Data patterns hain: database per service, Saga, CQRS, aur event sourcing. Resilience patterns hain: circuit breaker, bulkhead, retry with backoff, aur timeout.

Observability patterns hain: distributed tracing, centralized logging, aur health checks. Deployment patterns hain: blue-green, canary, aur sidecar. Interview me pattern ka naam batana kaafi nahi hai — ye bhi batana hota hai ki kis problem ke liye kaunsa pattern aur kyun.

  • 5 groups: communication → data → resilience → observability → deployment
  • Key patterns: API Gateway, Saga, CQRS, circuit breaker, bulkhead, distributed tracing, sidecar
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: pattern ka naam nahi, problem-pattern ka match yaad rakho — har pattern kisi specific dard ka ilaaj hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/microservices-design-patterns
← PehleHow Message Queues WorkAgla →How Nginx Was Able to Support 1 Million Concurrent Connections on a Single Server
124System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

How Nginx Was Able to Support 1 Million Concurrent Connections on a Single Server

Nginx ne ek hi server par 1 million concurrent connections support kiye — ye C10K problem ka jawab tha. 2000s ke shuru me har connection ke liye ek thread/process chahiye hota tha, aur 10,000 connections par hi servers fail ho jaate the. Nginx ne architecture hi badal di.

Nginx ka raaz hai event-driven, non-blocking architecture: ek worker process hazaron connections ko handle karta hai — har connection par thread nahi banta, balki events par kaam hota hai. Jab ek connection data ka wait karta hai to worker dusre connection ka kaam kar leta hai — koi blocking nahi.

Iske liye Nginx ne epoll (Linux) jaise efficient event notification mechanisms use kiye, aur memory footprint bahut chhota rakha — har connection sirf kuch KB memory leta hai. Isi liye Nginx aaj duniya ka sabse popular web server aur reverse proxy hai — C10K se C10M tak ka safar isi architecture ne tay kiya.

  • Key numbers: 1 million concurrent connections, ek server par; har connection ~kuch KB memory
  • Core pattern: event-driven + non-blocking I/O (epoll) — ek worker, hazaron connections, zero thread-per-connection
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: thread-per-connection scale nahi karta — event-driven hi C10K ka jawab hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/how-does-nginx-work
← Pehle22 Microservices Design PatternsAgla →Networking Concepts (Part 1–2)
125System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

Networking Concepts (Part 1–2)

Networking concepts — Part 1 aur 2 mil kar wo saari networking knowledge dete hain jo ek system design interview me chahiye: OSI model, TCP/IP, DNS, load balancing, aur protocols.

Part 1 me hain: OSI 7 layers (physical → data link → network → transport → session → presentation → application), TCP vs UDP (reliable vs fast), 3-way handshake, aur IP addressing/subnetting. Part 2 me hain: DNS resolution, HTTP/HTTPS, load balancing, CDN, aur WebSockets/SSE.

Networking ko samajhne ka sabse aasan tareeka hai layer model: har layer apne neeche wali layer ki service leti hai aur upar wali ko service deti hai. Jab koi request fail ho to layer-by-layer debug karo — pehle DNS, phir TCP, phir TLS, phir HTTP. Yehi approach production debugging me bhi kaam aati hai.

  • Part 1: OSI layers, TCP vs UDP, 3-way handshake, IP/subnetting
  • Part 2: DNS, HTTP/HTTPS, load balancing, CDN, WebSockets/SSE
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: layer-by-layer socho — har problem kisi ek layer ki hoti hai, aur wahi milta hai uska jawab

Dono parts ke links: Part 1 · Part 2

Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/networking-fundamentals-software-engineers
← PehleHow Nginx Was Able to Support 1 Million Concurrent Connections on a Single ServerAgla →Forward proxy vs Reverse proxy
126System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

Forward proxy vs Reverse proxy

Forward proxy aur reverse proxy dono beech me baith kar requests ko aage bhejte hain — farak hai kiski taraf se kaam karte hain. Forward proxy client ki taraf se kaam karta hai, reverse proxy server ki taraf se.

Forward proxy: client → proxy → internet. Iska use hota hai client ko chhupane (IP hide), content filtering (office/school me blocked sites), aur caching ke liye. Client ko pata hota hai ki proxy hai — wo use configure karta hai.

Reverse proxy: internet → proxy → servers. Iska use hota hai server ko chhupane (real servers ke IPs nahi dikhte), load balancing, SSL termination, aur caching ke liye. Client ko pata nahi hota ki peeche proxy hai — use lagta hai wo seedha server se baat kar raha hai. Nginx aur Cloudflare reverse proxy ke examples hain.

  • Forward proxy: client ki taraf se — IP hide, filtering, client-side caching
  • Reverse proxy: server ki taraf se — load balancing, SSL termination, server hide
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: "forward = client chhupta hai, reverse = server chhupta hai" — ye ek line interview me kaafi hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/forward-proxy-vs-reverse-proxy

127System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

10 System Design Patterns

System design ke 10 sabse important patterns — ye wo building blocks hain jinse lagbhag har system banta hai: load balancing, caching, sharding, replication, message queues, CDN, rate limiting, consistent hashing, CAP trade-offs, aur idempotency.

Har pattern ek problem ka jawab hai: load balancing traffic baantne ka, caching fast reads ka, sharding data baantne ka, replication availability ka, message queues decoupling ka, CDN global latency ka, rate limiting abuse rokne ka, consistent hashing minimal data movement ka, CAP consistency vs availability ka, aur idempotency safe retries ka.

Interview me in 10 patterns ko "toolbox" ki tarah use karo: pehle problem ko samjho, phir toolbox se sahi tool chuno, aur trade-off batao. Jo candidate har problem par ek hi pattern lagata hai (jaise har cheez me cache), wo fail hota hai — sahi tool, sahi problem hi asli skill hai.

  • 10 patterns: load balancing, caching, sharding, replication, queues, CDN, rate limiting, consistent hashing, CAP, idempotency
  • Key rule: pehle problem, phir pattern — pattern ko problem par thopna galat hai
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: ye 10 patterns tumhara toolbox hain — interview me inhe mix karke system banao
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/system-design-patterns
← PehleForward proxy vs Reverse proxyAgla →Quotient Filter
128System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

Quotient Filter

Quotient filter ek space-efficient probabilistic data structure hai — Bloom filter ka cousin, lekin isme kuch extra powers hain. Ye batata hai ki koi element set me hai ya nahi — Bloom filter ki tarah false positives possible hain, lekin false negatives kabhi nahi.

Quotient filter kaam karta hai hash ko do hisson me baant kar: quotient (bucket index) aur remainder (fingerprint). Har bucket me remainder store hota hai, aur jab bucket bhar jaaye to aage ke buckets use hote hain — isliye quotient filter mergeable hai (do filters ko ek me milaya ja sakta hai) aur deletions support karta hai — dono cheezein Bloom filter nahi kar sakta.

Iska use hota hai databases aur storage engines me — jaise LSM-tree based systems (RocksDB, Cassandra) me ye batane ke liye ki koi key disk par hai ya nahi, bina disk padhe. Bloom filter se iska comparison interview me ek accha "deep knowledge" signal hai.

  • Core idea: hash = quotient (bucket) + remainder (fingerprint); false positives possible, false negatives nahi
  • Bloom filter se behtar: deletions + merging possible
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: Bloom filter se aage badho to quotient filter — deletion aur merge hi iski pehchan hai
Source:https://systemdesign.one/quotient-filter-explained/
← Pehle10 System Design PatternsAgla →Redis Use Cases
129System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

Redis Use Cases

Redis ke use cases — Redis ek in-memory data structure store hai jo cache se kai zyada hai. Iske main use cases hain: caching, sessions, leaderboards, rate limiting, queues, aur pub/sub.

Caching: sabse common — database ke saamne cache layer. Sessions: user sessions store karna, taaki koi bhi server request handle kar sake. Leaderboards: sorted sets se real-time ranking (gaming, contests). Rate limiting: counters + TTL se request limits. Queues: lists se simple job queues. Pub/Sub: real-time notifications aur chat.

Redis ki data structures (strings, lists, sets, sorted sets, hashes, streams) hi iski taakat hain — har use case ke liye sahi structure hai. Lekin Redis in-memory hai — isliye ye primary database nahi hai (data loss ka risk), aur bade datasets ke liye mehenga padta hai. Cache ke liye best, source of truth ke liye nahi.

  • Top use cases: caching, sessions, leaderboards (sorted sets), rate limiting, queues, pub/sub
  • Key trade-off: in-memory speed (microseconds) vs data loss risk + cost — cache ke liye best, source of truth ke liye nahi
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: Redis ki data structures hi iski taakat hain — use case ke hisaab se structure chuno
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/redis-use-cases
← PehleQuotient FilterAgla →Rate Limiting Strategies Explained
130System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

Rate Limiting Strategies Explained

Rate limiting strategies — API ko abuse se bachane ke tareeke. Sabse common strategies hain: token bucket, leaky bucket, fixed window, sliding window log, aur sliding window counter.

Token bucket me bucket me tokens bharte rehte hain (fixed rate se) — har request ek token leti hai; bucket khaali ho to request reject. Isme burst allowed hai (bucket bhara ho to ek saath kai requests). Leaky bucket me requests ek fixed rate se bahar nikalti hain — burst smooth ho jaata hai.

Fixed window me har time window (jaise 1 minute) me N requests allowed — simple lekin window boundary par burst ho sakta hai (59th second par 100 + 61st second par 100). Sliding window is problem ko solve karta hai — wo pichhle window ka weighted count use karta hai. Production me aksar token bucket (Redis) + sliding window ka combination use hota hai.

  • Strategies: token bucket (burst OK) → leaky bucket (smooth) → fixed window (simple, boundary burst) → sliding window (accurate)
  • Key trade-off: accuracy (sliding window) vs simplicity + memory (fixed window/token bucket)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: token bucket burst deta hai, leaky bucket smooth karta hai — API ke nature se chuno
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/rate-limiting
← PehleRedis Use CasesAgla →How Remote Procedure Call Works
131System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

How Remote Procedure Call Works

RPC (Remote Procedure Call) ka matlab hai: doosri machine par function call karna, jaise wo local function ho. Developer getUser(id) likhta hai — aur RPC framework use network call me badal deta hai, result wapas laata hai.

RPC ka flow hai: client stub ko call karta hai → stub request ko serialize karta hai (jaise Protocol Buffers) → network se server tak bhejta hai → server deserialize karke actual function chalata hai → result wapas serialize karke bhejta hai → client stub use deserialize karke return karta hai.

Modern RPC hai gRPC (Google) — ye HTTP/2 par chalta hai, Protocol Buffers use karta hai, aur streaming support karta hai (server streaming, client streaming, bidirectional). REST se farak ye hai: RPC actions par focus karta hai (function calls), REST resources par (URLs). Microservices ke beech internal communication me gRPC bahut popular hai.

  • Flow: stub → serialize → network → deserialize → function → result wapas
  • Key trade-off: gRPC (fast, typed, streaming — lekin binary, browser-unfriendly) vs REST (simple, universal — lekin verbose)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: RPC "function call" jaisa lagta hai, lekin network hai — latency aur failure ko kabhi mat bhoolo
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/how-rpc-works
← PehleRate Limiting Strategies ExplainedAgla →Service Discovery
132System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

Service Discovery

Service discovery ka matlab hai: ek service dusri service ko kaise dhoondhe — jab services ke IPs har waqt badal rahe hon (containers, autoscaling). Hardcoded IPs microservices me kaam nahi karte.

Do tareeke hain: client-side discovery (client pehle registry se service ke instances poochhta hai, phir seedha call karta hai — Netflix Eureka iska example hai) aur server-side discovery (client load balancer ko call karta hai, load balancer registry se instance dhoondh kar request forward karta hai — AWS ELB iska example hai).

Registry (jaise Consul, etcd, Eureka) me har service apne aap ko register karti hai aur heartbeat bhejti rehti hai — heartbeat ruk jaaye to registry use dead maan kar hata deti hai. Isse system me dynamic scaling possible hota hai — naye instances aate hi discoverable ho jaate hain.

  • 2 patterns: client-side (client registry poochhta hai) vs server-side (load balancer dhoondhta hai)
  • Core mechanism: register + heartbeat — dead instances automatically hat jaate hain
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: hardcoded IPs microservices me nahi chalte — registry hi address book hai
Source:https://systemdesign.one/what-is-service-discovery/
← PehleHow Remote Procedure Call WorksAgla →How Sidecar Pattern Works
133System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

How Sidecar Pattern Works

Sidecar pattern me har application container ke saath ek helper container chalta hai — jaise bike ke saath sidecar. Main app business logic par focus karti hai, sidecar logging, monitoring, security, aur networking jaise cross-cutting concerns sambhalta hai.

Example: ek service ke saath Envoy proxy sidecar chalta hai — saara traffic sidecar se hokar jaata hai, aur sidecar load balancing, retries, TLS, aur metrics handle karta hai. App ko in sab ke baare me kuch nahi pata hota — wo sirf localhost par sidecar se baat karti hai.

Sidecar pattern service mesh (Istio, Linkerd) ki neev hai. Iska fayda hai: app code clean rehta hai, aur infrastructure features ko bina app badle update kiya ja sakta hai. Nuksaan hai: extra resource usage (har pod me ek aur container) aur operational complexity.

  • Core idea: main container (business logic) + sidecar container (logging, proxy, security) — ek pod me
  • Key trade-off: clean app code + uniform infra (lekin extra resources per pod)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: sidecar se app ko infra ki parwaah nahi karni padti — service mesh isi pattern par khada hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/sidecar-pattern
← PehleService DiscoveryAgla →Stacked Diffs, Simply Explained
134System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

Stacked Diffs, Simply Explained

Stacked diffs ek code review workflow hai jisme ek bade change ko chhote, dependent diffs ki stack me toda jaata hai — har diff pehle wale ke upar banta hai. Ek 1000-line PR ki jagah 5 chhote PRs (200 lines each) — har ek alag review hota hai.

Workflow hai: pehla diff banao (base feature) → review aur approve → uske upar dusra diff (next layer) → review → aur aise hi aage. Har diff chhota aur focused hota hai, isliye review fast aur accurate hota hai. Tools jaise Graphite aur Sapling is workflow ko manage karte hain — ek diff update ho to uske upar wale automatically rebase ho jaate hain.

Iska fayda hai faster reviews aur faster shipping — chhote diffs jaldi review hote hain, aur ek diff merge ho to baaki aage badhte hain. Nuksaan hai complexity — stack manage karna seekhna padta hai, aur ek diff me badlaav ho to upar wale sab update karne padte hain. Badi tech companies (Meta, Google) is workflow ko use karti hain.

  • Workflow: bade change → chhote dependent diffs ki stack → har diff alag review → ek-ek karke merge
  • Key trade-off: fast reviews (chhote diffs) vs stack management complexity
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: review ki speed diff ke size se tay hoti hai — stack karke bada kaam bhi chhota dikhao
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/stacked-diffs
← PehleHow Sidecar Pattern WorksAgla →Stock Exchange System Design (Part 1–3)
135System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

Stock Exchange System Design (Part 1–3)

Stock exchange system design — duniya ke sabse demanding systems me se ek, jahan microseconds matter karte hain. Part 1–3 mil kar exchange ka poora design samjhate hain: order matching, market data, aur low latency.

Part 1 me hai order book: buy aur sell orders ko price-time priority se match karna — sabse acchi price pehle, phir sabse purana order. Matching engine exchange ka dil hai — ye deterministic aur bahut fast hona chahiye (microseconds me match).

Part 2 me hai market data dissemination: har trade aur order book change ko hazaron subscribers tak milliseconds me pahunchana — multicast aur feed handlers se. Part 3 me hai low latency techniques: kernel bypass, FPGA, colocation (exchange ke paas server rakhna), aur single-threaded matching engine. Interview me ye case study "extreme performance" ka best example hai.

  • Part 1: order book + matching engine (price-time priority, microseconds)
  • Part 2: market data (multicast, feed handlers)
  • Part 3: low latency (kernel bypass, FPGA, colocation)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: exchange me latency hi paisa hai — har microsecond ki keemat hoti hai

Teeno parts ke links: Part 1 · Part 2 · Part 3

Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/stock-exchange-system-design

136System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

Scalability Patterns - Part 1

Scalability patterns — wo patterns jo system ko badhte load ke saath scale karne dete hain. Part 1 ke core patterns hain: horizontal scaling, load balancing, caching, database sharding, aur async processing.

Horizontal scaling (aur servers jodna) vertical scaling (bada server) se behtar hai — kyunki iski koi ceiling nahi hoti. Load balancing traffic ko servers me baant ta hai. Caching database ka load kam karta hai. Sharding data ko baant ta hai. Async processing (queues) heavy kaam ko background me le jaata hai.

In patterns ko ek saath use karna hi scalability hai — koi ek pattern kaafi nahi hota. Aur har pattern ke saath ek trade-off aata hai: caching se staleness, sharding se cross-shard queries mushkil, async se eventual consistency. Interview me pattern ka naam nahi, kab kaunsa pattern aur kyun — ye batana hota hai.

  • Part 1 patterns: horizontal scaling → load balancing → caching → sharding → async processing
  • Key principle: scale karna matlab bottleneck ko hatana — pehle bottleneck dhoondho, phir pattern lagao
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: har scalability pattern ke saath ek trade-off aata hai — free lunch nahi hota
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/scalability-patterns-system-design

137System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

50 System Design Tradeoffs

System design ke 50 tradeoffs — ye wo decisions hain jo har design me lene padte hain, aur jin ka koi ek sahi jawab nahi hota. Interview me in tradeoffs ko samajhna hi asli skill hai.

Sabse important tradeoffs hain: consistency vs availability (CAP), latency vs throughput, strong vs eventual consistency, SQL vs NoSQL, sync vs async, cache vs database, monolith vs microservices, push vs pull, read vs write optimization, aur cost vs performance.

Har tradeoff ka jawab hai: "it depends" — requirements par. Consistency chahiye ya availability? Ye use case batata hai — payments me consistency, social feed me availability. Interview me jab bhi koi decision lo to tradeoff ka naam lo aur kyun ye side chuni — ye hi senior engineer ki pehchan hai.

  • Top tradeoffs: consistency vs availability, latency vs throughput, SQL vs NoSQL, sync vs async, cost vs performance
  • Key rule: har decision ka jawab "it depends" se shuru hota hai — requirements batate hain kis side jana hai
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: tradeoff ka naam lena aur reason dena hi interview me marks dilata hai — "best" kuch nahi hota, "sahi" hota hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/system-design-tradeoffs
← PehleScalability Patterns - Part 1Agla →How Terraform Works
138System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

How Terraform Works

Terraform infrastructure ko code se manage karne ka tool hai (Infrastructure as Code) — servers, databases, networks, sab kuch ek config file me likha jaata hai, aur Terraform use real infrastructure me badal deta hai.

Terraform ka workflow hai: write → plan → apply. Write me HCL language me desired state likhte ho (jaise "mujhe 3 EC2 instances chahiye"). Plan me Terraform batata hai kya badlega (kya banega, kya badlega, kya delete hoga) — bina kuch badle. Apply me wo changes execute hote hain. Terraform state file me track karta hai ki actual infrastructure kya hai, taaki agli baar sirf diff apply ho.

Terraform ka fayda hai reproducibility aur versioning — infrastructure Git me hai, review hoti hai, aur kisi bhi environment ko dobara banaya ja sakta hai. Cloud-agnostic bhi hai — AWS, GCP, Azure sab par chalta hai. Nuksaan hai: state file manage karni padti hai (remote backend + locking zaroori hai), aur bade setups me plan slow ho sakta hai.

  • Workflow: write (HCL) → plan (preview) → apply (execute) — state file tracks reality
  • Key trade-off: reproducibility + versioning (lekin state file management + learning curve)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: infrastructure bhi code hai — plan bina apply ke, aur state bina lock ke kabhi mat karo
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/how-terraform-works-internally
← Pehle50 System Design TradeoffsAgla →How Do Websockets Work
139System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

How Do Websockets Work

WebSockets ek aisa protocol hai jo browser aur server ke beech persistent, two-way connection banata hai — dono taraf se kabhi bhi message bhej sakte hain. HTTP request-response se alag, WebSocket connection ek baar banta hai aur khula rehta hai.

Flow hai: client HTTP request bhejta hai jisme Upgrade: websocket header hota hai → server agree karta hai (101 Switching Protocols) → connection WebSocket me badal jaata hai → uske baad dono taraf se frames (chhote messages) aate-jaate rehte hain — bina har baar naya connection banaye.

WebSockets ka use hota hai chat apps, live notifications, collaborative editing, aur real-time dashboards me — jahan server ko client ko turant update bhejna hota hai. HTTP polling se farak ye hai: polling me client baar-baar poochhta hai ("kuch naya hai?"), WebSocket me server khud bhejta hai jab kuch ho. Nuksaan hai: connection state — server ko har connection yaad rakhna padta hai, isliye scale karna HTTP se mushkil hai.

  • Flow: HTTP upgrade (101) → persistent connection → two-way frames
  • Key trade-off: real-time push (lekin stateful connections — scale mushkil) vs polling (stateless — lekin wasteful)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: jab server ko client ko turant batana ho to WebSocket — jab client khud poochh sakta hai to HTTP hi kaafi hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/how-do-websockets-work
← PehleHow Terraform WorksAgla →How Do Webhooks Work
140System Design Fundamentals — Core Technologies

How Do Webhooks Work

Webhooks reverse API hain — aap server ko call nahi karte, server aapko call karta hai jab kuch ho jaaye. "Don't call us, we'll call you" — yehi webhook ka idea hai.

Flow hai: aap webhook URL register karte ho (jaise "payment successful hone par is URL par POST karo") → event hota hai → server aapke URL par HTTP POST bhejta hai event data ke saath → aapka server use process karta hai. Stripe, GitHub, aur Slack — sab webhooks use karte hain.

Webhooks me dhyaan dene wali cheezein hain: signature verification (request sach me usi server se aayi hai — secret se HMAC verify karo), idempotency (ek hi event do baar aa sakta hai — retry par double kaam mat karo), aur retries (aapka server down ho to sender dobara try karta hai). Polling se farak ye hai: polling me aap baar-baar poochhte ho, webhook me event aate hi pata chal jaata hai.

  • Flow: register URL → event → HTTP POST to your URL → verify signature → process (idempotently)
  • Key trade-off: real-time + efficient (lekin aapko public URL + verification chahiye) vs polling (simple — lekin wasteful)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: webhook me hamesha signature verify karo aur idempotency rakho — nahi to fake events aur double processing dono honge
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/how-do-webhooks-work
← PehleHow Do Websockets WorkAgla →System Design Interview Framework
141System Design Interview — Framework & Design Questions

System Design Interview Framework

System design interview ka framework ek fixed structure hai jisse koi bhi design question solve kiya ja sakta hai. Interview 45–60 minute ka hota hai aur usme ek bada system design karna hota hai — jaise "Design Twitter" ya "Design a URL shortener". Framework ke 5 steps hain.

Step 1 — Requirements: pehle functional aur non-functional requirements poochho (kitne users? kitne requests/sec? read-heavy ya write-heavy?). Step 2 — Back-of-the-envelope: scale estimate karo (QPS, storage, bandwidth). Step 3 — High-level design: API, data model, aur main components ka diagram banao.

Step 4 — Deep dive: ek-do components ko detail me design karo (database schema, caching, sharding). Step 5 — Bottlenecks: single points of failure aur scaling issues batao, aur unka solution do. Interviewer tumhare final design se zyada tumhari sochne ki process dekhta hai — har decision ka reason batao.

  • 5 steps: requirements → estimates → high-level design → deep dive → bottlenecks
  • Key rule: pehle 5 minute requirements par — bina requirements ke design karna sabse badi galti hai
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: interviewer design nahi, tumhari decision-making dekhta hai — har choice ka trade-off batao
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/how-to-prepare-for-system-design-interview
← PehleHow Do Webhooks WorkAgla →Behavioral Interview Playbook
142System Design Interview — Framework & Design Questions

Behavioral Interview Playbook

Behavioral interview me tumhare past experience ke baare me poochha jaata hai — "Ek baar jab tumne ek mushkil problem solve ki" jaise sawaal. Iska maksad hai ye jaanna ki tum team me kaise kaam karte ho, conflict kaise handle karte ho, aur pressure me kya karte ho.

Har behavioral answer ka structure hai STAR: Situation (kya hua tha), Task (tumhari zimmedari kya thi), Action (tumne kya kiya), Result (kya hua — numbers ke saath). "Humne" ki jagah "Maine" bolo — interviewer tumhare contribution ko jaanna chahta hai, team ka nahi.

Taiyaari ka tareeka hai: 6–8 stories taiyaar rakho — ek tough technical problem, ek conflict, ek failure, ek leadership moment, ek tight deadline, aur ek bada impact. Har story ko STAR me likh kar practice karo. Behavioral round me sach aur specifics matter karte hain — vague answers fail hote hain.

  • STAR structure: Situation → Task → Action → Result (numbers ke saath)
  • Key rule: "Maine" bolo, "Humne" nahi — apna contribution saaf dikhao
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: 6–8 taiyaar stories har behavioral sawaal ka jawab de sakti hain — pehle se practice karo
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/common-behavioral-interview-questions
← PehleSystem Design Interview FrameworkAgla →Mobile System Design Interview Framework
143System Design Interview — Framework & Design Questions

Mobile System Design Interview Framework

Mobile system design interview me ek mobile app ka system design karna hota hai — jaise "Design a ride-sharing app" ya "Design Instagram for mobile". Backend design ke saath-saath yahan mobile-specific constraints bhi aate hain: battery, network, storage, aur offline support.

Framework backend jaisa hi hai — requirements, estimates, high-level design, deep dive — lekin 3 mobile cheezein extra hain. Pehli: offline support — app bina network ke bhi kaam kare (local database + sync). Dusri: push notifications — server se client tak real-time updates. Teesri: battery aur bandwidth — background sync kam karo, payload chhota rakho.

Mobile design me client-server sync sabse mushkil hissa hai: offline changes ko server se merge karna, conflicts resolve karna, aur data ko efficiently sync karna (delta sync — sirf badla hua data). Interview me ye dikhana hota hai ki tum mobile ki limitations ko samajhte ho, sirf backend nahi.

  • Mobile extras: offline support (local DB + sync) → push notifications → battery/bandwidth optimization
  • Key trade-off: rich offline experience (complex sync) vs simple online-only (lekin network par dependent)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: mobile me network kabhi reliable nahi hota — offline-first socho
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/mobile-system-design-interview

144System Design Interview — Framework & Design Questions

System Design Interview: Design Airbnb

Airbnb design interview ka classic question hai — ek aisi system jahan hosts listings daalte hain aur guests unhe search karke book karte hain. Iska sabse mushkil hissa hai search aur booking ka consistency.

Requirements: guests location, dates, guests count aur price se search karte hain; hosts listings manage karte hain; booking par payment hota hai; reviews aur ratings hote hain. Scale: 150 million+ users, 7 million+ listings — search read-heavy hai, booking write-heavy aur critical hai.

High-level design: search service (Elasticsearch — geo + filters + ranking), listing service, booking service (availability calendar + double-booking rokna), payment service, notification service. Sabse mushkil hissa hai availability aur double-booking: ek hi dates par do guests book na kar paayein — iske liye booking par pessimistic lock ya atomic check-and-book chahiye. Search ke liye listings ko Elasticsearch me index karo — geo queries aur filters fast ho jaate hain.

  • Scale: 150M+ users, 7M+ listings — search read-heavy, booking write-critical
  • Hardest part: double-booking rokna — atomic check-and-book / pessimistic lock
  • Key components: search (Elasticsearch) → listings → bookings (calendar) → payments → notifications
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: Airbnb me search aur booking do alag problems hain — search eventual consistency se chalti hai, booking strong consistency maangti hai

145System Design Interview — Framework & Design Questions

System Design Interview: Design ChatGPT

ChatGPT jaisa system design karna interview me ek naya aur popular question hai — ye ek LLM-powered chat application hai jahan users prompts bhejte hain aur AI streaming responses deta hai. Iska sabse alag hissa hai streaming aur inference.

Requirements: user prompt bhejta hai, response token-by-token stream hoti hai (jaise typing hoti dikhe), conversation history save hoti hai, aur lakhon users ek saath chat karte hain. Scale: 100 million+ users, har request par LLM inference — jo CPU nahi, GPU par chalta hai aur seconds lagta hai.

High-level design: API gateway → chat service (conversation history, context management) → inference service (GPU clusters, model serving, batching) → response streaming (Server-Sent Events ya WebSockets). Sabse mushkil hissa hai GPU inference scaling: requests ko batch karo (continuous batching), KV cache use karo, aur context window ka khayal rakho — lambi conversation ka context har request me bhejna padta hai, jo cost badhata hai. Rate limiting aur queueing zaroori hai kyunki GPU capacity limited hoti hai.

  • Scale: 100M+ users, har request GPU inference — seconds per response
  • Hardest part: GPU inference scaling — continuous batching, KV cache, context window cost
  • Key components: chat service (history/context) → inference service (GPU) → streaming (SSE/WebSocket) → rate limiting
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: ChatGPT me bottleneck compute hai, data nahi — GPU capacity hi scaling ki hadd hai

146System Design Interview — Framework & Design Questions

Live Streaming System Design

Live streaming system design — jaise YouTube Live ya Twitch — me ek broadcaster ka video lakhon viewers tak real-time me pahunchana hota hai, kam se kam delay ke saath. Iska sabse mushkil hissa hai ingest, transcode, aur distribute — teeno real-time me.

Flow hai: broadcaster RTMP se video ingest server ko bhejta hai → transcoding me video ko kai qualities me badla jaata hai (1080p, 720p, 480p — adaptive bitrate) → CDN se viewers tak distribute hota hai (HLS/DASH segments). Chat ke liye alag WebSocket + pub-sub system hota hai.

Latency ke levels hain: ultra-low latency (1–3 seconds, WebRTC — interactive streams ke liye) vs standard latency (10–30 seconds, HLS — zyada scale ke liye). Scale: ek popular stream par millions of concurrent viewers — isliye CDN aur edge caching hi iska jawab hai. Transcoding sabse mehenga hissa hai — GPU ya dedicated hardware chahiye.

  • Pipeline: ingest (RTMP) → transcode (multi-bitrate) → distribute (CDN, HLS/DASH) + chat (WebSocket)
  • Key trade-off: low latency (WebRTC, 1–3s, kam scale) vs high scale (HLS, 10–30s, millions of viewers)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: live streaming me sabse mehenga hissa transcoding hai — aur sabse mushkil hissa latency vs scale ka balance

147System Design Interview — Framework & Design Questions

Microservices Interview Questions

Microservices interview questions — ye wo sawaal hain jo microservices architecture par interviews me poochhe jaate hain. Inke jawab me pattern ka naam nahi, problem aur tradeoff batana hota hai.

Top questions hain: Monolith vs microservices — kab todna chahiye? (Jawab: jab team badi ho, deploy slow ho, ya scale alag ho.) Service boundaries kaise tay karein? (Domain-Driven Design — bounded contexts.) Distributed transactions kaise handle karein? (Saga pattern.) Services ke beech communication? (Sync REST/gRPC vs async events.)

Aur questions hain: Service discovery kaise hoti hai? (Registry + heartbeat.) Failure kaise handle karein? (Circuit breaker, bulkhead, retry with backoff.) Data consistency? (Eventual consistency, CQRS.) Testing aur deployment? (Contract testing, canary.) Har jawab me pehle problem, phir pattern, phir tradeoff — yehi structure marks dilata hai.

  • Top questions: monolith vs microservices → boundaries (DDD) → distributed transactions (Saga) → communication → failure handling
  • Key rule: har jawab me problem → pattern → tradeoff — naam ratna kaafi nahi hai
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: microservices ke sawaalon me "kab nahi karna" bhi utna hi important hai jitna "kab karna"
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/microservices-interview-questions

Software Engineer Resume

Software engineer ka resume — ye section batata hai ki ek aisa resume kaise banayein jo recruiter ke 6-second scan me bhi pasand aaye. Resume ka maksad interview dilana hai, poori kahani batana nahi.

Structure hai: pehle impact — har bullet me "kya kiya + kya result aaya (numbers ke saath)". "Worked on payment system" ki jagah "Payment system banaya jo 1M daily transactions handle karta hai, latency 200ms se 40ms ki". Numbers hi resume ki jaan hain — scale, latency, cost saving, users — kuch to number do.

Do aur rules: ek page (2 saal se kam experience ho to), aur skills jo job se match karein — har skill likhna zaroori nahi hai. Projects me wo projects dikhao jo system design dikhate hon — "built a URL shortener handling 10K req/sec" recruiter ko system design interview ke liye taiyaar candidate dikhata hai.

  • Resume rule: har bullet = kya kiya + result (numbers ke saath)
  • Key rule: ek page, relevant skills, impact-first bullets — duties nahi, achievements likho
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: recruiter 6 second me decide karta hai — pehli 3 lines me hi impact dikhao

System Design Interview: Design Spotify

Spotify design interview ka popular question hai — ek music streaming system jahan users gaane search karte hain, playlists banate hain, aur music stream karte hain. Iska sabse mushkil hissa hai audio delivery aur recommendations.

Requirements: 600 million+ users, 100 million+ tracks — music files bade hote hain (3–5MB per song), aur users ko turant play hona chahiye. Search, playlists, offline download, aur personalized recommendations — ye sab core features hain.

High-level design: audio storage (object storage — S3 jaisa, files ko chunks me), CDN (audio files edge se serve — sabse important, kyunki bandwidth hi cost hai), metadata service (tracks, albums, artists), playlist service, recommendation service (collaborative filtering + ML models, offline compute + online serving). Sabse mushkil hissa hai recommendations — Discover Weekly jaise features ke liye user behavior data ko process karke personalized playlists banana. Audio ko adaptive bitrate me stream karo — network slow ho to quality kam, fast ho to zyada.

  • Scale: 600M+ users, 100M+ tracks — audio files bade, bandwidth hi cost hai
  • Hardest part: recommendations (personalized playlists) + CDN-based audio delivery
  • Key components: audio storage (object store) → CDN → metadata → playlists → recommendations (offline ML + online serving)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: Spotify me audio files CDN se aate hain, metadata database se — dono ka scale alag hai

150System Design Interview — Framework & Design Questions

System Design Interview: Design Web Crawler and Search Engine

Web crawler aur search engine design — Google ka core system, aur interview ka sabse classic question. Isme do bade hisse hain: crawl karna (web ko scan karna) aur search karna (query ka jawab dena).

Crawler ka flow hai: seed URLs → URL frontier (priority queue — kaunsi URL pehle crawl karni hai) → fetcher (page download karo, politeness — ek site ko zyada load mat do, robots.txt respect karo) → parser (links nikalo, content nikalo) → dedup (same page dobara mat crawl karo — content hash se) → indexer (inverted index banao).

Search ka flow hai: query aati hai → inverted index se matching pages nikalo → ranking (relevance + authority — PageRank jaisa) → results dikhao. Scale: billions of pages, petabytes of data — isliye crawl aur index dono distributed hote hain (MapReduce jaisi processing). Sabse mushkil hissa hai freshness vs coverage — popular pages baar-baar crawl karo, baaki kabhi-kabhi.

  • Crawl pipeline: frontier → fetcher (politeness) → parser → dedup → indexer (inverted index)
  • Search pipeline: query → inverted index → ranking (relevance + authority) → results
  • Key trade-off: freshness (popular pages dobara) vs coverage (naye pages) — crawl budget hi hadd hai
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: crawler ka sabse bada dushman duplicate content hai — dedup ke bina storage khatam ho jaayega
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/web-crawler-system-design

151System Design Interview — Framework & Design Questions

System Design Interview: Design Amazon S3

Amazon S3 design — ek object storage system jo 100s of trillions of objects store karta hai, 11 nines (99.999999999%) durability ke saath. Interview me ye question durability aur scale ka test hai.

Requirements: objects (files) store karo — koi bhi size (KB se TB), unlimited scale, 11 nines durability (saal me ek object khone ki probability 1 in 100 billion), aur high availability. API simple hai: PUT, GET, DELETE, LIST — lekin andar ka system bahut complex hai.

High-level design: object ko chunks me todo → har chunk ko erasure coding se kai pieces me baanto (jaise 10 data + 4 parity — koi 4 pieces kho jaayein to bhi data recover) → pieces ko alag AZs aur racks me rakho → metadata index (object key → chunk locations) alag se maintain karo. Durability ka raaz hai replication + erasure coding + geographic distribution — ek data center khatam bhi ho jaaye to data safe rahe. Consistency: S3 ab strong read-after-write deta hai — PUT ke baad GET wahi data deta hai.

  • Scale: 100s of trillions of objects, 11 nines durability (99.999999999%)
  • Core pattern: chunking + erasure coding (10+4) + cross-AZ distribution + metadata index
  • Key trade-off: durability (zyada copies = zyada cost) vs cost — erasure coding replication se sasta hai
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: S3 me durability ka raaz copies nahi, erasure coding + distribution hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/aws-s3-system-design

Design Twitter/X Timeline - A Frontend Deep Dive

Twitter/X timeline ka frontend deep dive — ye section backend timeline ki jagah frontend par focus karta hai: timeline UI kaise banayi jaaye jo fast, smooth, aur real-time ho.

Frontend challenges hain: infinite scroll (hazaaron tweets, lekin DOM me sirf visible wale — virtualization), real-time updates (naye tweets upar aate hain — WebSocket ya polling se), media loading (images/videos lazy load karo, pehle low-res placeholder), aur interactions (like, retweet — optimistic UI: pehle UI update karo, phir server ko batao).

Architecture: component tree (Feed → Tweet → actions), state management (timeline state, cache), API layer (paginated fetch — cursor-based), aur performance (virtualized list, image optimization, code-splitting). Sabse mushkil hissa hai scroll position maintain karna jab upar naye tweets aayein — user ki jagah na badle.

  • Frontend challenges: virtualization (infinite scroll) → real-time updates → lazy media → optimistic UI
  • Key trade-off: real-time freshness (naye tweets turant) vs stable scroll (user ki jagah na badle)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: timeline frontend me DOM size hi bottleneck hai — virtualization ke bina 1000 tweets par page ruk jaayega

153System Design Interview — Framework & Design Questions

Design WhatsApp (Part 1–2)

WhatsApp design — duniya ka sabse bada messaging system, 2 billion+ users, 100 billion+ messages per day. Interview ka classic question, aur iska sabse mushkil hissa hai real-time delivery aur end-to-end encryption.

Part 1 me hai high-level design: WebSocket connections (har user ka ek persistent connection), message routing (sender → server → receiver ka connection dhoondho), message storage (messages tab tak store jab tak deliver na hon), aur presence (online/last seen). Fan-out group chats me hota hai — ek message kai receivers tak.

Part 2 me hai deep dive: end-to-end encryption (Signal protocol — server bhi message padh nahi sakta), media handling (images/videos object storage + CDN + thumbnails), delivery receipts (sent → delivered → read — ticks), aur offline messages (user offline ho to message queue me rakho, online aate hi deliver karo). Scale ka raaz hai connection management — 2 billion connections ko handle karna hi sabse badi engineering hai (Erlang jaise languages iske liye bane hain).

  • Scale: 2B+ users, 100B+ messages/day — real-time + encrypted
  • Part 1: WebSocket connections → message routing → storage → presence → group fan-out
  • Part 2: E2E encryption (Signal) → media (object store + CDN) → receipts → offline queue
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: WhatsApp me sabse mushkil hissa message nahi, connection hai — 2 billion persistent connections

Dono parts ke links: Part 1 · Part 2

Design YouTube

YouTube design — duniya ka sabse bada video platform, 2 billion+ users, 500+ hours of video har minute upload. Interview ka classic question, aur iska sabse mushkil hissa hai video storage, transcoding, aur delivery.

Requirements: users video upload karte hain, search karte hain, stream karte hain, comment aur like karte hain. Scale: 500 hours/minute upload, billions of views per day — video files bade hote hain, isliye storage aur bandwidth hi cost hai.

High-level design: upload service (resumable uploads — badi file chunks me) → transcoding pipeline (har video ko kai resolutions me — 144p se 4K, adaptive bitrate) → storage (object storage, hot vs cold — popular videos fast storage me, purane cold me) → CDN (videos edge se serve — sabse important) → metadata + search (titles, descriptions, tags — Elasticsearch) → recommendations (home feed, up next — ML models). Comments aur likes alag services hain. Sabse mehenga hissa transcoding hai, aur sabse mushkil hissa recommendations — user ko agla video dikhana hi YouTube ki jaan hai.

  • Scale: 500+ hours/minute upload, billions of views/day — video = storage + bandwidth cost
  • Pipeline: upload (resumable) → transcode (multi-resolution) → storage (hot/cold) → CDN → metadata/search → recommendations
  • Key trade-off: transcoding cost (har resolution mehenga) vs streaming quality (adaptive bitrate)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: YouTube me video CDN se aata hai, recommendations ML se — dono alag scale ki problems hain

155AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

How AI Agents Work

AI agents wo systems hain jo khud soch kar, plan bana kar, aur tools use karke kaam karte hain — sirf ek prompt ka jawab dene ki jagah, wo ek goal ko poora karne ke liye kai steps lete hain. Agent ka loop hai: perceive → reason → act → observe.

Agent ke components hain: LLM (brain) — reasoning aur planning karta hai; tools — search, code execution, APIs, databases — jinse agent duniya se interact karta hai; memory — past conversations aur facts yaad rakhta hai; aur planning — bade task ko chhote steps me todna (jaise ReAct: reason + act, baari-baari).

Example: "Mujhe kal ki flight book karo" — agent pehle flight search tool use karta hai, phir options compare karta hai, phir booking tool se book karta hai, aur har step ka result dekh kar agla step tay karta hai. Agent ki sabse badi mushkil hai reliability — ek galat step poora kaam bigaad sakta hai, isliye guardrails aur human-in-the-loop zaroori hain.

  • Agent loop: perceive → reason (plan) → act (tool) → observe → repeat
  • Components: LLM (brain) + tools + memory + planning (ReAct)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: agent = LLM + tools + loop — bina tools ke LLM sirf chatbot hai, agent nahi
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/ai-agents-explained
← PehleDesign YouTubeAgla →How to design your own AI agent
156AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

How to design your own AI agent

Apna AI agent design karna — iska matlab hai ek aisa system banana jo ek specific goal ko autonomously poora kare. Design ke 5 steps hain: goal define karo, tools chuno, memory design karo, planning strategy tay karo, aur guardrails lagao.

Step 1 — Goal: agent kya karega? "Research karo" bahut vague hai — "is topic par 5 sources se summary banao" specific hai. Step 2 — Tools: agent ko kya tools chahiye? Search, file read, code run, API calls — har tool ka clear description do, taaki LLM sahi tool chune.

Step 3 — Memory: short-term (conversation) aur long-term (facts, preferences) — kya yaad rakhna hai? Step 4 — Planning: simple tasks ke liye ReAct (ek step, phir socho), complex ke liye plan-then-execute (pehle poora plan, phir execute). Step 5 — Guardrails: agent kya nahi kar sakta — cost limits, destructive actions par confirmation, aur max steps.

  • 5 steps: goal (specific) → tools (clear descriptions) → memory → planning (ReAct vs plan-then-execute) → guardrails
  • Key trade-off: zyada autonomy (fast, lekin risky) vs zyada control (safe, lekin slow)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: agent design me sabse important hai goal ko specific banana — vague goal = vague agent
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/how-do-ai-agents-work

157AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

11 AI Concepts Explained, Simply

AI ke 11 core concepts, simply explained — ye wo terms hain jo har AI discussion me aate hain. Pehle 4: LLM, token, context window, aur embeddings. LLM (Large Language Model) ek aisa model hai jo text ko samajh kar text generate karta hai. Token text ka chhota unit hai (lagbhag 4 characters = 1 token). Context window wo limit hai jitna text model ek baar me dekh sakta hai. Embeddings text ko numbers (vectors) me badalne ka tareeka hai — similar meaning wale text ke vectors paas hote hain.

Agle 4: fine-tuning, RAG, prompt engineering, aur hallucination. Fine-tuning model ko specific task ke liye aur train karna hai. RAG (Retrieval-Augmented Generation) model ko external data de kar jawab dilwana hai. Prompt engineering model se behtar jawab nikalne ka tareeka hai. Hallucination jab model confident hokar galat jawab deta hai.

Aakhri 3: agents, inference, aur training. Agents wo systems hain jo tools use karke kaam karte hain. Inference model ko chalana (jawab nikalna) hai, training model ko sikhana hai. Ye 11 concepts AI engineering ki neev hain — inhe samajh lo to baaki sab aasan lagta hai.

  • Core 4: LLM, token, context window, embeddings
  • Technique 4: fine-tuning, RAG, prompt engineering, hallucination
  • System 3: agents, inference, training
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: token aur context window hi LLM ki cost aur limit tay karte hain
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/ai-concepts

158AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

Agentic AI Use Cases in Software Development Workflow

Agentic AI software development workflow me kaise use hoti hai — ye section batata hai ki AI agents developer ke kaam ke har stage me kya kar sakte hain: planning se lekar deployment tak.

Planning: agent requirements ko padh kar tasks break kar sakta hai, aur estimates de sakta hai. Coding: agent code likh sakta hai, tests likh sakta hai, aur PRs review kar sakta hai. Debugging: agent error logs padh kar root cause dhoondh sakta hai aur fix suggest kar sakta hai.

Testing aur deployment: agent test cases generate kar sakta hai, CI failures ko analyze kar sakta hai, aur deployment ko monitor kar sakta hai. Lekin har stage me human review zaroori hai — agent code likh sakta hai, lekin architecture decisions aur security review insaan ka kaam hai. Best workflow hai agent drafts, human reviews.

  • Use cases: planning (task breakdown) → coding (code + tests) → debugging (root cause) → testing/deployment (CI analysis)
  • Key trade-off: agent speed (fast, lekin mistakes) vs human review (slow, lekin reliable)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: agent ko draft karne do, review tum karo — yehi aaj ka best workflow hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/agentic-ai-use-cases

159AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

30 Agentic Engineering Concepts, Explained Simply

Agentic engineering ke 30 concepts — ye wo terms hain jo AI agents banate waqt aate hain. Inhe 5 groups me baanta ja sakta hai: agent basics, tools, memory, planning, aur evaluation.

Agent basics: agent, autonomy, loop, goal, task decomposition. Tools: function calling, tool use, MCP (Model Context Protocol), API integration. Memory: short-term, long-term, episodic, semantic, vector memory. Planning: ReAct, plan-then-execute, reflection, self-correction, multi-step reasoning.

Evaluation: evals, guardrails, hallucination detection, human-in-the-loop, cost tracking. In 30 concepts ko samajhna hi agentic engineering hai — har concept ek problem ka naam hai, aur har problem ka solution ek pattern hai. Interview ya real work me jab agent banayein to in concepts ko checklist ki tarah use karo.

  • 5 groups: agent basics → tools → memory → planning → evaluation
  • Key concepts: ReAct, function calling, MCP, vector memory, reflection, evals, guardrails
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: agent engineering = LLM + tools + memory + planning + evals — ek bhi missing ho to agent unreliable hoga
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/agentic-engineering
← PehleAgentic AI Use Cases in Software Development WorkflowAgla →How Agent to Agent Protocol Works
160AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

How Agent to Agent Protocol Works

Agent to Agent (A2A) protocol wo standard hai jisse ek AI agent dusre AI agent se baat kar sakta hai — bina dono ko ek hi system ka hissa bane. Jaise HTTP ne websites ko connect kiya, A2A agents ko connect karta hai.

Protocol ka flow hai: har agent apni Agent Card publish karta hai — isme likha hota hai wo kya kar sakta hai, kaunse tools use karta hai, aur kaise contact karein. Dusra agent Agent Card padh kar use task bhejta hai — task me goal, context, aur expected output hota hai. Dono agents messages exchange karte hain jab tak task poora na ho.

A2A ka fayda hai interoperability: alag companies ke agents ek dusre ke saath kaam kar sakte hain — jaise ek travel agent ek booking agent se baat karke poori trip plan kar de. MCP (Model Context Protocol) se farak ye hai: MCP agent ko tools se connect karta hai, A2A agent ko agents se connect karta hai.

  • Core: Agent Card (capabilities) → task exchange → messages → result
  • MCP vs A2A: MCP = agent ↔ tools; A2A = agent ↔ agents
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: A2A ka maksad hai agents ka internet — alag agents ka ek dusre se baat karna
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/agent-to-agent-protocol

162AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

AI Coding Workflow

AI coding workflow — AI tools ke saath code likhne ka sahi tareeka. Ye workflow 4 stages ka hai: plan, prompt, review, aur iterate. Jo developer seedha "code likh do" bolta hai, wo galat workflow use kar raha hai.

Plan: pehle khud socho — kya banana hai, kaunse files badlenge, kaunsa approach hoga. AI ko plan do, sirf goal nahi. Prompt: specific aur context-rich prompt do — relevant files, constraints, aur examples ke saath. Review: AI ka code padho — har line samjho, tests chalao, edge cases check karo. Iterate: galti mile to AI ko error dikha kar fix karwao — dobara seedha prompt mat do.

Best practices hain: chhote tasks do (ek function, ek file), tests pehle likhwao, aur AI ko reviewer bhi banao (apna code review karwao). AI coding ka maksad typing bachana nahi hai — maksad hai sochne ka time bachana repetitive kaam par.

  • Workflow: plan (khud socho) → prompt (specific + context) → review (har line padho) → iterate (error dikha kar fix)
  • Key rule: chhote tasks do, tests pehle likhwao, AI ka code bina padhe merge mat karo
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: AI tumhara pair programmer hai, replacement nahi — review tumhari zimmedari hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/ai-coding-workflow
