163AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

What is Context Engineering?

Context engineering ka matlab hai: LLM ko sahi time par sahi information dena — taaki wo behtar jawab de. Prompt engineering sirf ek prompt likhne ka tareeka hai; context engineering poora system hai jo decide karta hai ki model ko kya dikhana hai.

Context me kya aata hai: system instructions (model ka role), user prompt, conversation history, retrieved documents (RAG se), tool results, aur examples (few-shot). Context engineering ka kaam hai in sab ko context window ki limit me fit karna — sabse relevant cheezein rakhna, bekaar cheezein hatana.

Techniques hain: summarization (purani history ko summarize karo), retrieval (sirf relevant docs lao), prioritization (important cheezein pehle), aur compaction (context bhar jaaye to purana hissa compress karo). Context engineering hi hai jo RAG aur agents ko reliable banata hai.

  • Context = instructions + prompt + history + retrieved docs + tool results + examples
  • Techniques: summarization, retrieval, prioritization, compaction
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: LLM ki quality uske context ki quality hai — garbage in, garbage out
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/what-is-context-engineering
← PehleAI Coding WorkflowAgla →Context Engineering vs Prompt Engineering
164AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

Context Engineering vs Prompt Engineering

Context engineering vs prompt engineering — dono ka maksad LLM se behtar jawab nikalna hai, lekin scope alag hai. Prompt engineering ek prompt ko behtar likhne ka tareeka hai. Context engineering poore context ko manage karne ka system hai.

Prompt engineering me aata hai: clear instructions likhna, examples dena (few-shot), role dena ("tum ek expert ho"), aur output format batana. Ye ek interaction ki baat hai — ek prompt, ek jawab.

Context engineering me aata hai: kya retrieve karna hai, kitni history rakhni hai, tools ke results kaise dikhane hain, context window kaise manage karni hai, aur jab context bhar jaaye to kya karna hai. Ye poori application ki baat hai — RAG, agents, aur long conversations me yehi kaam aata hai. Simple chatbot me prompt engineering kaafi hai; agents aur RAG apps me context engineering zaroori hai.

  • Prompt engineering: ek prompt ko behtar likhna (instructions, examples, format)
  • Context engineering: poora context manage karna (retrieval, history, tools, window limits)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: prompt = ek baar ki baat, context = poori application ki strategy
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/context-engineering-vs-prompt-engineering

165AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

How ChatGPT Apps Work

ChatGPT apps wo applications hain jo ChatGPT ke andar chalte hain — user ChatGPT se baat karte hue hi app use kar sakta hai, bina alag website khole. Example: ChatGPT me "mere liye ek playlist banao" bolte hi Spotify app khul jaata hai aur playlist ban jaati hai.

Apps kaam karte hain MCP (Model Context Protocol) par: app ek MCP server expose karta hai — jisme tools, resources, aur UI components hote hain. ChatGPT model decide karta hai kab app ko call karna hai, app apna UI ChatGPT ke andar render karta hai, aur user usi se interact karta hai.

App developers ke liye iska fayda hai distribution — ChatGPT ke hundreds of millions users tak seedha pahunch. User ke liye fayda hai no context switch — baat-cheet me hi kaam ho jaata hai. Ye apps ka naya model hai: conversational interface + app functionality.

  • How it works: MCP server (tools + UI) → ChatGPT model decides when to call → app renders inside ChatGPT
  • Key trade-off: distribution (ChatGPT ke users) vs control (app ChatGPT ke UI rules me chalta hai)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: ChatGPT apps ka idea hai — user ko app tak nahi, app ko user tak le jao

Design a personal AI chat assistant

Personal AI chat assistant design — ek aisa assistant jo tumhe jaanta hai: tumhari emails, calendar, notes, aur preferences — aur tumhare liye kaam karta hai. Ye sirf chatbot nahi hai, ye tumhara digital assistant hai.

Architecture ke parts hain: LLM (reasoning), memory system (long-term — tumhari preferences aur facts; short-term — current conversation), data connectors (email, calendar, files — MCP ya APIs se), RAG (tumhare documents se jawab), aur tools (calendar me event daalna, email bhejna).

Sabse mushkil hissa hai memory aur privacy: assistant ko tumhare baare me bahut kuch pata hona chahiye, lekin wo data safe bhi rehna chahiye. Memory ko embeddings + vector DB me store karo, aur sensitive data ko encrypt karo. Personalization vs privacy hi is system ka core tradeoff hai.

  • Components: LLM + memory (long/short-term) + data connectors + RAG + tools
  • Hardest part: memory + privacy — assistant ko sab pata hona chahiye, lekin data safe bhi rehna chahiye
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: personal assistant me memory hi product hai — bina memory ke wo sirf chatbot hai

How to build an AI coding assistant

AI coding assistant banana — jaise Copilot ya Cursor — iska matlab hai ek aisa system jo developer ke code ko samajh kar, autocomplete, chat, aur edits de. Iske 4 core components hain.

Pehla: context gathering — current file, cursor position, open files, aur relevant code (RAG se codebase se) — ye sab LLM ko dena. Dusra: LLM — code generation ke liye fine-tuned ya general model. Teesra: code intelligence — language server (LSP) se symbols, types, aur definitions — taaki suggestions accurate hon.

Chautha: UX — inline autocomplete (ghost text), chat panel, aur agent mode (multi-file edits). Sabse mushkil hissa hai context: bade codebase me se sahi code dhoondh kar LLM ko dena — iske liye embeddings + retrieval + ranking chahiye. Aur latency: autocomplete 100ms me aana chahiye, warna developer ruk jaata hai.

  • Components: context gathering (RAG + LSP) → LLM → code intelligence → UX (autocomplete/chat/agent)
  • Hardest parts: relevant context retrieval + low latency (autocomplete < 100ms)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: coding assistant me context hi sab kuch hai — galat context = galat code

Claude folder (`.claude/`) ek project me wo directory hai jahan Claude Code ke liye project-specific configuration rakhi jaati hai — jaise instructions, commands, aur agents. Ye folder project ko Claude ke liye "smart" banata hai.

  • Key benefit: consistency — har session me same project rules follow hote hain
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: CLAUDE.md ko Git me commit karo — ye team ka shared brain hai

170AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

Agentic Design Patterns

Agentic design patterns — AI agents banate waqt use hone wale reusable solutions. Main patterns hain: ReAct, planning, reflection, tool use, multi-agent, aur human-in-the-loop.

ReAct (Reason + Act): agent ek step sochta hai, phir action leta hai, phir result dekh kar agla step sochta hai — simple tasks ke liye best. Planning: pehle poora plan banao, phir execute karo — complex tasks ke liye. Reflection: agent apne kaam ko khud check karta hai aur galti mile to sudharta hai.

Tool use: agent ko tools dena — har tool ka clear description. Multi-agent: kai agents mil kar kaam karein — ek planner, ek executor, ek reviewer. Human-in-the-loop: risky actions se pehle insaan se approval lo. Pattern chunne ka rule: task ki complexity se chuno — simple task me ReAct, complex me planning + reflection.

  • Patterns: ReAct (simple) → planning (complex) → reflection (self-correction) → tool use → multi-agent → human-in-the-loop
  • Key rule: task ki complexity se pattern chuno — har task ko multi-agent mat banao
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: agent patterns ka maksad reliability hai — zyada steps = zyada chances galti ke, isliye guardrails zaroori hain
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/agentic-design-patterns
← PehleClaude Code (Part 1–5)Agla →How to Build a Domain Expert AI
171AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

How to Build a Domain Expert AI

Domain expert AI banana — ek aisa AI jo kisi ek field (jaise law, medicine, ya finance) ka expert ho. General LLM ko domain expert banane ke 3 tareeke hain: RAG, fine-tuning, aur dono ka combination.

RAG se model ko domain documents diye jaate hain — model unhe padh kar jawab deta hai. Ye fast aur sasta hai, aur documents update karna aasan hai. Fine-tuning se model ko domain data par aur train kiya jaata hai — model domain ki language aur patterns seekh leta hai, lekin ye mehenga hai aur data chahiye.

Best approach hai dono ka combination: fine-tuning se model domain ki style aur terminology seekhe, RAG se wo latest facts laaye. Aur evaluation zaroori hai — domain experts se test karwao ki AI ke jawab sahi hain ya nahi. Domain expert AI me accuracy hi sab kuch hai — ek galat jawab (jaise galat legal advice) nuksaan pahuncha sakta hai.

  • 3 approaches: RAG (fast, sasta, updatable) → fine-tuning (deep, mehenga) → dono (best)
  • Key trade-off: RAG (facts fresh, lekin retrieval par dependent) vs fine-tuning (knowledge andar, lekin update mushkil)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: domain AI me accuracy hi product hai — experts se eval karwana zaroori hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/fine-tuning-ai-models

172AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

29 LLM Evaluation Concepts

LLM evaluation ke 29 concepts — ye wo terms hain jo LLM apps ko test karte waqt aate hain. Evaluation ka matlab hai: LLM ke jawab sahi hain ya nahi, kaise naapein — kyunki LLM ke jawab deterministic nahi hote, traditional testing kaam nahi karta.

Concepts 4 groups me hain: metrics (accuracy, relevance, faithfulness, hallucination rate, latency, cost), methods (human eval, LLM-as-judge, golden datasets, A/B testing), types (offline evals, online evals, red-teaming, regression testing), aur infrastructure (eval datasets, tracing, observability, prompt versioning).

Evaluation ka sabse important concept hai golden dataset: ek set of test cases jinke sahi jawab pata hon — har change ke baad in par model ko test karo. Aur LLM-as-judge: ek aur LLM se jawab ko score karwao — fast hai, lekin judge bhi biased ho sakta hai. Production LLM apps me evals hi quality ki guarantee hain.

  • 4 groups: metrics (accuracy, faithfulness, hallucination) → methods (human, LLM-as-judge, golden data) → types (offline/online/red-team) → infra (tracing, versioning)
  • Key concepts: golden dataset + LLM-as-judge + regression evals
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: LLM apps me evals hi tests hain — bina evals ke har deploy ek gamble hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/llm-evals

173AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

AI Evals, Guardrails and Security

AI evals, guardrails aur security — ye teeno mil kar LLM apps ko reliable aur safe banate hain. Evals batate hain ki model sahi jawab de raha hai ya nahi. Guardrails galat ya harmful output ko rokte hain. Security attacks se bachati hai.

Evals: har release se pehle golden dataset par test karo — accuracy, faithfulness, aur hallucination rate naapo. Guardrails: input par (harmful prompt block karo), output par (PII, harmful content filter karo), aur tools par (agent kya kar sakta hai, limit karo). Guardrails ke liye alag models ya rule-based filters use hote hain.

Security: sabse bade attacks hain prompt injection (user model ko galat instructions de), jailbreaking (safety rules todna), aur data leakage (model se sensitive data nikalna). Defense hai: input validation, output filtering, least privilege tools, aur monitoring. Production AI apps me ye teeno — evals, guardrails, security — non-negotiable hain.

  • 3 pillars: evals (quality naapo) → guardrails (galat output roko) → security (attacks se bachao)
  • Top attacks: prompt injection, jailbreaking, data leakage
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: LLM apps me safety ek feature nahi hai — ye foundation hai; pehle din se lagao
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/llm-evaluation-and-guardrails
← Pehle29 LLM Evaluation ConceptsAgla →How Fine Tuning Works
174AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

How Fine Tuning Works

Fine-tuning ka matlab hai: pehle se trained LLM ko apne data par aur train karna — taaki wo tumhare task ya domain me behtar ho jaaye. Base model general hota hai; fine-tuned model specialized hota hai.

Fine-tuning ka flow hai: dataset taiyaar karo (input-output pairs — jaise 1000 examples of good responses) → training (model ke weights ko thoda adjust karo — poora model ya sirf kuch layers) → evaluate karo (kya model behtar hua?) → deploy karo.

LoRA/QLoRA fine-tuning ke efficient tareeke hain: poora model train karne ki jagah sirf chhote adapter weights train karte hain — isse cost aur memory dono kam hoti hai. Fine-tuning kab karein? Jab style, format, ya domain language sikhana ho. Kab nahi? Jab facts sikhane hon — uske liye RAG behtar hai, kyunki facts update karna fine-tuning se mushkil hai.

  • Flow: dataset (input-output pairs) → training (weights adjust) → evaluate → deploy
  • LoRA/QLoRA: sirf adapter weights train karo — sasta aur fast fine-tuning
  • Key rule: style/format ke liye fine-tuning, facts ke liye RAG
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: fine-tuning knowledge nahi, behavior sikhata hai — facts RAG se aate hain
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/llm-fine-tuning-guide-with-lora-and-qlora

175AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

Everything You Need to Know to Design GenAI Systems From Scratch

GenAI systems ko scratch se design karna — ye section poora blueprint deta hai: LLM app banane ke liye kya-kya chahiye, aur har component kaise judta hai.

Blueprint ke parts hain: LLM layer (model choice — hosted API vs self-hosted, cost vs control), prompt layer (system prompts, templates, versioning), retrieval layer (RAG — embeddings, vector DB, chunking), memory layer (conversation + long-term), tools layer (function calling, MCP), guardrails (safety + validation), observability (tracing, evals, cost tracking), aur serving (API, caching, rate limiting).

Design ke principles hain: pehle simple banao (prompt + RAG se shuru karo, agents baad me), evals pehle din se, cost track karo (har request ka token cost), aur fallback rakho (LLM fail ho to kya hoga). GenAI system design me sabse badi galti hai pehle din se complex agent banana — simple pipeline se shuru karo, phir complexity badhao.

  • Blueprint layers: LLM → prompt → retrieval (RAG) → memory → tools → guardrails → observability → serving
  • Key principles: simple se shuru karo, evals pehle din se, cost track karo, fallback rakho
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: GenAI system me complexity dheere badhao — simple RAG pehle, agent baad me

← PehleHow Fine Tuning WorksAgla →AI Agent - Graph Shaped Memory
176AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

AI Agent - Graph Shaped Memory

Graph-shaped memory AI agents ke liye ek aisi memory hai jo facts ko graph ki tarah store karti hai — nodes (entities) aur edges (relationships). Normal vector memory "similar" cheezein dhoondhti hai; graph memory "connected" cheezein dhoondhti hai.

Example: agent ko yaad hai "Rahul — works at — Acme" aur "Acme — building — payment product". Jab user poochhe "Rahul kis product par kaam karta hai?" to graph memory dono facts ko connect karke jawab de sakti hai — vector search se ye connection nahi milta. Graph memory me entities extract karo (LLM se), relationships banao, aur graph traversal se relevant facts nikalo.

Iska use hota hai long-term agent memory me — jahan agent ko months ki conversations se facts yaad rakhne hote hain. Tools jaise Graphiti aur Zep isi approach ko use karte hain. Tradeoff hai: graph memory build karna complex hai (entity extraction + graph maintenance), lekin multi-hop questions ka jawab sirf graph hi de sakta hai.

  • Core idea: facts = nodes + edges (relationships); traversal se multi-hop answers
  • Vector vs graph memory: vector = similar dhoondhta hai; graph = connected dhoondhta hai
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: multi-hop questions ("Rahul kis product par kaam karta hai?") ka jawab sirf graph memory de sakti hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/graph-based-agent-memory
← PehleEverything You Need to Know to Design GenAI Systems From ScratchAgla →How GraphRAG Works
177AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

How GraphRAG Works

GraphRAG (Graph Retrieval-Augmented Generation) RAG ka advanced version hai — ye documents ko knowledge graph me badal kar retrieval karta hai. Normal RAG chunks dhoondhta hai; GraphRAG entities aur relationships dhoondhta hai.

GraphRAG ka flow hai: documents ko graph me badlo (LLM se entities aur relationships extract karo) → communities banao (related entities ko groups me) → query aane par graph traverse karo (relevant entities + relationships nikalo) → LLM ko graph context de kar jawab dilwao.

GraphRAG ka fayda hai global questions me: "Is dataset ka overall theme kya hai?" — normal RAG iska jawab nahi de sakta kyunki wo sirf similar chunks dhoondhta hai, theme nahi samajhta. GraphRAG communities ka summary de kar theme bata sakta hai. Nuksaan hai cost aur complexity — graph banana mehenga hai (LLM se extraction), aur har update par graph dobara banana padta hai.

  • Flow: documents → knowledge graph (entities + relationships) → communities → graph traversal → LLM answer
  • Best for: global/theme questions — normal RAG sirf specific facts dhoondhta hai
  • Key trade-off: better global understanding (lekin graph building mehenga aur slow)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: "overall theme kya hai?" jaise sawaalon ka jawab GraphRAG deta hai, normal RAG nahi
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/graph-retrieval-augmented-generation
← PehleAI Agent - Graph Shaped MemoryAgla →What is AI Infrastructure
178AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

What is AI Infrastructure

AI infrastructure wo sab kuch hai jo AI models ko train aur serve karne ke liye chahiye: GPUs, networking, storage, orchestration, aur serving frameworks. AI apps ka backend AI infrastructure par chalta hai.

Training infrastructure me hain: GPU clusters (hazaaron GPUs — NVIDIA H100 jaise), high-speed networking (InfiniBand — GPUs ke beech fast communication), distributed training frameworks (PyTorch DDP, DeepSpeed), aur checkpointing (training ruk jaaye to wapas se shuru karna).

Inference infrastructure me hain: model serving (vLLM, TensorRT-LLM — batching + KV cache se fast inference), autoscaling (load ke hisaab se GPUs badhao/ghatao), caching (prompt cache, semantic cache), aur cost optimization (spot instances, quantization). AI infrastructure ka sabse bada cost hai GPUs — isliye utilization hi sab kuch hai.

  • Training infra: GPU clusters + InfiniBand + distributed training + checkpointing
  • Inference infra: serving (vLLM) + autoscaling + caching + cost optimization
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: AI infra me GPU utilization hi sab kuch hai — idle GPU paisa jala raha hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/what-is-ai-infrastructure
← PehleHow GraphRAG WorksAgla →How to Build AI Agents That Don’t Start Over When They Fail
179AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

How to Build AI Agents That Don’t Start Over When They Fail

Durable AI agents — wo agents jo fail hone par dobara shuru se nahi karte, balki wahi se continue karte hain jahan ruke the. Normal agent crash ho to poora kaam dobara karna padta hai; durable agent apni state save karta rehta hai.

Durability ke 3 pillars hain: persistent state (har step ke baad state database me save karo — kya ho gaya, kya baaki hai), idempotent steps (har step dobara chalaya ja sake bina double kaam ke), aur checkpointing (lambi tasks me beech-beech me checkpoints banao).

Implementation me workflow engines (jaise Temporal, Inngest, ya LangGraph ki persistence) use hote hain — ye agent ke har step ko durable task ki tarah treat karte hain. Agar agent beech me crash ho ya timeout ho to engine use wahi se dobara chalata hai. Lambi tasks (research, data processing) me durability must-have hai — bina iske agent unreliable hoga.

  • 3 pillars: persistent state (har step save) → idempotent steps (dobara safe) → checkpointing
  • Tools: workflow engines (Temporal, Inngest, LangGraph persistence)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: agent ko durable banao — crash hone par wahi se continue kare, shuru se nahi
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/durable-ai-agents
← PehleWhat is AI InfrastructureAgla →How LLM Inference Works
180AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

How LLM Inference Works

LLM inference ka matlab hai: trained model se jawab nikalna — prompt do, tokens pao. Inference ke andar kya hota hai, ye samajhna LLM apps ko optimize karne ke liye zaroori hai.

Inference ka flow hai: prefill (prompt ke saare tokens ko ek saath process karo — aur unke key-value pairs ko KV cache me save karo) → decode (ek-ek token generate karo — har naya token pichhle tokens + KV cache ko dekh kar banta hai). Decode hi slow hissa hai — har token ke liye poora model chalana padta hai.

Inference ki speed ko tokens per second me naapa jaata hai. Speed badhane ke tareeke hain: KV cache (dobara compute mat karo), batching (kai requests ek saath), quantization (weights ko chhota karo — FP16 se INT8), aur speculative decoding (chhota model guess kare, bada model verify kare). Inference cost hi LLM apps ka sabse bada cost hai.

  • 2 phases: prefill (prompt process + KV cache) → decode (ek-ek token generate — slow hissa)
  • Speed tricks: KV cache + batching + quantization + speculative decoding
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: decode hi bottleneck hai — har token ke liye poora model chalta hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/how-llm-inference-works
← PehleHow to Build AI Agents That Don’t Start Over When They FailAgla →How to make LLM Inference Faster and More Efficient
181AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

How to make LLM Inference Faster and More Efficient

LLM inference ko fast aur efficient banana — ye wo techniques hain jo production LLM apps me use hoti hain. 5 main techniques hain: batching, KV cache, quantization, speculative decoding, aur caching.

Continuous batching (vLLM): kai requests ko ek saath process karo — GPU idle nahi rehta. KV cache: prompt ke key-value pairs ko save karo — dobara compute mat karo. Quantization: model weights ko FP16 se INT8/INT4 me badlo — memory aur compute dono kam, quality thodi kam.

Speculative decoding: chhota draft model kai tokens guess karta hai, bada model ek saath verify karta hai — 2–3x speedup. Caching: prompt cache (same prompt dobara aaye to result cache se) aur semantic cache (similar prompt ka cached jawab). In techniques ko combine karke inference 5–10x sasta ho sakta hai.

  • 5 techniques: continuous batching → KV cache → quantization → speculative decoding → caching (prompt + semantic)
  • Key trade-off: speed/cost (quantization, caching) vs quality (quantization se thodi quality kam)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: inference optimization me batching aur KV cache sabse bada fayda dete hain — pehle ye lagao
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/how-to-optimize-llm-inference
← PehleHow LLM Inference WorksAgla →Design Knowledge Q&A System
182AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

Design Knowledge Q&A System

Knowledge Q&A system design — ek aisa system jo company ke documents se sawaalon ke jawab de — jaise "hamari leave policy kya hai?" ya "is product ka pricing kya hai?". Ye RAG ka sabse common production use case hai.

Architecture hai: ingestion pipeline (documents ko chunk karo → embeddings banao → vector DB me store karo) → query pipeline (user ka sawaal → embedding → vector DB se relevant chunks → LLM ko context de kar jawab) → citation (jawab ke saath source documents dikhao).

Mushkil hisse hain: chunking (chunks ka size — chhota to context tootta hai, bada to irrelevant), retrieval quality (hybrid search — vector + keyword), freshness (documents update hon to index bhi update ho), aur evaluation (jawab sahi hain ya nahi — golden dataset se). Enterprise Q&A me access control bhi zaroori hai — user ko sirf wahi documents dikhein jinke liye use permission hai.

  • Pipeline: ingestion (chunk → embed → vector DB) → query (retrieve → LLM → answer + citations)
  • Hardest parts: chunking, retrieval quality (hybrid search), freshness, access control
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: Q&A system me citation zaroori hai — bina source ke jawab par bharosa nahi hota
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/ai-based-knowledge-management-system
← PehleHow to make LLM Inference Faster and More EfficientAgla →LLM Concepts, Simply Explained
183AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

LLM Concepts, Simply Explained

LLM concepts, simply explained — ye wo core terms hain jo LLMs ko samajhne ke liye chahiye: transformer, attention, tokens, parameters, aur training.

Transformer wo architecture hai jis par saare modern LLMs bane hain (2017 ka "Attention Is All You Need" paper). Attention mechanism model ko batata hai ki ek word ka matlab samajhne ke liye sentence ke kaunse words important hain. Tokens text ke chhote units hain — model tokens par kaam karta hai, words par nahi.

Parameters model ke weights hain — jaise GPT-4 ke ~1.8 trillion parameters (rumored). Zyada parameters = zyada capability, lekin zyada cost bhi. Training me model ko trillions of tokens dikhaye jaate hain taaki wo language ke patterns seekh le. Inference me trained model se jawab nikale jaate hain. Ye concepts LLM ki neev hain.

  • Core: transformer (architecture) → attention (kaunsa word important hai) → tokens (units) → parameters (weights)
  • Training vs inference: training = sikhana (trillions of tokens), inference = jawab nikalna
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: attention hi transformer ki jaan hai — model ko pata chalta hai kis word par dhyaan dena hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/llm-concepts
← PehleDesign Knowledge Q&A SystemAgla →LLM as a Judge
184AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

LLM as a Judge

LLM-as-a-judge ka matlab hai: ek LLM se dusre LLM ke jawab ko score karwana — evaluation ka fast tareeka. Human evaluation accurate lekin slow aur mehenga hai; LLM-as-judge fast aur sasta hai, lekin biased ho sakta hai.

Kaam aise karta hai: judge LLM ko prompt, model ka jawab, aur scoring criteria (jaise accuracy, relevance, tone) diye jaate hain — judge score deta hai (jaise 1–5) aur reason bhi. Isse thousands of test cases ko minutes me evaluate kiya ja sakta hai.

Judge ki problems hain: bias (judge apne jaisi style ko zyada score deta hai), verbosity bias (lambe jawab ko behtar maanta hai), aur self-preference (apne model ke jawab ko zyada score). Solutions hain: multiple judges, human spot-checks (kuch cases insaan check kare), aur clear rubrics (scoring criteria bahut specific likho). LLM-as-judge relative comparison (A vs B) me best hai, absolute scoring me kam.

  • How it works: judge LLM + prompt + answer + rubric → score + reason
  • Biases: self-preference, verbosity bias, style bias — solutions: multiple judges + human spot-checks + clear rubrics
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: LLM-as-judge relative comparison (A vs B) me best hai — absolute score par bharosa mat karo
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/how-to-build-llm-judge
← PehleLLM Concepts, Simply ExplainedAgla →MCP - A Deep Dive
185AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

MCP - A Deep Dive

MCP (Model Context Protocol) ek open standard hai jisse LLM apps tools aur data sources se connect hote hain — jaise USB standard ne devices ko connect kiya, MCP ne AI apps ko tools se connect kiya.

MCP ke 3 main parts hain: hosts (jaise Claude Desktop — jahan LLM chalta hai), clients (host ke andar ka connector), aur servers (jo tools, resources, aur prompts expose karte hain). Server batata hai "mere paas ye tools hain" — jaise database query tool, file read tool, ya calendar tool. LLM decide karta hai kab kaunsa tool use karna hai.

MCP se pehle har tool ke liye alag integration likhni padti thi (M×N problem). MCP se ek baar server likho, har MCP client se chalao. MCP servers har cheez ke liye ban rahe hain — databases, GitHub, Slack, filesystems. MCP hi ChatGPT apps aur Claude ke tool ecosystem ki neev hai.

  • Parts: host (LLM app) → client (connector) → server (tools, resources, prompts)
  • Key benefit: M×N integration problem khatam — ek server, har client
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: MCP = AI apps ke liye USB standard — tools ko ek standard tareeke se connect karo
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/how-mcp-works
← PehleLLM as a JudgeAgla →Multi-Agent Architectures, Clearly Explained
186AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

Multi-Agent Architectures, Clearly Explained

Multi-agent architectures — jab kai AI agents mil kar ek kaam karte hain, har agent ki apni role hoti hai. Ek agent sab kuch karne ki jagah, agents ki team banayi jaati hai — jaise insaanon ki team.

Common patterns hain: supervisor pattern (ek supervisor agent tasks ko tod kar worker agents ko deta hai, phir results ko combine karta hai), sequential pipeline (agents ek ke baad ek kaam karte hain — jaise writer → reviewer → editor), parallel workers (kai agents ek saath alag hisse karte hain), aur debate (agents ek dusre se behes karke behtar jawab nikalte hain).

Multi-agent ka fayda hai specialization — har agent apne kaam me expert hota hai. Nuksaan hai complexity aur cost — agents ke beech communication, coordination, aur har agent ka LLM cost. Multi-agent sirf tab jab task complex aur decomposable ho — simple tasks ke liye ek agent kaafi hai.

  • Patterns: supervisor (orchestrator + workers) → sequential pipeline → parallel workers → debate
  • Key trade-off: specialization (behtar quality) vs complexity + cost (coordination + zyada LLM calls)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: multi-agent tab jab task complex ho — simple kaam me ek agent hi kaafi hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/multi-agent-system

187AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

How ML Systems Actually Work: From Data to Deployment

ML systems kaise kaam karte hain — data se lekar deployment tak. ML system sirf model nahi hai; wo poora pipeline hai: data collection → training → evaluation → deployment → monitoring.

Data: model ki quality data ki quality hai — data collect karo, clean karo, label karo, aur version karo. Training: model ko data par train karo — experiments track karo (kaunsa data, kaunse hyperparameters, kya result). Evaluation: model ko test data par naapo — accuracy, precision, recall, aur business metrics.

Deployment: model ko API ya batch job ki tarah serve karo — versioning aur rollback ke saath. Monitoring: production me model ka performance track karo — data drift (input data badal gaya) aur concept drift (duniya badal gayi) ko pakdo, aur model ko retrain karo. ML systems me model code ka sirf 5% hissa hota hai — baaki data, infra, aur monitoring hai.

  • Pipeline: data → training (experiments) → evaluation → deployment (versioning) → monitoring (drift)
  • Key insight: model code sirf ~5% hai — baaki data, infra, monitoring hai
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: production ML me drift hi sabse bada dushman hai — monitor karo, retrain karo
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/machine-learning-system-design-interview

188AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

AI Agents: State, Memory, Consistency

AI agents me state, memory aur consistency — ye wo problems hain jo agents ko reliable banane me aati hain. Agent ke paas state hoti hai (wo kahan hai, kya kar chuka hai), memory hoti hai (kya yaad hai), aur consistency ka sawaal hota hai (state aur memory sync me hain ya nahi).

State agent ka current progress hai — kaunsa step chal raha hai, kya tools use hue. State ko persist karna zaroori hai (crash par bhi na khoye). Memory do tarah ki hai: short-term (current conversation) aur long-term (facts, preferences — vector DB ya graph me). Consistency ka matlab hai: agent ki memory aur duniya ki asli state match karni chahiye — agar user ne kuch badla hai to agent ko pata hona chahiye.

Mushkil hissa hai multi-agent consistency: kai agents ek hi state par kaam karein to conflicts hote hain — iske liye locking, versioning, ya event sourcing use hoti hai. Agent systems me state management hi sabse zyada bugs deta hai — isliye explicit state machines (LangGraph jaisi) use karo, implicit state mat rakho.

  • 3 concepts: state (progress — persist karo) → memory (short/long-term) → consistency (memory = reality)
  • Key rule: state explicit rakho (state machines) — implicit state bugs deti hai
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: agent ki reliability uski state management se tay hoti hai — crash par state na khoye
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/ai-agent-memory

How to use NotebookLM

NotebookLM ek AI tool hai jo tumhare documents ko padh kar un par baat karta hai — jaise ek research assistant jo tumhari files ko samajhta hai. Tum PDFs, docs, ya web pages upload karte ho, aur NotebookLM unse jawab deta hai, summaries banata hai, aur audio overviews bhi banata hai.

Kaam aise karta hai: documents ko index kiya jaata hai (chunks + embeddings) → tum sawaal poochhte ho → relevant chunks retrieve hote hain → LLM jawab deta hai citations ke saath (jawab kis document ke kis hisse se aaya). Sabse popular feature hai Audio Overview — documents ko do hosts ki podcast-style baat-cheet me badal deta hai.

NotebookLM ka use hota hai research, study, aur knowledge work me — students apne notes se padhte hain, researchers papers se summary nikalte hain. Iska fayda hai grounding: jawab sirf tumhare documents se aate hain, model ki general knowledge se nahi — isliye hallucination kam hota hai.

  • How it works: upload docs → index (chunks + embeddings) → ask → retrieve → answer with citations
  • Killer feature: Audio Overview — documents se podcast-style conversation
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: NotebookLM ka jawab tumhare documents se grounded hota hai — isliye hallucination kam hai

How OpenClaw Works

OpenClaw ek open-source AI agent framework hai jo tumhare computer par chalta hai — ye WhatsApp, Telegram, Discord jaise messaging apps se connect hota hai aur tumhare liye kaam karta hai. Iska idea hai: apna personal AI assistant, apne control me.

OpenClaw kaam karta hai gateway + agents architecture par: gateway messaging apps se connect hota hai (messages aate hain), agents unhe process karte hain (LLM + tools se), aur jawab wapas bhejte hain. Tools me hain: file access, web search, shell commands, aur custom integrations.

OpenClaw ki khaasiyat hai self-hosted aur open-source — tumhara data tumhare paas rehta hai, aur tum koi bhi LLM use kar sakte ho (hosted ya local). Iska use hota hai personal automation me — messages ka jawab dena, reminders, file management, aur daily tasks. Security ka dhyaan rakho: agent ko shell access dena powerful hai, lekin risky bhi — guardrails zaroori hain.

  • Architecture: gateway (messaging apps) → agents (LLM + tools) → actions
  • Key benefit: self-hosted + open-source — data tumhare paas, koi bhi LLM
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: personal agent ko shell access dena powerful hai — guardrails ke bina mat do

AI Agent Observability

AI agent observability ka matlab hai: agent kya kar raha hai, ye dekh paana — uske thoughts, tool calls, costs, aur errors ko track karna. Normal apps me logs kaafi hote hain; agents me traces chahiye — kyunki agent ka har step LLM call hai.

Observability ke parts hain: tracing (har agent run ka step-by-step record — kaunsa prompt, kaunsa tool, kya result, kitna time), metrics (success rate, latency, token cost, tool error rate), evals (jawab ki quality), aur alerts (agent loop me phas gaya ya cost badh gayi to alert).

Tools jaise LangSmith, Langfuse, aur OpenTelemetry agent traces ko capture karte hain. Best practice hai: har LLM call ko trace karo (prompt + response + tokens + cost), har tool call ko log karo, aur cost per run ko dashboard par dikhao. Bina observability ke agent ek black box hai — aur black box ko debug nahi kiya ja sakta.

  • Parts: tracing (step-by-step) → metrics (success, latency, cost) → evals → alerts
  • Key rule: har LLM call trace karo (prompt + response + tokens + cost)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: agent bina observability ke black box hai — debug karne ke liye traces chahiye

192AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

Prompt Engineering - A Deep Dive

Prompt engineering ka deep dive — LLM se behtar jawab nikalne ki kala aur science. Prompt engineering ke 5 core techniques hain: clear instructions, few-shot examples, chain-of-thought, role prompting, aur structured output.

Clear instructions: jo chahiye wahi likho — vague prompt = vague jawab. Few-shot: 2–3 examples do — model pattern samajh jaata hai. Chain-of-thought: "step by step socho" bolo — complex reasoning me accuracy badhti hai. Role prompting: "tum ek senior engineer ho" — model us style me jawab deta hai. Structured output: JSON ya table format maango — taaki output ko code se parse kar sako.

Advanced techniques hain: self-consistency (kai baar jawab nikal kar majority lo), prompt chaining (bade task ko chhote prompts me todo), aur system prompt design (model ka behavior set karo). Prompt engineering me sabse important hai iterate karna — pehla prompt kabhi perfect nahi hota, evals se measure karo aur sudharo.

  • 5 core techniques: clear instructions → few-shot → chain-of-thought → role prompting → structured output
  • Advanced: self-consistency, prompt chaining, system prompt design
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: prompt engineering iteration hai — evals se measure karo, phir sudharo
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/prompt-engineering-guide
← PehleAI Agent ObservabilityAgla →What Is Reinforcement Learning
193AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

What Is Reinforcement Learning

Reinforcement learning (RL) machine learning ka wo tareeka hai jisme agent try karke seekhta hai — jaise bachcha seekhta hai: sahi kaam par reward, galat par punishment. RL me agent environment se interact karta hai, actions leta hai, aur rewards paata hai — aur dheere-dheere behtar policy seekh leta hai.

RL ke core concepts hain: agent (jo seekhta hai), environment (duniya), state (current situation), action (kya karna hai), reward (feedback), aur policy (state se action ka mapping). Agent ka goal hai cumulative reward maximize karna — sirf abhi ka reward nahi, future ka bhi.

RL ka sabse bada challenge hai exploration vs exploitation: naya try karo (explore) ya jo pata hai use karo (exploit)? RL ke famous examples hain: game playing (AlphaGo), robotics, aur LLM training (RLHF — human feedback se LLMs ko align karna). RLHF hi hai jo ChatGPT ko helpful aur safe banata hai.

  • Core loop: state → action → reward → learn → better policy
  • Key trade-off: exploration (naya try karo) vs exploitation (jo pata hai use karo)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: RLHF hi LLMs ko helpful banata hai — RL sirf games ke liye nahi hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/what-is-reinforcement-learning
← PehlePrompt Engineering - A Deep DiveAgla →How RAG Works
194AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

How RAG Works

RAG (Retrieval-Augmented Generation) ka matlab hai: LLM ko jawab dene se pehle relevant documents dhoondh kar dena — taaki wo apni training knowledge ki jagah tumhare data se jawab de. RAG ke bina LLM purani ya galat jaankari de sakta hai; RAG se jawab grounded aur fresh hota hai.

RAG ka flow hai: indexing (documents ko chunks me todo → har chunk ka embedding banao → vector database me store karo) → retrieval (user ka sawaal aaye to uska embedding banao → vector DB se sabse similar chunks dhoondho) → generation (chunks + sawaal ko LLM ko do → jawab banao).

RAG ko behtar banane ke tareeke hain: chunking strategy (chunk size aur overlap), hybrid search (vector + keyword — BM25), reranking (retrieved chunks ko dobara rank karo), aur query rewriting (sawaal ko behtar banao retrieval ke liye). RAG ka sabse bada fayda hai: knowledge update karna aasan — documents badlo, index update karo, model ko retrain nahi karna padta.

  • Flow: indexing (chunk → embed → vector DB) → retrieval (similar chunks) → generation (LLM + context → answer)
  • Improvements: hybrid search (vector + keyword) → reranking → query rewriting
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: RAG facts ke liye hai, fine-tuning behavior ke liye — dono ko confuse mat karo
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/how-rag-works
← PehleWhat Is Reinforcement LearningAgla →How AI Research Agent Works
195AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

How AI Research Agent Works

AI research agent ek aisa agent hai jo kisi topic par research karke report banata hai — sources dhoondhta hai, padhta hai, summarize karta hai, aur findings ko organize karta hai. Ye multi-step agent ka best example hai.

Research agent ka flow hai: plan (topic ko sub-questions me todo) → search (web search / MCP tools se sources dhoondho) → read (sources ko padho aur relevant parts nikalo) → synthesize (findings ko combine karke report banao — citations ke saath) → verify (claims ko dobara check karo).

Mushkil hisse hain: source quality (reliable sources chunna), coverage (kuch miss to nahi hua), aur hallucination (agent ne kuch invent to nahi kiya — isliye har claim ka citation zaroori hai). Research agents me MCP ka use hota hai — search tools, browser tools, aur document tools ko connect karne ke liye.

  • Flow: plan (sub-questions) → search → read → synthesize (citations) → verify
  • Hardest parts: source quality, coverage, hallucination (citation se roko)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: research agent me har claim ka citation zaroori hai — bina source ke report par bharosa nahi
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/how-to-build-an-ai-research-agent-with-mcp

196AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

How Realtime AI Agent Works

Realtime AI agent ek aisa agent hai jo bol kar baat karta hai aur turant jawab deta hai — jaise phone par baat kar rahe hon. Isme text ki jagah voice hoti hai, aur latency milliseconds me honi chahiye.

Realtime agent ka pipeline hai: speech-to-text (user ki awaaz ko text me badlo — streaming) → LLM (jawab socho — streaming tokens) → text-to-speech (jawab ko awaaz me badlo — streaming) → audio output. Har stage streaming hoti hai — user ko poora jawab aane ka wait nahi karna padta, pehla word aate hi bolna shuru ho jaata hai.

Mushkil hisse hain: latency (total < 500ms hona chahiye — nahi to baat-cheet awkward lagti hai), interruption (user beech me bole to agent ruk jaaye — barge-in), aur turn-taking (kab user bol raha hai, kab agent). Event-driven architecture use hoti hai — har event (speech start, speech end, tool result) par agent react karta hai.

  • Pipeline: STT (streaming) → LLM (streaming tokens) → TTS (streaming) → audio
  • Hardest parts: latency (< 500ms) → interruption (barge-in) → turn-taking
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: realtime agent me latency hi product hai — 500ms se zyada ho to baat-cheet toot jaati hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/event-driven-ai-agent-architecture
← PehleHow AI Research Agent WorksAgla →Spec driven development for AI agents
197AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

Spec driven development for AI agents

Spec-driven development AI agents ke liye ek aisa approach hai jisme pehle spec likhi jaati hai, phir agent banaya jaata hai — jaise TDD me pehle test likha jaata hai. Spec me likha hota hai: agent kya karega, kya nahi karega, kaunse tools use karega, aur success kya hai.

Spec ke parts hain: goal (agent ka maksad), inputs/outputs (kya aayega, kya jaayega), tools (kaunse tools, kya permissions), constraints (kya nahi karna — cost limits, destructive actions), evals (success kaise naapein — test cases), aur failure modes (kya galat ho sakta hai, aur tab kya karein).

Iska fayda hai clarity aur testability: spec se pata chalta hai agent ko kya karna hai, aur evals se pata chalta hai wo kar raha hai ya nahi. Spec-driven approach se vibe coding ki problem khatam hoti hai — jahan agent kuch bhi banata hai aur pata nahi chalta sahi hai ya nahi. Best practice hai: spec likho, evals likho, phir agent banao.

  • Spec parts: goal → inputs/outputs → tools → constraints → evals → failure modes
  • Key benefit: clarity + testability — spec se pata chalta hai kya karna hai, evals se pata chalta hai ho raha hai ya nahi
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: spec pehle, agent baad me — vibe coding se spec-driven development behtar hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/spec-driven-development-ai-agents/
← PehleHow Realtime AI Agent WorksAgla →How to Scale an AI App to 1 Million Users in Production
198AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

How to Scale an AI App to 1 Million Users in Production

AI app ko 1 million users tak scale karna — iska matlab hai LLM inference ko scale karna, jo normal apps se alag hai kyunki har request GPU compute khati hai. Scale karne ke 5 levers hain: caching, batching, smaller models, async, aur cost control.

Caching: prompt cache (same prompt dobara aaye to cached jawab) aur semantic cache (similar sawaal ka cached jawab) — 30–50% requests cache se serve ho sakti hain. Batching: continuous batching se GPU utilization badhao. Smaller models: har request ko bade model par mat bhejo — simple sawaal chhote model se, mushkil bade se (routing).

Async: lambi tasks ko background me karo — user ko turant "kaam ho raha hai" batao, result baad me do. Cost control: har request ka token cost track karo, rate limits lagao, aur context window ko chhota rakho (sirf relevant context bhejo). AI apps me cost hi scaling ki hadd hai — 1M users par har request ka 1 cent bhi $10,000 per day hai.

  • 5 levers: caching (30–50% requests) → batching → model routing (chhota/bada) → async → cost control
  • Key numbers: 1M users × $0.01/request = $10,000/day — cost hi scaling ki hadd hai
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: AI apps me pehle cost optimize karo, phir scale karo — nahi to bill hi scale hoga
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/llm-inference-cost-optimization
← PehleSpec driven development for AI agentsAgla →Vector Database - A Deep Dive
199AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

Vector Database - A Deep Dive

Vector database ek aisa database hai jo embeddings (vectors) ko store aur search karta hai — RAG aur AI apps ki neev. Normal database exact match dhoondhta hai; vector database similar cheezein dhoondhta hai.

Vector DB ke parts hain: storage (vectors + metadata), index (fast search ke liye — HNSW, IVF), aur query (kisi vector ke sabse paas wale vectors nikalo — top-k). HNSW (Hierarchical Navigable Small World) sabse popular index hai — graph-based, fast aur accurate.

Vector DB chunne ke criteria hain: scale (kitne vectors — millions ya billions), latency (search kitni fast — milliseconds), filtering (metadata se filter karna — jaise sirf is user ke documents), aur cost. Popular options hain: pgvector (Postgres me), Pinecone (managed), Qdrant, Weaviate, aur Milvus. RAG apps me vector DB hi retrieval ka engine hai.

  • Parts: storage (vectors + metadata) → index (HNSW/IVF) → query (top-k nearest)
  • Selection criteria: scale → latency → filtering → cost
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: vector DB "similar" dhoondhta hai, "exact" nahi — yehi RAG ki neev hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/what-is-a-vector-database
← PehleHow to Scale an AI App to 1 Million Users in ProductionAgla →Vertical Small LLM
200AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

Vertical Small LLM

Vertical small LLM ka matlab hai: kisi ek field ke liye chhota, specialized language model — bade general model ki jagah. Example: sirf legal documents ke liye, ya sirf medical notes ke liye ek chhota model.

Small LLMs (1B–8B parameters) ko fine-tune karke domain expert banaya jaata hai. Inke fayde hain: sasta (chhota model = kam compute = kam cost), fast (apne servers par chalao, latency kam), private (data bahar nahi jaata), aur specialized (domain me bade model se behtar).

Tradeoff hai: chhota model general knowledge me kamzor hota hai — domain se bahar ke sawaalon par galat jawab de sakta hai. Best approach hai routing: simple/domain sawaal chhote model se, complex/general sawaal bade model se. Vertical SLMs ka trend badh raha hai — har company apne domain ka apna model bana rahi hai.

  • Why small: sasta + fast + private + specialized (domain me bade se behtar)
  • Trade-off: general knowledge me kamzor — domain se bahar galat jawab
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: har kaam ke liye bada model zaroori nahi — domain ka chhota model aksar behtar aur sasta hota hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/fine-tuning-small-language-models
← PehleVector Database - A Deep DiveAgla →How Vector Database Search Billions of Vectors in Milliseconds
201AI Engineering — Agents, RAG & LLM Systems

How Vector Database Search Billions of Vectors in Milliseconds

Vector database billions of vectors ko milliseconds me kaise search karta hai — iska jawab hai approximate nearest neighbor (ANN) indexes, khaas taur par HNSW. Brute force (har vector se compare) billions par impossible hai — ANN smart shortcuts use karta hai.

HNSW ek graph banata hai: har vector ek node hai, aur har node apne sabse paas wale vectors se connected hai. Search karte waqt graph me greedy traversal hoti hai — ek node se shuru karo, uske neighbors me sabse paas wala chuno, aage badho — kuch hi steps me sabse paas wale vectors mil jaate hain. Graph hierarchical hota hai — upar ki layers me kam nodes (long jumps), neeche ki layers me zyada nodes (fine search).

Tradeoff hai accuracy vs speed: ANN exact nearest nahi deta, approximate deta hai — lekin 95%+ accuracy milliseconds me, jo RAG ke liye kaafi hai. IVF (Inverted File Index) ek aur approach hai — vectors ko clusters me baanto, sirf paas wale clusters search karo. Billions-scale search me sharding + quantization bhi use hota hai — vectors ko chhota karke memory me fit karo.

  • Core: HNSW graph + greedy traversal — kuch steps me nearest vectors
  • Key trade-off: approximate (95%+ accuracy, milliseconds) vs exact (100% accuracy, bahut slow)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: vector search me "exact" nahi, "kaafi accurate aur fast" chahiye — ANN hi jawab hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/hnsw-vector-search-explained
← PehleVertical Small LLMAgla →Amazon Dynamo
202Software White Papers — Foundational Papers

Amazon Dynamo

Amazon Dynamo wo paper hai jisne distributed key-value stores ki neev rakhi — 2007 me publish hua, aur is par Cassandra, Riak, aur DynamoDB bane. Dynamo ka problem tha: Amazon ke shopping cart ko hamesha available rehna tha — chahe servers fail hon, network toote, ya data center down ho.

Dynamo ke core ideas hain: consistent hashing (keys ko ring par baantna — node add/remove par sirf thoda data move hota hai), replication (har key ki N copies — N=3 typical), vector clocks (versions track karna — conflicts ko detect karna), aur sloppy quorum + hinted handoff (node down ho to bhi writes chalti rahein).

Dynamo ne availability ko consistency se upar rakha: writes kabhi fail nahi hoti — conflicts ko baad me resolve kiya jaata hai (eventual consistency). Ye tradeoff shopping cart ke liye sahi tha — cart ka data thoda stale ho to chalega, lekin cart kaam na kare to sale kho jaayegi. Dynamo paper hi hai jisne CAP theorem ko practical banaya — har distributed database aaj Dynamo ke ideas use karti hai.

  • Core ideas: consistent hashing → replication (N=3) → vector clocks → sloppy quorum + hinted handoff
  • Key trade-off: availability (writes kabhi fail nahi) vs consistency (conflicts baad me resolve)
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: Dynamo ne availability ko pehle rakha — aur yehi aaj ke distributed databases ki neev hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/amazon-dynamo-architecture
← PehleHow Vector Database Search Billions of Vectors in MillisecondsAgla →Google Spanner
203Software White Papers — Foundational Papers

Google Spanner

Google Spanner wo paper hai jisne globally distributed database ko possible banaya — ek aisa database jo duniya bhar me chalta hai, lekin strong consistency deta hai. Spanner se pehle mana jaata tha ki global scale par sirf eventual consistency mil sakti hai — Spanner ne ye galat saabit kiya.

Spanner ka raaz hai TrueTime — Google ke data centers me atomic clocks aur GPS receivers lage hain, jo time ko 7ms ke andar accurate rakhte hain. Is accurate time se Spanner har transaction ko ek global timestamp de sakta hai — aur timestamps se hi ordering tay hoti hai. Isse externally consistent transactions milti hain — duniya bhar me koi bhi do transactions ka order sahi hota hai.

Spanner SQL + strong consistency + global scale — teeno ek saath deta hai. Iska use hota hai Google ke critical systems me — jaise ads aur payments. Spanner ka lesson hai: hardware (atomic clocks) se software problem solve ki ja sakti hai — aur strong consistency global scale par possible hai, agar tum time ko control kar sako.

  • Core idea: TrueTime (atomic clocks + GPS) → global timestamps → externally consistent transactions
  • Key numbers: time accuracy 7ms ke andar; global scale + strong consistency + SQL
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: Spanner ne dikhaya ki global scale par strong consistency possible hai — time ko control karo to ordering mil jaati hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/cloud-spanner-database
← PehleAmazon DynamoAgla →Meta Serverless Architecture: XFaaS
204Software White Papers — Foundational Papers

Meta Serverless Architecture: XFaaS

Meta ka XFaaS (eXtensible Function-as-a-Service) wo serverless architecture hai jo Meta ke scale par chalti hai — billions of users, millions of functions. XFaaS ka problem tha: Meta ke andar hazaaron teams ko server manage kiye bina code chalana tha — aur wo bhi Meta ke massive scale par.

XFaaS ke core ideas hain: functions as units (chhote functions — har function ek kaam karta hai), automatic scaling (load aane par functions automatically scale hote hain — zero se millions tak), multi-tenancy (hazaaron teams ek hi platform par — isolation ke saath), aur deep integration (Meta ke data, storage, aur services se seedha connect).

XFaaS ka sabse bada challenge hai cold starts aur performance Meta ke scale par — millions of functions ko milliseconds me start karna. Iske liye XFaaS ne warm pools, snapshotting, aur smart scheduling use kiya. XFaaS ka lesson hai: serverless sirf chhote apps ke liye nahi hai — sahi architecture se serverless Meta ke scale par bhi chalta hai.

  • Core ideas: functions as units → automatic scaling (zero to millions) → multi-tenancy → deep integration
  • Key challenge: cold starts at Meta scale — warm pools + snapshotting + smart scheduling
  • Yaad rakhne wali baat: serverless sirf chhote apps ke liye nahi — sahi architecture se ye duniya ke sabse bade scale par bhi chalta hai
Source:https://newsletter.systemdesign.one/p/serverless-architecture

Cache Pattern Picker — Kaunsi Situation Me Kaunsa Pattern?

Teen scenarios diye hain — har scenario ke liye sahi cache pattern chuno: Cache-Aside, Write-Through, ya Write-Behind. Galat chuno to kyun galat hai, wo bhi bataya jaayega.

Scenario 1: Read-heavy app hai (100 reads : 1 write). Data stale ho to chalega, lekin reads bahut fast hone chahiye. Kaunsa pattern?

Scenario 2: Write-heavy app hai. Har write ke baad data ka kho jaana acceptable nahi hai — durability sabse important hai. Kaunsa pattern?

Scenario 3: Write bursts aate hain (ek saath hazaaron writes). Database slow hai, aur thoda data loss chalega. Kaunsa pattern?

Use case 1: Payment system — paise ka transaction hai, galat balance dikhana ya double charge karna nahi chalega.

Use case 3: Shopping cart — user cart me item daale, wo dikhna chahiye; lekin checkout par stock galat ho to nuksaan hai.

← PehleMeta Serverless Architecture: XFaaSAgla →Interview FAQs
206Interview FAQs

Interview FAQs

System design interviews me sabse zyada poochhe jaane wale 36 sawaal — har sawaal par click karo, jawab khulega.

Q01System design interview me sabse pehle kya karna chahiye — seedha diagram banana ya requirements poochhna?

Pehle requirements poochho — bina requirements ke design karna sabse badi galti hai. Functional requirements (kya features chahiye), non-functional requirements (scale, latency, availability), aur constraints (budget, timeline) — ye teeno poochh kar hi design shuru karo. Interviewer tumhari sochne ki process dekhta hai, final diagram nahi. Pehle 5 minute requirements par lagao, phir back-of-the-envelope estimates, phir high-level design, phir deep dive, aur ant me bottlenecks. Jo candidate seedha diagram banata hai, wo requirements miss kar deta hai aur galat system design kar deta hai.

Q02WhatsApp aur Telegram jaise chat apps me message delivery kaise kaam karti hai?

Chat apps me har user ka ek persistent WebSocket connection server se hota hai. Jab tum message bhejte ho to wo server tak jaata hai, server use store karta hai, phir receiver ke connection ko dhoondh kar push karta hai. Receiver offline ho to message queue me rehta hai aur online aate hi deliver hota hai. Group chats me fan-out hota hai — ek message kai receivers tak jaata hai. Delivery receipts (sent, delivered, read) alag se track hote hain. End-to-end encryption me server message padh nahi sakta — sirf sender aur receiver ke paas keys hoti hain.

Q03URL shortener (TinyURL) design karte waqt sabse mushkil hissa kya hai?

Sabse mushkil hissa hai unique short codes generate karna aur unhe scale par serve karna. Base62 encoding se har long URL ko chhota code milta hai, lekin collisions se bachna hota hai. Dusra mushkil hissa hai read-heavy traffic — har click par redirect hota hai, isliye caching zaroori hai. Teesra hai analytics — kitne clicks hue, kahan se hue. Database me short code primary key hota hai, aur popular URLs ko cache me rakha jaata hai. 100 million URLs ke liye 7-character Base62 code kaafi hota hai (62^7 = 3.5 trillion combinations).

Q04Notification system me push, email aur SMS ko ek saath kaise manage karte hain?

Notification system me ek central service hoti hai jo saare channels (push, email, SMS, in-app) ko manage karti hai. Flow hai: event hota hai (jaise order placed) → notification service user ki preferences dekhti hai (kaunsa channel, kab) → message ko template se banati hai → relevant channel provider ko bhejti hai (FCM for push, SES for email, Twilio for SMS). Rate limiting zaroori hai — user ko spam nahi karna. Retry logic bhi chahiye — agar push fail ho to email try karo. Preferences aur templates ko alag se store karo taaki har notification type ko customize kar sako.

Q05Leaderboard system me top-100 dikhana aasan hai, lekin ek player ki apni rank nikalna mushkil kyun hai?

Top-100 nikalna aasan hai kyunki sorted set se pehle 100 elements turant mil jaate hain (Redis ZSET me ZRANGE). Lekin ek player ki apni rank (jaise 4,32,891st) nikalne ke liye uske score se kam wale saare players ko count karna padta hai — ye O(N) ka kaam hai. Solutions hain: score ranges ko buckets me baantna (har bucket me kitne players hain, ye count rakho), ya periodic snapshots lena. Write-heavy games me updates ko message queue me daal kar batch me apply kiya jaata hai taaki reads slow na hon.

Q06Payment gateway design me sabse important cheez kya hai — speed ya correctness?

Correctness — paise ke mamle me ek bhi galat transaction nahi chalega. Payment gateway me idempotency sabse important hai: ek hi payment dobara process na ho (network retry par bhi). Dusra hai strong consistency — balance hamesha sahi hona chahiye. Teesra hai audit trail — har transaction ka poora record. Speed bhi important hai lekin correctness se kam — user 2 second wait kar lega, lekin galat charge nahi sahega. Isliye payments me CP (consistency over availability) chuna jaata hai, aur har step ko transaction me rakha jaata hai.

Q07Search engine (Google jaisa) aur e-commerce search me kya farak hai?

Web search engine (Google) billions of pages ko crawl karke inverted index banata hai aur relevance + authority se rank karta hai. E-commerce search (Amazon) chhote catalog par chalta hai lekin filters (price, brand, rating), personalization, aur real-time inventory zyada important hote hain. Web search me freshness vs coverage ka tradeoff hota hai; e-commerce me ranking me conversion rate bhi factor hota hai — jo product zyada bikta hai use upar dikhao. Dono me inverted index use hota hai, lekin ranking signals alag hote hain.

Q08Real-time collaborative editor (Google Docs jaisa) me do log ek saath type karein to conflict kaise solve hota hai?

Collaborative editors me Operational Transformation (OT) ya CRDTs use hote hain. OT me har edit ko operation ki tarah treat kiya jaata hai — jab do operations ek saath aayein to unhe transform karke merge kiya jaata hai taaki dono users ko same final document dikhe. CRDTs me data structure hi aisi hoti hai ki operations kisi bhi order me apply karo, result same aata hai. WebSocket se har keystroke server ko jaata hai aur dusre users tak broadcast hota hai. Sabse mushkil hissa hai cursor positions aur selections ko sync rakhna — iske liye alag se presence system hota hai.

Q09CAP theorem ko ek line me samjhao — aur interview me iska jawab kaise dena chahiye?

CAP theorem kehta hai ki distributed system me network partition hone par tum Consistency ya Availability me se ek hi chun sakte ho, dono nahi. Interview me sirf ye line bolna kaafi nahi hai — ye bhi batana hota hai ki tumhare use case me kaunsi side chunni hai aur kyun. Payments me consistency (CP), social feed me availability (AP). Aur ye bhi batao ki normal time (partition nahi hai) me latency vs consistency ka tradeoff hota hai — ye PACELC theorem hai. Interviewer ye dekhna chahta hai ki tum tradeoff ko use case se jod kar sochte ho.

Q10SQL vs NoSQL — interview me database kaise chunein?

Pehle data model aur access pattern batao, phir database chuno. Structured data + complex queries + transactions chahiye to SQL (Postgres/MySQL). Flexible schema + massive scale + simple lookups chahiye to NoSQL. Key-value lookups ke liye Redis/DynamoDB, documents ke liye MongoDB, wide-column aur time-series ke liye Cassandra, relationships ke liye Neo4j, search ke liye Elasticsearch. Interview me 'MongoDB use karenge' bina reason ke galat jawab hai — pehle batao data kaisa hai, queries kaisi hain, phir database chuno. Aksar ek system me kai databases hote hain — har access pattern ke liye sahi type.

Q11Caching ke 4 patterns me se kaunsa kab use karein?

Cache-Aside read-heavy apps ke liye — app pehle cache dekhti hai, miss ho to DB se la kar cache bhar deti hai. Write-Through jab durability chahiye — har write cache aur DB dono me jaati hai, data kabhi kho nahi sakta. Write-Behind jab write bursts hain — writes cache me jama karke batch me DB me likhi jaati hain, lekin data loss ka risk hota hai. Refresh-Ahead jab predictable reads hain — cache expiry se pehle hi refresh kar deta hai. Interview me pattern ka naam nahi, scenario-pattern ka match batana hota hai — pehle problem, phir pattern.

Q12Load balancer aur reverse proxy me kya farak hai?

Load balancer ka kaam hai traffic ko kai servers me baantna — wo health checks karta hai aur kharab server ko traffic se hatata hai. Reverse proxy ka kaam hai client aur server ke beech me baith kar requests ko forward karna — wo SSL termination, caching, compression, aur security bhi karta hai. Aksar ek hi tool (jaise Nginx) dono kaam karta hai — isliye confusion hoti hai. Interview me farak saaf rakho: load balancing traffic baantne ka kaam hai, reverse proxy server ko chhupane aur request ko manage karne ka kaam hai. Dono aksar saath use hote hain.

Q13Consistent hashing normal hashing se behtar kyun hai?

Normal hashing me key ka hash lekar hash % N se node chuna jaata hai — N (nodes) badalte hi lagbhag saare keys ki jagah badal jaati hai aur poora data dobara move karna padta hai. Consistent hashing me keys aur nodes dono ko ek circular ring par rakha jaata hai — har key us node par jaati hai jo ring par uske baad aata hai. Node add ya remove ho to sirf 1/N data move hota hai, baaki sab wahi rehta hai. Virtual nodes load ko evenly baant te hain. Dynamo, Cassandra aur Memcached — sab consistent hashing use karte hain.

Q14Microservices kab use karne chahiye aur kab nahi?

Microservices tab jab team badi ho (kai teams ek codebase par), deploy slow ho (ek change ke liye poora monolith deploy karna), ya alag parts ko alag scale chahiye (ek service ko 100x traffic, dusri ko 1x). Microservices mat use karo jab team chhoti ho, domain abhi clear nahi hai, ya tumhe distributed systems ki complexity ka experience nahi hai. Pehle modular monolith banao — andar se modules me bante hue, clear boundaries ke saath. Sirf wahi module alag karo jo sach me alag scale ya alag team maangta hai. Shopify ka example hai — 30TB/minute traffic modular monolith par handle karte hain.

Q15Database sharding aur replication me kya farak hai?

Sharding data ko baantna hai — har shard me data ka ek hissa hota hai, aur saare shards mil kar poora data banate hain. Sharding scale ke liye hota hai (data ek machine par fit nahi hota). Replication data ki copies banana hai — har copy me poora data hota hai. Replication availability aur read scale ke liye hota hai (ek copy fail ho to dusri chale, reads copies par baante jaayein). Dono aksar saath use hote hain: har shard ki kai copies (replicas) hoti hain. Interview me farak saaf rakho: sharding = data baantna (write scale), replication = copies banana (availability + read scale).

Q16Message queue aur Kafka me kya farak hai?

Message queue (jaise RabbitMQ, SQS) messages ko deliver karti hai — consumer message ko process karke acknowledge karta hai, phir message delete ho jaata hai. Kafka ek distributed event log hai — events disk par store hote hain aur configurable time tak rehte hain, consumer chahe to purane events dobara padh sakta hai (replay). Queue task distribution ke liye best hai (ek message, ek consumer); Kafka event streaming ke liye best hai (ek event, kai consumers, replay possible). Interview me use case se chuno: job processing ke liye queue, event sourcing ya stream processing ke liye Kafka.

Q17Rate limiting ke liye token bucket aur fixed window me se kaunsa behtar hai?

Dono ka apna use hai. Token bucket burst allow karta hai — bucket bhara ho to ek saath kai requests ja sakti hain, isliye user experience behtar rehta hai. Fixed window simple hai lekin window boundary par burst ho sakta hai (59th second par 100 requests + 61st second par 100 requests = 2 second me 200). Sliding window sabse accurate hai lekin zyada memory khati hai. Production me aksar token bucket (Redis se) use hota hai — simple, burst-friendly, aur distributed systems me implement karna aasan hai. Interview me algorithm ka naam nahi, tradeoff batana hota hai.

Q18System design me 'back-of-the-envelope' calculation kyun zaroori hai?

Back-of-the-envelope se pata chalta hai ki system kitna bada hoga — kitne requests/sec, kitna storage, kitni bandwidth. Bina estimate ke tum galat decisions loge — jaise 100 req/sec ke liye sharding karna, ya 100K req/sec ke liye single database rakhna. Interview me ye dikhata hai ki tum scale ko samajhte ho. Yaad rakhne wale numbers: 1 million requests/day lagbhag 12 requests/sec, 1 saal me 30 million seconds, memory access 100ns, SSD read 100µs, network same data center me 0.5ms. In numbers se tum kisi bhi system ka rough size nikal sakte ho.

Q19System design interview me 45 minute ka time kaise manage karein?

Time ko 5 hisson me baanto: pehle 5 minute requirements (functional + non-functional + scale), agle 5 minute back-of-the-envelope estimates, agle 10 minute high-level design (API + data model + components diagram), agle 15 minute deep dive (1-2 components ko detail me — database schema, caching, sharding), aur aakhri 10 minute bottlenecks aur tradeoffs. Har step par interviewer se baat karte raho — wo tumhe hints dega aur tumhare direction ko correct karega. Agar deep dive me phas jao to wapas high-level par aao — poora system dikhana, ek component me atakna nahi.

Q20Design YouTube aur Design Netflix me kya farak hai?

Dono video streaming hain lekin use case alag hai. YouTube me user-generated content hai — har minute 500+ ghante upload hote hain, isliye upload + transcoding pipeline sabse important hai. Netflix me curated content hai — upload kam hai lekin streaming quality aur recommendations zyada important hain. YouTube me search aur discovery (creators, trending) important hai; Netflix me personalization (har user ko alag home page) important hai. Dono me CDN sabse bada cost hai, lekin YouTube me transcoding cost zyada hai aur Netflix me licensing + recommendations zyada important hain.

Q21Behavioral interview me STAR method kaise use karein?

STAR ka matlab hai Situation, Task, Action, Result. Situation me context do (kya hua tha, kab, kahan), Task me tumhari zimmedari batao (tumhe kya karna tha), Action me wo steps batao jo tumne liye (specifically tumne kya kiya — 'humne' nahi, 'maine'), aur Result me outcome batao numbers ke saath (latency 200ms se 40ms ki, ya 1M users handle kiye). 6-8 stories taiyaar rakho — tough problem, conflict, failure, leadership, tight deadline, bada impact. Har story ko 2 minute me khatam karo — lambi kahani interviewer ko bore karti hai.

Q22System design interview me agar koi sawaal ka jawab nahi pata ho to kya karein?

Jhooth mat bolo aur guess mat karo — seedha kaho 'ye mujhe nahi pata, lekin main is tarah sochunga'. Phir apni reasoning dikhao — pehle principles se socho, assumptions batao, aur ek reasonable approach suggest karo. Interviewer ko tumhari soch dekhni hai, har answer ka ratta hua jawab nahi. Agar ek component nahi pata to use black box ki tarah treat karo aur aage badho — poora system rukna nahi chahiye. Aur interview ke baad wo topic padh lo — agli baar ke liye taiyaar raho.

Q23Mobile system design interview me backend se alag kya poochha jaata hai?

Mobile interviews me 3 cheezein extra hain: offline support (app bina network ke bhi kaam kare — local database + sync), push notifications (server se client tak real-time updates), aur battery/bandwidth optimization (background sync kam karo, payload chhota rakho). Sabse mushkil hissa hai client-server sync — offline changes ko server se merge karna aur conflicts resolve karna. Interview me ye dikhana hota hai ki tum mobile ki limitations (unreliable network, limited battery, limited storage) ko samajhte ho — sirf backend design karna kaafi nahi hai.

Q24Resume me system design skills kaise dikhayein?

Resume me har bullet me impact likho numbers ke saath — 'payment system banaya jo 1M daily transactions handle karta hai, latency 200ms se 40ms ki' — ye line recruiter ko system design interview ke liye taiyaar candidate dikhati hai. Projects me wo projects dikhao jo scale dikhate hon — 'URL shortener banaya jo 10K req/sec handle karta hai'. Skills me sirf relevant technologies likho — har tool likhna zaroori nahi hai. Aur ek page me rakho — recruiter 6 second me decide karta hai, pehli 3 lines me hi impact dikhao.

Q25RAG aur fine-tuning me se kab kya use karein?

RAG jab facts sikhane hon — model ko external documents de kar jawab dilwana hai. RAG fast hai, sasta hai, aur documents update karna aasan hai — model ko retrain nahi karna padta. Fine-tuning jab behavior ya style sikhana ho — model ko specific format, tone, ya domain language me jawab dena sikhana hai. Fine-tuning mehenga hai aur data chahiye. Best approach aksar dono ka combination hai: fine-tuning se model domain ki style seekhe, RAG se wo latest facts laaye. Yaad rakho: fine-tuning knowledge nahi, behavior sikhata hai — facts RAG se aate hain.

Q26AI agent aur chatbot me kya farak hai?

Chatbot sirf prompt ka jawab deta hai — ek input, ek output. AI agent ek goal ko poora karne ke liye kai steps leta hai — wo plan banata hai, tools use karta hai (search, APIs, code execution), results ko dekh kar agla step tay karta hai, aur jab tak goal poora na ho, loop me chalta hai. Agent ka loop hai perceive → reason → act → observe. Bina tools ke LLM sirf chatbot hai; tools + loop + memory mil kar agent banta hai. Agent ki sabse badi mushkil hai reliability — ek galat step poora kaam bigaad sakta hai, isliye guardrails zaroori hain.

Q27LLM apps me hallucination ko kaise kam karein?

Hallucination kam karne ke 4 tareeke hain: pehla, RAG — model ko relevant documents do taaki wo apni training knowledge ki jagah tumhare data se jawab de. Dusra, grounding — jawab me citations maango, taaki har claim ka source ho. Teesra, low temperature — deterministic jawab ke liye temperature kam rakho (0-0.3). Chautha, verification — critical claims ko dobara check karwao (self-verification ya dusre model se). Aur sabse important: model ko 'pata nahi' bolna sikhao — jab information na ho to model ko guess karne ki jagah ye kehna chahiye ki use nahi pata.

Q28Vector database kaam kaise karta hai — normal database se kya farak hai?

Normal database exact match dhoondhta hai — jaise 'user_id = 123'. Vector database similar cheezein dhoondhta hai — jaise 'is sawaal se milte-julte documents'. Text ko embeddings (numbers ke vectors) me badla jaata hai — similar meaning wale text ke vectors paas hote hain. Search karte waqt query ka vector banaya jaata hai aur sabse paas wale vectors nikale jaate hain (top-k). Billions of vectors ko milliseconds me search karne ke liye HNSW jaise approximate indexes use hote hain — exact nahi, lekin 95%+ accurate. Yehi RAG ki neev hai.

Q29MCP (Model Context Protocol) kya hai aur ye kyun important hai?

MCP ek open standard hai jisse LLM apps tools aur data sources se connect hote hain. MCP se pehle har tool ke liye alag integration likhni padti thi — 10 apps × 10 tools = 100 integrations. MCP se ek baar server likho, har MCP client se chalao. MCP ke 3 parts hain: host (LLM app, jaise Claude Desktop), client (connector), aur server (jo tools, resources, aur prompts expose karta hai). MCP hi ChatGPT apps aur Claude ke tool ecosystem ki neev hai — jaise USB standard ne devices ko connect kiya, MCP ne AI apps ko tools se connect kiya.

Q30LLM inference ko sasta kaise karein — production me cost kaise control karein?

Inference cost control karne ke 5 levers hain: caching (prompt cache aur semantic cache — 30-50% requests cache se serve ho sakti hain), batching (continuous batching se GPU utilization badhao), model routing (simple sawaal chhote model se, mushkil bade se), context optimization (sirf relevant context bhejo — context window chhota rakho), aur quantization (model weights ko chhota karo). Har request ka token cost track karo — 1M users par har request ka 1 cent bhi $10,000 per day hai. AI apps me cost hi scaling ki hadd hai.

Q31AI agents ke liye evals kyun zaroori hain — normal testing kyun kaam nahi karta?

Normal testing deterministic hota hai — input X do, output Y aana chahiye. LLM ke jawab deterministic nahi hote — same prompt par alag jawab aa sakta hai. Isliye evals chahiye: golden dataset (test cases jinke sahi jawab pata hon), LLM-as-judge (ek LLM se dusre ke jawab ko score karwana), aur metrics (accuracy, faithfulness, hallucination rate). Har change ke baad evals chalao — bina evals ke har deploy ek gamble hai. Production LLM apps me evals hi quality ki guarantee hain — ye testing ka AI version hai.

Q32Multi-agent system kab banayein aur kab ek agent kaafi hai?

Multi-agent tab jab task complex aur decomposable ho — jaise research (ek agent search kare, ek summarize kare, ek verify kare), ya software development (planner, coder, reviewer). Har agent apne kaam me expert hota hai — specialization se quality badhti hai. Lekin multi-agent me complexity aur cost badhti hai — agents ke beech communication, coordination, aur har agent ka LLM cost. Simple tasks ke liye ek agent kaafi hai — ReAct loop se kaam ho jaata hai. Rule hai: pehle ek agent se shuru karo, aur jab wo fail hone lage (task bahut bada ya complex ho), tab multi-agent me todo.

Q33Amazon Dynamo paper kyun itna important hai — isne kya badla?

Dynamo paper (2007) ne distributed key-value stores ki neev rakhi — is par Cassandra, Riak, aur DynamoDB bane. Dynamo ne 4 ideas diye jo aaj har distributed database use karta hai: consistent hashing (node badalne par sirf thoda data move ho), replication (har key ki N copies), vector clocks (versions track karke conflicts detect karna), aur sloppy quorum + hinted handoff (node down ho to bhi writes chalti rahein). Dynamo ne availability ko consistency se upar rakha — aur yehi tradeoff aaj ke distributed systems ki pehchan hai. Interview me Dynamo ka naam lena dikhata hai ki tum distributed systems ki history samajhte ho.

Q34Google Spanner ne CAP theorem ko kaise challenge kiya?

Spanner ne dikhaya ki global scale par strong consistency possible hai — agar tum time ko control kar sako. Spanner TrueTime use karta hai — data centers me atomic clocks aur GPS receivers jo time ko 7ms ke andar accurate rakhte hain. Is accurate time se har transaction ko ek global timestamp milta hai, aur timestamps se hi ordering tay hoti hai. Isse externally consistent transactions milti hain — duniya bhar me koi bhi do transactions ka order sahi hota hai. Spanner ka lesson hai: hardware se software problem solve ki ja sakti hai — aur CAP theorem ka matlab ye nahi hai ki global strong consistency impossible hai.

Q35White papers padhna system design interview ke liye kyun zaroori hai?

White papers wo original source hain jahan se saare distributed systems ke ideas aaye hain — Dynamo, Spanner, Raft, BigTable, MapReduce. Interview me jab tum kehte ho 'ye Dynamo paper ka idea hai' to interviewer ko pata chalta hai ki tum concepts ko depth me samajhte ho, sirf naam nahi ratte. Papers padhne se tumhe pata chalta hai ki har design decision ke peeche kya problem thi aur kya tradeoff tha — aur yehi soch interview me marks dilati hai. Har paper ko poora padhna zaroori nahi hai — abstract, introduction, aur design section padh lo, kaafi hai.

Q36Serverless architecture bade scale par kaam karti hai ya sirf chhote apps ke liye hai?

Serverless bade scale par bhi kaam karti hai — Meta ka XFaaS iska proof hai, jo billions of users ke scale par millions of functions chalata hai. Serverless ka fayda hai automatic scaling (zero se millions tak) aur no server management. Challenges hain cold starts (function ko start hone me time lagta hai), state management (functions stateless hote hain), aur cost (bahut zyada traffic par hamesha-chalte servers saste pad sakte hain). Interview me serverless ko ek tradeoff ki tarah present karo — kab use karna hai (spiky traffic, event-driven work) aur kab nahi (long-running, latency-sensitive work).
